# Decisions — Cadra

Every product and technical decision, with its date, the reasoning, and the handoff that landed it. Moved out of `CLAUDE.md` by handoff 19 because it had grown to 43% of a file loaded into every Builder session. **No entry was reworded, reordered or dropped on the way.**

Read the entries for the area you are about to change before changing it. **Add to this file in the same commit as the work.**

> **Ruled ≠ landed.** A line here records what was *decided*, not what exists in the code. Tag each one **`[ruled]`** when the Architect makes it, and change it to **`[landed <commit>]`** only when a Builder report confirms it shipped. The Architect may write `[ruled]`; only a report moves a line to `[landed]`. `docs/STATUS.md` lists **landed** work only.
>
> This convention exists because handoff 04 found four decisions recorded here as done that had never been implemented — including one the list contradicted itself on. A decision written down and not built is worse than one not yet made, because the next handoff builds on it.

*The first block below sat under `## Status` in `CLAUDE.md` rather than under `## Decisions`. It is ledger, and it is kept here in the order it appeared.*

- 2026-09-05 · **`.env` untracked from git and the dev client secret rotated.** Vendra's `site_config` holds that secret — rotating it without updating Vendra breaks INT-03. · It was tracked *and* gitignored, so a real secret briefly sat one `git add .` from a commit.
- 2026-09-05 · **`asset_tag` removed from `_WRITABLE_FIELDS`.** Breaking, announced; Vendra (the only live consumer) does not write it. · A client could PATCH `tag` until it no longer matched the physical label, defeating the affix control.
- 2026-09-05 · **Empty scopes means no permissions, not wildcard.** · A client created without scopes receiving everything is privilege escalation waiting for a typo — the opposite of the Tag-Issuing Unit separation.
- 2026-09-05 · **`data.tag` deprecated by annotation, not removed.** · Breaking a published field to fix a naming problem is not a trade worth making with a live consumer.
- 2026-09-05 · **The affix endpoint verifies `serial_number` when supplied.** · The spec asks for the serial-to-tag match; checking one side is half a control.

- 2026-09-05 · **`nrs19` is the working tenant for integration and demo work**, replacing `cadra19`. · The 19-era database with the real dataset (~11,878 assets) and the migration target; its `jwt_secret` and API client were provisioned in handoff 03. Vendra's `Host` header, category ids and client credentials are all per-tenant — none of them carry over from `cadra19`.

- 2026-09-05 · `[landed b8b6f64]` **`/auth/login` no longer grants `["*"]`** — scopes derive from the user's Odoo groups (`utils.GROUP_SCOPES` / `scopes_for_user`), `tagging:write` comes from the new `group_tag_issuing_unit` alone, and a user with no Cadra group gets an empty scope list. · Handoff 04 Q1; handoff 05.
- 2026-09-05 · `[landed]` **`ensure_vendra_client()` stays.** The demo's asset-creating half needs a credential that is *not* `test_client` — an admin wildcard that passes `tagging:write` without holding it would make the demo prove nothing. · The separation is the control; same argument as ruling 6 applied to the other side.
- 2026-09-05 · `[landed f7bf682]` **The shared `docs/api/.creds` is deleted.** Only 2 of 8 tenants had a `.creds.<db>`, so the ruling's literal precondition was never met — deleted on the evidence that no reader can reach it instead (both readers fall back only on the no-tenant branch, which cannot authenticate against a host-routed stack). · Handoff 05 Part C.
- 2026-09-05 · `[landed]` **`nrs19` client split**: `vendra` (assets:read/write, deliberately no `tagging:write`) and `tag_issuing_unit` (`tagging:write` only). Proven live in both directions with 403s. · An asset-creating credential that can also confirm its own tag defeats the control.

- 2026-09-06 · `[landed b8b6f64]` **`admin` is a wildcard, so `settings:write` exists.** Six tenant-configuration write routes moved off `admin`. Granting an Asset Manager `admin` to keep the SPA's Settings screens would have handed them `tagging:write` — the hole reopened in the name of not breaking the UI. · Handoff 05 Part A.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed b8b6f64]` **Session refresh RE-DERIVES scopes from groups; client refresh replays the client record's.** `verify_and_rotate` used to replay the stored string either way, so removing someone from the Tag-Issuing Unit left them holding `tagging:write` for up to the 30-day sliding window. · A group change must bite within the hour, not the month.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed b8b6f64]` **An internal user with no Cadra group gets NO scopes.** Every list endpoint runs `sudo()`, so a scope is the only gate between a logged-in account and the register; a "read scopes only" default would hand the asset base to anyone with an Odoo password. · Handoff 05.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed 82da6ab]` **A tag label id is not an asset id and the spaces collide** — 22 assets on `nrs19` have an asset id that is a valid label id for a *different* asset. `print-url` takes a label id and returns a URL containing an asset id; feeding the wrong one returns `200` and renders the wrong machine's label. `data.tagging.label_id` exists so an integrator never has to guess. · Handoff 05 Part B.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed 82da6ab]` **`/report/pdf/<...>` resolves its second segment against `ir.actions.report.report_name`, not the action xmlid.** `print-url` emitted the xmlid and 404'd; the one published route from an asset to a printable label had never rendered anything. · Handoff 05.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed 82da6ab]` **`dict(field.selection)` is wrong for a *related* Selection** — `.selection` is a callable there, so it raises `TypeError`. Use `fields_get`. This 500'd `/barcode/lookup` for every asset it found, and broke the barcode station's own scan box, not just the API. · Handoff 05.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed 82da6ab]` **A query-enum validator must never disable itself on failure**, and `"null"` is not a "no filter" sentinel where the filter is an enum — treating it as one returned the whole register. · Handoff 05 verification pass; `utils._selection_allowed_values` returns `None` on error and rolls the cursor back, so it must not be used on a read path.
- 2026-09-06 · **Odoo caches group membership** (`res.users._get_group_ids` is `@ormcache`d). A group change made from a separate `odoo shell` process does not reliably invalidate a running HTTP worker; changes made through the running server (UI or XML-RPC) do. Stock Odoo behaviour — but it will make anyone testing the scope mapping from a shell think it is broken. · Handoff 05.

- 2026-09-06 · `[landed b8b6f64]` **`/auth/login` derives scopes from Odoo groups**, and a new **`settings:write`** scope carries the six tenant-config routes. · `admin` is itself a wildcard in `require_jwt`, so mapping Asset Manager to `admin` would have moved the hole rather than closed it. Additive, non-breaking; ships a data migration because refresh tokens replay stored scopes for 30 days.
- 2026-09-06 · `[ruled]` **`tagging:write` stays inside the `admin` wildcard.** "Absolute below system-administrator" is the accepted posture; document it where the scope is defined so it reads as a decision, not an oversight.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed 82da6ab]` **`print-url` built its path from the report action's xmlid, not its `report_name`** — it resolved to nothing and returned the website's 404 page as a 26KB HTML body. The one published route from an asset to a printable label had never rendered anything. · Read `report_name` off the action record so a rename cannot reintroduce it.
- 2026-09-06 · `[ruled]` **Fix the `phase1_*` seed users' roles** — empty scopes by accident must not look like empty scopes by design.

- 2026-09-05 · Architect/Builder split adopted for this repo, mirroring `vendra_network`. · One person owns two codebases in one programme; the handoff trail is how the integration stays coherent across both.
- 2026-09-05 · **Serialised provenance is a nested `data.provenance` block**, not six top-level keys. · Matches the existing grouping; one new envelope key instead of six for a contract Vendra is building against.
- 2026-09-05 · **`vendra` added to the `import_source` enum.** · The enum already names integration partners; `other` + a ref throws away the signal the field exists to carry.
- 2026-09-05 · **`q` also matches `grn_reference` and `purchase_order_reference`.** Intended behaviour — an auditor pastes a GRN into search before finding a named filter. Do not "fix" it.
- 2026-09-05 · **Idempotency header length is validated on all routes; the header is not rejected on non-idempotent ones.** · A caller sending an over-long key deserves an error; a caller harmlessly spraying the header should not start failing. Document which routes honour it.
- 2026-09-05 · **Tag capture before tag transport.** Cadra records neither printing nor affixing, and `nrs.asset.tag.label.state` is a replacement lifecycle that reads `active` from creation — exposing it would reproduce the false-completeness bug in a new field. Vendra shows `Registered` and claims nothing further until capture exists. · Handoff 01 Part C.
- 2026-09-05 · **`dist/` is Odoo 15 only and its `_sql_constraints` are correct** — it is not on the 19 addons path, so the "44 unassessed vendored files" were never a hole. `crnd-19/`/`oca-19/` are 19-only and need no version guard. · Handoff 02 audit.
  - **Reason corrected 2026-09-15 (handoff 24).** See the companion `dist/` entry below, dated the same day. Since handoff 20 retired the Odoo 15 stack, `dist/`'s `_sql_constraints` are **unreached, not correct**; the conclusion that they were never a hole still holds.
- 2026-09-05 · **The six `crnd-19/` holes were our own porting bug**, not vendor debt — `generic_resource`/`generic_location`/`generic_team` are hand-ported by us and are not fetched by `scripts/fetch-crnd-19.sh`, so patching them carries no upstream-clobber cost. · Handoff 02.
- 2026-09-05 · **`models.Constraint` on an AbstractModel does propagate to concrete inheritors** on Odoo 19 (verified on `generic.resource.mixin` → `generic_resource_simple`). · Handoff 02.
- 2026-09-05 · **`dist/` is not on the Odoo 19 addons path and is out of scope permanently.** Its constraints are correct for the 15 stack and die with it. · Three independent proofs in `docs/constraint-audit.md`; the trees are mutually exclusive by Odoo version.
  - **Reason corrected 2026-09-15 (handoff 24).** The conclusion is unchanged — leave them alone — but "correct for the 15 stack and die with it" stopped being true when handoff 20 retired that stack. `dist/` is now on **no addons path at all**, so its 61 constraints are **unreached** rather than correct (`docs/constraint-audit.md:62-68, 81-82`). The surviving reason is that nothing loads a vendored tree we do not control.
- 2026-09-05 · **`asset_code` becomes `NOT NULL`** alongside its unique constraint. · INT-06/07 dual-code reconciliation is a named control point; "one asset per *non-null* code" is weaker than it assumes.
- 2026-09-05 · **Vendored trees are guarded by a startup assertion on the warning count, not by the source test.** · A source test cannot cover code an upstream refresh may reintroduce.
- 2026-09-05 · **Tag capture is built before tag transport.** Cadra records neither printing nor affixing; the Tag-Issuing Unit's independence must survive into whatever records it. · Handoff 01 Part C, handoff 03.
- 2026-09-05 · **`label_state` lives on `nrs.asset.tag.label`, is readonly, and moves only through `_mark_printed` / `_mark_affixed`.** `is_tagged` is true only after an affix confirmation whose scanned tag number matched the issued one. · A status an operator can set from a desk is evidence of nothing; the serial-to-tag match IS the control.
- 2026-09-05 · **The print funnel is `ir.actions.report._render_qweb_pdf`, not the action methods.** The label wizard, the barcode station's raw `/report/pdf/` URL and Odoo's own Print-menu binding all bypass `action_print_tag`. · Handoff 03; stamping only the action methods would have under-recorded printing badly.
- 2026-09-05 · **Tag ids collide with asset ids, and the four tag reports all declare `model = nrs.asset`** — so anything resolving a report's `res_ids` must not assume which. Two callers pass tag ids in. · Handoff 03 V1: a hook that guessed wrong fabricated print records on unrelated assets. Under-record rather than fabricate.
- 2026-09-05 · **`readonly=True` is a web-client hint, not an ORM guard.** Fields that record an observed event are protected by a `write()` override keyed on a context flag the recording method sets. · Handoff 03 V1; `group_asset_manager` can write over XML-RPC.
- 2026-09-05 · **`active_tag_id` excludes a tag pending replacement; `physical_tag_id` does not.** The physical question must use the latter — requesting a replacement does not peel the label off the asset. · Handoff 03 V3.
- 2026-09-05 · **Unknown API scopes are rejected on save** (`myservice.api.client`), and an empty scopes field remains a wildcard. · A typo used to mint a token and produce a 403 indistinguishable from a real permission decision.
- 2026-09-05 · **Installing modules is not provisioning a tenant.** `-i`/`-u` never run `scripts/bootstrap.py`, so a tenant created the documented way gets no `jwt_secret` and no API client. · How `nrs19`/`test19` ended up unable to authenticate; `k8s/dev/MULTI-TENANT.md` now says so.
- 2026-09-05 · **`_sql_constraints` is dead on Odoo 19** — declared constraints silently do not exist. Ours use the dual-spelling pattern in `api_idempotency.py`; a guard must fail loudly on any new use. · 2 live holes in our modules + 44 files in `dist/`; handoff 02. **Corrected by the handoff 02 audit:** the `dist/` files are not reachable by Odoo 19 and were never holes; the real remainder was 6 sites in `crnd-19/`. All 8 are now fixed.

- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **The affix endpoint queues the event; it does not POST it.** Delivering inline would announce an affix before its transaction committed — a rollback would leave Vendra holding `Tagged` for an asset Cadra does not think is tagged — and would make the Tag-Issuing Unit's confirmation latency a function of whether Vendra is up. `ir.cron._trigger()` books the drain inside the same transaction, so delivery follows the commit by seconds without being part of it. · Handoff 06 B2.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **Any 4xx except 408/425/429 dead-letters on the first attempt.** A request does not become valid by being sent eight more times; burning the budget only delays the moment a human sees the problem. 5xx, 429 and network errors back off. Mirrors Vendra's own classifier pointing the other way. · Handoff 06.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **A `409` from the receiver is a delivery, not a failure.** It is the receiver saying it already has this `event_id` — which is the outcome the idempotency key exists to produce. Treating it as an error would dead-letter events that landed perfectly. · Handoff 06.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **The drain is scoped by `event_type`, not by status.** Request-approval dispatches also sit at `pending`/`error` in this table, and a queue scoped merely by status would re-POST a human approval nobody asked to resubmit. The handoff said "drain `pending` and `error`"; taken literally that is a defect. · Handoff 06; covered by `test_the_drain_never_touches_a_request_approval_row`.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **A repeat affix confirmation raises no second event.** `_mark_affixed` already refuses to rewrite the original record, so the event fires on the state TRANSITION. One physical act is one event. · Handoff 06 B4.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **A tenant with no `asset_events` config emits nothing, and says so at INFO.** That is the correct state for seven of eight tenants. Queueing events that can only dead-letter would turn "no Vendra here" into a permanent alarm. · Handoff 06 B3.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **`max_attempts` is snapshotted onto the row at enqueue.** Retuning the retry policy must not retroactively resurrect events already dead or kill events still in flight. · Handoff 06; same reasoning as Vendra's outbox.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **A manual retry extends the budget rather than resetting `attempts`.** The attempt count is the evidence of how hard the receiver was down; zeroing it erases the only record of that. · Handoff 06.
- 2026-09-06 · **[SUPERSEDED by handoff 08 — the diagnosis was right and the fix has landed; do not follow the throwaway-worker advice, cron now runs on every local tenant.]** **No `ir.cron` fires on the local dev stack.** `config/odoo.19.conf` sets `db_name = odoo`, which pins Odoo's cron worker to that one (empty) database — the log reads *"Skipping database odoo as its base version is not 19.0.1.3"* once a minute and no tenant cron has ever run here. Production is unaffected: `k8s/dev/10-configmap-odoo-19.yaml` deliberately sets **no** `db_name`, so the worker enumerates every tenant. This is not new and is not caused by INT-05 — but it means the depreciation, PM and token-GC crons are equally dormant locally, and anything cron-driven must be proven with a throwaway worker container (`--database=<db>` and no `db_name`). · Handoff 06 live proof.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **A `workflow.dispatch.log` row carrying delivery fields is a SEND INSTRUCTION, and is guarded like one.** `create`/`write` reject `event_type`, `config_id`, `next_attempt_at` and the rest unless the queue's own `cadra_event_dispatch` context flag is set. Without it any internal user — including handoff 05's deliberately scope-less `h05_nobody` — could hand-craft a row over `call_kw` and have Cadra POST a forged `asset.tag.affixed` to Vendra signed with the tenant's credentials, walking round the `tagging:write` separation entirely. The ACL cannot carry this: `_send_to_workflow` writes its audit row as the acting user, so `perm_create` has to stay. · Handoff 06 verification; the same ruling as "`readonly=True` is a web-client hint, not an ORM guard", applied to a new model.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **No grace period may exclude the row whose own `_trigger` booked the run.** A 20-second "don't race the triggered delivery" clause excluded every freshly queued row, and Odoo consumes the trigger before running the action — so the wake-up was spent on an empty pass and every FIRST delivery waited for the 5-minute sweep. The race it guarded against cannot happen: Odoo holds `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` on the `ir.cron` row for the whole action, serialising drains cluster-wide. That guarantee holds only while the cron is the sole entry point — an API route or server action over the queue would need its own row-level lock. · Handoff 06 verification; delivery went from 12 minutes to 11 seconds.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **`event_enabled` pauses delivery; it does not discard events.** Dropping events raised while the switch is off means turning it back on silently loses every affix in between, with nothing to notice or backfill from. The enqueue path queues regardless; the drain excludes disabled targets in its own domain. · Handoff 06 verification; rule 3 — something that silently degrades is a defect even when it returns 200.
- 2026-09-06 · `[landed c2b124a]` **A status label must be true for every row that can carry it.** `error` was relabelled "Failed — Will Retry", which is a false promise on a request-approval dispatch — those are one-shot and are never retried. Labels stay neutral; the `Retrying` and `Dead Letters` filters carry the queue semantics instead. · Handoff 06 verification.

- 2026-09-07 · `[landed]` **`db_name` pins the cron worker, not just HTTP exposure.** `cron_database_list()` is `config['db_name'] or list_dbs(True)`, so `db_name = odoo` in `config/odoo.19.conf` meant the scheduler served one database — and that one is a 15-era database a 19 worker skips as incompatible, so **no `ir.cron` ran on any tenant, ever**. Exposure is now `dbfilter`'s job alone (`^odoo$` single-database, `^%d$` host-routed); `dbfilter` does not narrow `list_dbs`, so cron sees every database whatever it is set to. `k8s/dev/10-configmap-odoo-19.yaml` never set `db_name` — **the cluster was always unaffected; this was a local-dev defect.** · Handoff 08; guarded by `test_no_server_config_pins_the_cron_worker_to_one_database`.
- 2026-09-07 · `[landed]` **A test that invokes the thing under test cannot see that nothing else invokes it.** Every INT-05 delivery test calls `_cron_drain_outbound_events()` directly — legitimate, and it is why handoff 06's retry proofs are real, but it proved delivery *works*, never that delivery *happens*. Corollary shipped: `test_every_scheduled_method_exists` asserts every `ir.cron` we ship names a method that exists, and CI now runs the 72 in-registry Odoo tests — including handoff 06's `test_the_drain_cron_exists_and_trigger_resolves_it`, the only test in the repo that checks a production trigger, which had never once executed. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · `[landed]` **Two `ir.cron` rows on `nrs19`/`jaizbankplc` scheduled methods absent from the code that runs them** (`_cron_refresh_overdue`, `_cron_refresh_verification`). **The methods DO exist — in `c673ed1` on `feat/rfp-phase1-authorisation`, which was never merged**; that commit added the crons, their data file and batched implementations together, and these two databases were upgraded from it. The rows survived, the code did not. Deleted, not deactivated: a scheduled job that cannot run is not a placeholder for one that will, and disabling it preserves the appearance of a guarantee while removing the only thing that would make its absence audible. **`c673ed1` is where the `is_verified`/`is_overdue` fix already lives** — start there, not from scratch. · Handoff 08; migration `myservice_asset_register 19.0.1.10.0`.
- 2026-09-07 · **Depreciation needs no backfill — the engine is a pure function of `as_of`.** `value_at` rebuilds the whole schedule from the start date and reads off the last elapsed period; it never appends or increments, and no period rows are persisted. Missing twelve runs and missing one produce identical output. Proven: one cron run corrected `cadra19` by ₦2,725,958.31 and `gtb19` by ₦5,259,107.63, exactly the drift measured beforehand. ~₦23.1 m estate-wide, all of it **one month** stale, not years. `nrs19`'s 11,878 assets are `erp_managed` and out of the cron's domain by design — the register size is not the depreciation workload. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · `[landed]` **An API client runs as its own Odoo user.** Odoo 19's `sudo()` does not change `env.uid`, so `affixed_by` was faithfully recording the truth — and the truth was that `scripts/bootstrap.py` pointed every client at `base.user_admin`. The scope separation held perfectly while the record it exists to produce said "Administrator" for the Tag-Issuing Unit and for Vendra alike. **289 historic affix records carry the useless value and are NOT rewritten** — no evidence says which client wrote which, and inventing one would fabricate an audit trail. · Handoff 08 Part C.
- 2026-09-07 · `[landed]` **`SCOPE_GROUPS` is deliberately not the inverse of `GROUP_SCOPES`.** `assets:read` is granted by three groups there and `dashboards:read` by two unrelated ones, so an automatic inverse has no basis to choose; and `admin` would invert to `base.group_system`, letting a scope string typed into a form provision a system administrator. A source guard asserts `group_system` never appears in the table. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · `[landed]` **A client refresh family resolves its user from the client record**, the same way session families re-derive scopes from groups. Replaying the user stored at mint time meant re-homing a client had no effect on any session in flight for the full 30-day sliding window — the same defect handoff 05's migration exists to clean up, left live going forward. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · `[ruled — not built]` **The three `myservice.dashboard` routes are the only ones that do not `sudo()`**, so a permissions failure surfaces as `400 validation_failed` leaking Odoo group names into the published envelope. Handoff 08 shipped the conservative fix (derive the groups from the scope, so the two cannot drift). The right fix — `sudo()` and a 403 naming the scope — also changes what a **session** token sees and needs a ruling. · Handoff 08 open question 1.
  - **Tag stale, corrected 2026-09-15 (handoff 24).** The factual claim is now false of the code. The three `myservice.dashboard` routes **do** `sudo()`, and the scope is the gate — landed in handoff 09 R26 — see that handoff's entry, "The three `myservice.dashboard` routes `sudo()`, and the scope is the gate."
- 2026-09-07 · `[ruled — not built]` **Three stored computes read the clock but depend on nothing the clock changes**, so they are permanently wrong: `sla_breached` (215 of 215 requests on `jaizbankplc` are past their deadline and *none* is flagged), `is_verified` (never expires after 365 days), `is_overdue` (backs a live "Overdue PM" menu that is empty forever). One rule catches all three. `is_verified` is published API surface, so correcting it changes what Vendra sees and needs a ruling before it is touched. · Handoff 08.
  - **Tag stale, corrected 2026-09-15 (handoff 24).** All three were subsequently built, so `[ruled — not built]` no longer describes the code. `is_verified` landed in handoff 09 R29; `is_overdue` and the PM anchoring landed in handoff 10 R45 (the "never-serviced PM schedule" entry); `sla_breached` is recorded as built in `docs/STATUS.md` — it went **0 → 215 on `jaizbankplc` unattended, one minute after the upgrade**.
- 2026-09-07 · **Odoo's `Publisher: Update Notification` cron POSTs the company name, email and phone to `services.odoo.com` weekly, per database** — on these tenants that is NRS, GTB, Jaiz Bank and Lagos State Ministry of Health. Neutralised locally by `publisher_warranty_url` pointing at a closed port *and* deactivating the row (the server action passes `cron_mode=None`, so it raises rather than failing silently). **The cluster has been sending these all along**, because its config correctly has no `db_name`; stopping it there is a product decision and was left to the owner. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · **`vendra.network` resolves to `127.0.0.1` inside the Odoo container** — which is the container — so every INT-05 event dead-ended against Cadra itself. `docker-compose.19.yml` now maps it to `host-gateway`. The hostname is load-bearing: Frappe selects the site from the `Host` header, so rewriting the URL to `host.docker.internal` reaches the wrong site and 404s. · Handoff 08 Part B.
- 2026-09-07 · **A delivery that times out may still have landed, and the `event_id` idempotency is what saves it.** Vendra exceeded the config's 10s `rest_timeout`; Cadra recorded a failure for a request Vendra had processed, and the retry got `409 duplicate`. Handoff 06's ruling that a 409 is a delivery turned that into `acknowledged` rather than a spurious dead-letter — proven live on its first real timeout. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · **The cron watchdog is `limit_time_real` (120s) unless `limit_time_real_cron` is set.** A job over that has its worker SIGKILLed and its transaction rolled back, forever, on every retry. Harmless while the scheduler was dead; not harmless on a first tick carrying weeks of catch-up. `config/odoo.19.conf` now sets 1200, matching what k8s already gives the cluster. · Handoff 08.
- 2026-09-07 · **`max_cron_threads = 1` is sufficient for eight tenants, on measurement.** `WorkerCron` processes one database per call and only sleeps once its queue is empty, so one thread walks every tenant per cycle rather than one tenant per minute; `_trigger`'s NOTIFY both interrupts the sleep and puts the triggered database at the head of the queue. The whole first tick — 192 jobs, 8 databases, weeks overdue — took **3m38s**, longest single job 9.89s. It also matches the cluster, so raising it locally would make dev less representative. · Handoff 08.

- 2026-09-08 · `[landed]` **A report's `res_ids` are ids of the report's DECLARED MODEL, and that is settled at the boundary, not guessed at the render.** The print hook declined to stamp whenever `res_ids` could be tag-label ids; on `nrs19` 11,928 of 11,932 asset ids are, because every asset mints a label on creation and the two sequences advance together. Fixing the id space was necessary and NOT sufficient. Worse than "printing never records": Odoo's own Print menu goes through `/report/download`, which is read/write and calls `report_routes` as a plain Python call, so the route flag does not apply — printing records from the web client and silently does not from the published `GET /api/v1/barcode/print-url`. What changed is the justification: **"partner paywall" describes a shared cluster, and a client does not run on one.** The owner's single-tenancy ruling (2026-09-09, "Multi-tenancy is development and demo only") establishes that multi-tenancy is a development and demo arrangement only and every client runs single-tenant on its own server, so the cap's purpose is **rate limiting and nothing else**. It found a fifth site in the same file the handoff named (`team_workload`) and reduced 12 findings across three files to zero. What an AST genuinely cannot tell is "how many are there" from "how many did I just fetch" — `len(records) > limit` inside a paginator is correct code with the identical shape — so it does not try: four honest sites carry an inline `capped-count-ok:` comment in the CONTIGUOUS block above the line, and removing that comment fails the guard. The new code answered 661, 158 and 261, matching SQL exactly, and its per-category columns sum to the headline in the same response. · Handoff 13 A1/A2; the probe rows were deleted and the tenant is back at 261.

- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **Changing a client's pagination limits revokes its refresh families.** `/auth/refresh` already re-reads the limits off the client record, so widening bites on the next refresh; what was left open is a family that keeps rotating under the OLD ceiling for a 30-day sliding window, and narrowing is the direction that matters — a cap is reduced because somebody decided this credential should see less. Widening revokes too, because a rule with an exception is a rule somebody has to remember. **What this does NOT close: the access JWT carries the cap as a claim and is unrevocable for its TTL.** Every client on every tenant is at the 3600-second default today, which is the window the ruling itself calls acceptable; the 86400 a client can be configured with would not be. · Handoff 13 B1.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **The source IP is logged on `/auth/token` failures, with a label saying how much it is worth.** `ip_source` is `xff` (one declared hop — the caller), `xff+N` (the nearest trusted address, not the caller), or `peer`; a private, loopback or link-local value is additionally marked `,infra`, because a field that always reads the same is worse than no field. Only the RIGHT-most `X-Forwarded-For` entry is used — the one our own proxy appended — and it is printed only after `ipaddress` has parsed it, so an unauthenticated caller cannot append a field to our log line through a header value where spaces are legal and `ip=` is last. · Handoff 13 B2.

It did **not** take down `cadra19`, which ran first and passed, because that tenant has no custodian tracking values and the script returns before reaching the query. A branch that only executes where there is data is invisible on the empty tenant you test against — the same shape as the print-hook guard that fired on 99.97% of prints. Companion to the `groups_id` → `group_ids` note below. · Handoff 14.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **The chain of custody is recorded in `nrs.asset.write`, not in the transfer route.** The handoff said `POST /assets/<id>/transfer-custody` is the only writer of `custodian_id`; it is not. The field is in `_WRITABLE_FIELDS`, so `POST /assets` and `PATCH /assets/<id>` write it, `nrs.asset.transfer.action_complete` writes it, `myservice_identity.action_mark_retrieved` clears it when an employee leaves, and it is editable on the Odoo form. Recording in the route would have produced a chain incomplete on its first day — and **a gap in an append-only record is indistinguishable from a period of no movement**, which is the one failure this model cannot recover from. · Handoff 14 B, report question 2.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **`write` and `unlink` raise on `nrs.asset.custody` itself, not by ACL — and the message says it refuses sudo.** Every API route in this codebase calls `sudo()` on every request, and `sudo()` bypasses every ACL and record rule by design, so a guard expressed as configuration would be exactly as strong as the promise that nobody calls it. The asset link is `ondelete='restrict'` for the same reason: cascading would let anybody able to delete an asset delete its history with it. · Handoff 14; the same ruling as "`readonly=True` is a web-client hint, not an ORM guard", one model further on.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **A backfilled custody link says so on its face, and the API publishes the field.** `source = 'tracking_backfill'` marks a link reconstructed from the chatter tracking values. The reconstruction is faithful — those rows carry both employees, the acting user and the timestamp — but it begins where `custodian_id` became a tracked field, not where the register does, so three of eight tenants start empty and `nrs19` starts with one link against 11,878 assets. **An auditor told where the record begins can work with it; one who discovers the gap later cannot trust any of it, including the part that is complete.** · Handoff 14.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **A custody link snapshots both custodian names and the acting user's name.** A m2o to a deleted `hr.employee` goes null, so without the snapshot an append-only row could still be emptied of its content from outside the model — the same defect the model exists to remove, one join away. The link degrades, the evidence does not. · Handoff 14.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **`include=custody` is uncapped where `include=transfers` takes the last 20.** "The last twenty custodians" is precisely the answer an auditor cannot use: a truncated chain cannot be told from a complete one, so the question comes back wrong with no sign that it did. The paginated route exists for a chain long enough to want paging and inherits `paginate()`'s true `total_estimate` for free. · Handoff 14, report question 3.
- 2026-09-09 · `[landed]` **A client whose pagination limits differ from the default cannot hold a token above 3600 seconds.** The cap is a JWT claim and an access token is unrevocable for its lifetime, so the window in which a withdrawn limit is still in force cannot be removed — only bounded. `-1` (uncapped) is a non-default limit like any other, and the worst instance rather than an exemption. Every client on every tenant is already at 3600. · Handoff 14 C; the Architect's own correction of handoff 13's unachievable acceptance criterion.

- 2026-09-09 · **Odoo 19 applies ProxyFix only when the request carries `X-Forwarded-Host`** (`odoo/http.py`, `Application.__call__`), and the dev nginx sends `X-Forwarded-For` and `X-Forwarded-Proto` but not that one. So `proxy_mode = True` is not enough: `remote_addr` was the nginx container, and `last_used_ip` read `172.20.0.4` on every API client row in every local tenant. Cadra now resolves the address itself, identically with or without ProxyFix, and `last_used_ip` records the caller. · Handoff 13 B2; an ENVIRONMENT-FACTS candidate.
- 2026-09-09 · **Multi-tenancy is development and demo only; every client runs single-tenant on its own server.** Recorded because it silently reframes three things that were argued from the shared cluster — what a per-tenant measurement means, why the per-tenant upgrade fan-out exists, and what the external record cap is for. The fan-out stays mandatory here and is meaningless there; the cap stays and its reason changes from a shared-cluster paywall to rate limiting. · Owner, on requesting dedicated servers from NRS.

## 2026-09-10 — handoff 18

- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **A callback fires on the TRANSITION, not on the call.** Both callback routes acted on the CALL, so a second byte-identical POST answered `200 {"ok": true}` and did all three effects again — another dispatch-log row, another chatter post, and another write of the configured `approved_stage_id`. The third is the damaging one: a replay arriving after a human had moved the request onward **dragged it back**. Handoff 06 settled the shape in the other direction (`_mark_affixed` fires on the transition, one physical act is one event); this is the same rule pointed inbound. · Handoff 18 A.

## 2026-09-15 — handoff 23

- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **"The data does not support this" is a DELIVERABLE, and it is published in the response rather than in a document beside it.** §13.4's fourth report asks who was granted or removed from what, when, and by whom. Cadra cannot answer it and no query ever will: `res_groups_users_rel` is two integer columns, `(gid, uid)`, with no timestamp and no actor, so a grant writes nothing that outlives it; `res.users.group_ids` carries no `tracking=` in core, first-party, `crnd-19/` or `oca-19/`; `mail_tracking_value` holds **0 rows** on `res.users` and `res.groups` across all eight tenants; `ir_logging` holds **0 rows** because `log_db` is unset; and no audit module is installed or vendored. **Nothing was synthesised.** The endpoint reports the events that ARE evidenced — accounts created (`precision: exact`), accounts last modified (`precision: last_write_only`, one stamp covering every change ever made, not saying which field moved) — and `summary.group_changes_in_period` is a **structural zero in the payload** so a consumer reading counts sees the absence rather than inferring it from a missing key. · Handoff 23 B1.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A boundary that is a CONSTANT is a boundary that goes stale silently, so both reports re-measure theirs on every request.** `access_change_fidelity()` reads `information_schema` for the membership table's real columns and counts the tracking and logging rows live; `configuration_change_fidelity()` reads whether the `value` field is tracked. The day somebody adds `tracking=True` or installs an audit module, `coverage` flips to `partial` and the report says the record has grown — instead of continuing to publish a limitation that stopped being true. The verification pass proved the point immediately: the first version read `self._fields[field].tracking` directly and 500'd, because a plain `Many2many` in Odoo 19 does not carry the attribute at all. A hard-coded "no tracking" would have shipped and been wrong in silence. · Handoff 23 B.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **An audit report that publishes configuration must REDACT by key name, not by a list of the secrets somebody remembered.** `ir_config_parameter` is where `myservice_api.jwt_secret` lives — the key every access token in the tenant is signed with — beside `database.secret` and the relay, webhook and API keys. A configuration log that published values verbatim would hand the token-signing secret to every holder of the read scope, and an audit credential is precisely the broad, long-lived, widely-shared credential that must not carry it. The value becomes `[redacted] sha256:<16 hex>` — **no prefix, no last-four, no length**, because a truncated secret is a head start and a published length narrows a brute force; the fingerprint answers the only legitimate question a redacted value has, which is *is this the same value as the one over there*. The first rule required a delimiter on both sides and silently missed `database.secretkey`, `cadra.jwtSecret` and `myservice_api.apikey`; it is now two tiers, verified against 27 keys including that `cadra.concert_hall` and `cadra.salted_caramel` are **not** redacted — a rule that fires on innocent keys is a rule somebody switches off, and the real secrets go with it. · Handoff 23 B2.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **`Vendra Auditor` does not exist in Cadra, and the handoff's acceptance criterion assumed it did.** It is a Frappe role in the sibling repository; INT-10 §4.3 had already recorded Cadra's `audit_viewer` as absent. So `myservice_api.group_auditor` was built — six READ scopes, no write scope of any kind, **implied by nothing and implying nothing**, because an auditor who is also an Asset Manager is not independent of what they are auditing. **The `audit:read` scope §4.3 proposed was deliberately NOT built**: the five reports are views of the register, the identity roster and the configuration, so they take those things' scopes, and a scope no group grants makes a report unreachable on the day it ships — the ruling `v1_assets.py` already made. `settings:read` is new and is a READ scope rather than a reuse of `settings:write`, because an auditor whose only way to read the settings is to hold the right to change them is an auditor the control has been handed to. · Handoff 23 B, deviation from the instruction with evidence.

A second approval is `409` naming who holds the first, because an approval is a one-time act. Proved live: a call asking for approver uid 2 was refused, the next call recorded uid 287 — the caller — so the refused request wrote nothing. · Handoff 23 C, the Architect's ruling.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A guard proved against a fixture it INVENTED is not proved.** `test_deployment_profile.py` skipped any line without `odoo` on it, and the pin it existed to catch — `SERIES_BRANCH: '15.0'` — has no `odoo` on that line, nor do the two `- "15.0"` refs beside it. Restoring the real `.gitlab-ci.yml` produced **0 offenders**; the new logic produces **3**. It passed only because the mutation proof wrote `ODOO_SERIES_BRANCH`, a variable that never existed. The `odoo`-on-the-line requirement is replaced by an allow-list of the two non-Odoo version shapes these files actually contain, and the proof now restores the real file byte for byte from `tests/fixtures/retired-gitlab-ci.yml`. **The fixture is committed rather than recovered from git at run time** because `actions/checkout@v4` clones at depth 1, and a proof that skips where history is shallow is this file's own documented failure mode; a second test re-derives the bytes from history wherever the clone is deep enough to hold them. · Handoff 23 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A published query parameter that does nothing is the same defect as a filter that answers with everything.** Both new reports declared `limit` and `cursor` and read neither, so `?limit=1` returned every row, `?limit=abc` answered `200` where the disposal report answers `400`, and there was no `pagination` block at all — a caller walking `next_cursor` read `None` on page one and stopped, correct by accident. The roster was unbounded, which is the uncapped-response shape the external record cap exists to prevent, reachable with a read-only scope. **A page of the access report is a window of ACCOUNTS, not of events**, so the route spells out its own `limit` description instead of taking the shared one: `roster` and `events` are two views of the same accounts on one cursor, because paging them independently would let a caller hold an event for an account whose roster row is three pages away. Found by the test author, not by the brief. · Handoff 23, verification finding.

Four values now, and `partial` is **reachable but unreached**, so the report cannot keep publishing `last_write_only` for ever while the database holds more. · Handoff 23, verification finding.
- 2026-09-15 · **A test that is not imported never runs, and reports "0 failed" while doing it.** `myservice_api/tests/test_auditor_access.py` — the acting proof that is a named acceptance criterion of this handoff — was written and never registered in `myservice_api/tests/__init__.py`. The suite reported **121 tests, 0 failed**; with the import it reports **126**. It was caught by comparing the denominator across a change that should have moved it, which is the discipline CLAUDE.md asks for, applied to the one file whose absence nothing else would have revealed. · Handoff 23, found during verification.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A fix that reopens its own hole in a new shape is why the mutation table exists.** The replacement CI series guard exempted any line carrying a `*_version:` key **anywhere along it**, so `ODOO_VERSION: '15.0'` — the most natural spelling of the very pin the guard was rewritten to catch — was waved through **because it contains the word `version`**, along with `ODOO_SERIES_VERSION`, `odoo_addons_version` and every number on an inline-flow line with a `python-version:` key elsewhere on it. The exemption is now anchored to the key the number is the value OF, and a line naming `odoo` or `cadra` is exempt from nothing — there is no spelling of a pattern rule that gets `odoo_version` right, because it is a runtime version by name and a series pin in fact. **Ten must-flag and five must-not-flag lines are now a parametrised table.** · Handoff 23, second verification pass.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **An acceptance criterion carried by a docstring is not met.** Every automated test of `POST /assets/<id>/approve-disposal` asserted a **403**, because neither the pytest credential nor the Auditor holds `approvals:write` — so the 200 path, the approver actually written, the 400 that refuses a named approver and the 409 had **zero executed coverage**. That is a claim about the request path and only a request can prove it. - 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A published enum value the code can never return is a dead arm in every consumer that branches on it.** `ACCESS_COVERAGE_EMPTY` was declared, returned nowhere, and published as contract in the changelog, the OpenAPI description and INT-10 §2.10 — and the test asserted only that `coverage` was one of the three, **so it passed on the broken contract**. Deleted rather than made reachable: the record cannot hold a group change in ANY period, so a quiet quarter is not a different KIND of answer here, unlike the disposal report where an empty period genuinely is. It is the data §13.4's fourth report is for and `identity:read` is the identity register's scope, so the grouping is right — but a changelog line saying "no existing credential changes" beside it was not. Its own **WIDENING** section now. · Handoff 23, second verification pass.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A fingerprint never proved to move is not evidence.** Both read-only proofs had correct plumbing — flush, precommit, invalidate, per-table md5 — and **no negative control**, so a `FINGERPRINTED` dict naming tables that do not exist would have produced an identical before and after on every run and read exactly like a proof. - 2026-09-15 · **A test whose TITLE describes a different layer from its assertion is how a contract comes to have two answers.** The model sorted access events chronologically, the controller re-sorted by ascending account id so the cursor could page honestly, and the model's test asserted chronological order under the title *"a report an auditor reads top to bottom"* — a claim about the published payload, which did not have it. Nothing asserted the API's real order at all. - 2026-09-15 · **Three smaller defects a fresh reader found in code an hour old.** `fidelity.tracked` was true if ANY field on the model carried tracking, including one contributed by another module — a fidelity block that OVERSTATES is worse than one that is thin. The closing bound kept `23:59:59.000000`, a one-second hole at the top of every period documented as inclusive of the whole closing day. And the roster ran one `myservice.api.client` search per user, N+1 on a report that did not paginate. None was found by the author. · Handoff 23, verification findings.

## 2026-09-14 — handoff 22

- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A front end may not declare a set the back end owns, and the GUARD is the deliverable rather than the corrections.** Five enum sets in the SPA had drifted from the model and none of them ever said a word, because a `<select>` populated from a stale list is indistinguishable from one populated from a fresh one. Correcting five copies would have left the mechanism intact and bought a year. So `GET /api/v1/meta/enums` derives every published Selection from the registry at request time, `src/lib/enums.ts` is the one seed the front end may hold, and `tests/test_frontend_enums.py` parses the Odoo model source and the TypeScript and fails on any disagreement in **values, order or labels**. Order is checked deliberately: `priority`'s keys read like a scale, so a set right as a bag and wrong as a sequence is exactly the bug that shipped. Proved to fail first on four mutations, each a reconstruction of a real defect. · Handoff 22 A+B.
- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A form's default is a VALUE the model owns, not a position in a list the front end maintains.** The request form started on `"2"` because it is the middle of five options; `"2"` is High and the model's default is `"1"`. The screen said "Normal" while the database recorded High, and **35 requests across three tenants carry it**. `ENUM_DEFAULT` is now pinned to the model's `default=` by the guard, and typed with `satisfies` so the key cannot be absent — an unset value renders a select showing its FIRST option, which is the same defect one value along. · Handoff 22 A.
- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A value the current build does not recognise stays selected rather than being silently retyped.** `location_type` offered two values the model never had and omitted `wing`, so a wing opened for edit rendered a blank select and could not be saved back unchanged — the record was retyped by the act of being opened. `withCurrent()` appends an unrecognised key to the option list, labelled with itself, because showing the raw key is honest and showing nothing is how the record got lost. · Handoff 22 B.
- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A corrupt cursor is refused `400`, and documented silent degradation is still silent degradation.** `decode_cursor` returned `{}` for anything it could not decode, and `{}` means "start at the beginning", so a caller paging with a corrupted cursor silently restarted at page one while believing it was on page three. It was documented behaviour and it is withdrawn anyway: the argument that cleared handoff 21's twenty-six parameters applies unmodified — **an answer that looks like an answer is worse than a refusal**. The payload's SHAPE is checked too, not just its encoding: a token decoding to a non-object used to answer page one and a non-integer `last_id` used to answer **500**. An absent or empty cursor is a different input and keeps its answer. BREAKING, in `docs/api/CHANGELOG.md`. · Handoff 22 C.
- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A partition assertion that can pass on an empty half is not an assertion.** `?has_coordinates=false` — "which sites has nobody mapped" — answered **0** on a tenant where 250 of 380 sites are unmapped. `latitude`/`longitude` have no `default=`, so unset is SQL NULL and never `0.0`, and Odoo renders `('latitude','!=',0.0)` as `(latitude != 0 OR latitude IS NULL)` — putting every unmapped site on the has-coordinates side. Handoff 21's De Morgan reasoning was sound; its premise that these are "plain Floats defaulting to zero" was not, and the negation was checked against the comment rather than against the column. **The old behaviour passed a naive partition check** — 380 + 0 = 380 — which is CLAUDE.md's collapsed denominator one layer up. The regression test now asserts the sum AND that neither half is degenerate. · Handoff 22, verification finding.
- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A test that counts a PAGE cannot make a claim about a REGISTER.** `test_query_params._count` returned `len(data)`, bounded by the 50-row page limit, so its partition assertion sat behind `if unfiltered < 50` — "only check endpoints smaller than one page" — and never ran on `/api/v1/assets` at all. Silently: nothing skipped, nothing printed. It now reads `pagination.total_estimate`, which is documented as always being a `search_count` of the caller's unclamped domain, and the gate is gone. Mutation-proved: reverting `is_verified`'s false branch now fails where it previously passed. · Handoff 22, verification finding.
- 2026-09-14 · `[landed]` **A display value that is not the stored value will disagree with the filter built on it.** `CONDITION_MAP` collapsed `new → "Good"` for display while the filter sent `condition=good`, so **282 assets** across three tenants were labelled Good on screen, excluded from the Good filter, and reachable by no filter at all because "New" was not offered. The model's key now runs end to end and the model's label is rendered at the edges. Same shape as `fuel_type`, which showed `electric` and both plug-in hybrids as **CNG** and `hydrogen` as **Petrol**. · Handoff 22, verification finding.
- 2026-09-14 · **A guard that matches raw text flags its own explanation.** The first version of `test_frontend_enums` failed on the explanatory comments of the files it was checking — the trap `test_query_param_guard.py` names out loud, hit a second time by the next guard written. It strips comments and reads code; styling maps are waived by name with a reason, and a second test checks each waived file really has the fallback its waiver claims. `conditionConfig[condition]` was an unguarded index that would have thrown inside render, white-screening the asset register from a Python change. · Handoff 22.
- 2026-09-14 · **A handoff verified by its author is the weaker claim, and this is what it costs.** A fresh read-only pass over handoff 21's merged diff found nothing wrong with the mechanism and a good deal wrong with the evidence that the mechanism works: **four of its nine findings are tests or guards that pass without testing anything**, plus a missing CHANGELOG entry for ~26 parameters whose status code changed, a missed `priority` annotation, and an `include` parameter that refuses whitespace on one route out of three. · Handoff 22 §1.

## 2026-09-10 — handoff 21

- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **A filter whose value we cannot read is REFUSED, not dropped.** Seven booleans, thirteen enums and nineteen foreign keys across five controllers fell through every branch on an unrecognised value, appended no domain term, and answered with the list the caller would have received with no filter at all. **An empty list reads as "no matches"; a full list reads as an answer**, and nothing in the response tells the two apart — so `?is_verified=1` handed back the whole register and a client branching on the result treated every asset in it as verified. It is not hypothetical for an auditor: `?has_sap_code=false` is "which assets has finance not booked", `?has_coordinates=false` is "which sites has nobody mapped", `?has_deadline=false` is "which work has nobody put a date on", and each one answered with everything. Handoff 20 built the auditor a snapshot and a disposal report so they could satisfy themselves rather than ask us; a filter that answers with everything makes that worse, not better. `is_tagged` has been the correct shape since handoff 01 and is the precedent every other filter now follows. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **The validator is a door, and the door does no database work.** `myservice_api/controllers/query_params.py` — `parse_bool`, `parse_enum`, `parse_int`, `int_filter_terms`, `BadFilter` — **imports nothing at all** and never mentions the request, the environment or the cursor. That is a rule rather than tidiness: `utils._selection_allowed_values` is the obvious thing to reuse and is forbidden on a read path, because it calls `cr.rollback()` when it cannot read a field — written for the error path after a failed write, where the cursor is already dirty (ruling of 2026-09-06). A read path runs it on the happy path of every list request. `tests/test_query_param_guard.py::test_the_door_does_no_database_work` reads the module's AST, not its text, so its docstring may name what it forbids without tripping it. The accepted values of an enum still come from the model: `utils.selection_values(model, field, fallback)` reads the registry with a published floor and never touches the cursor, and it replaces the two hand-rolled readers `v1_assets` had grown. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **`priority` published four values and the model has five, so the DOCUMENTATION was corrected.** `request.request.priority` is `0 Low, 1 Normal, 2 High, 3 Critical, 4 Very Urgent`; the route published `["0","1","2","3"]` and described `3` as "urgent". Rule 4 decides it — the model wins — and this is the one place where getting it the other way round would have broken a caller: `tests/test_requests.py` creates a request with `priority: "4"`, so validating against the annotation would have 400'd our own suite. Every enum is validated against the model's live Selection rather than against its annotation, precisely so the accepted set can never be narrower than what the model allows. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **The Cadra SPA was sending `?condition=damaged`, a value the register has never had.** Rule 4's other direction: the client was wrong. `damaged` appears in no Odoo model — `nrs.asset.condition` is `new, good, fair, poor, decommissioned` — so the asset register's "Faulty" filter chip and the "Damaged" dashboard tile have always answered `200 []` and `0`, reading as "there are none" rather than "that filter does not work", and the create form's fifth option has always been rejected by the ORM. Server-side validation turns that silence into a 400, so the SPA is corrected in the same change: the phantom becomes the model's real fifth value and the label becomes "Decommissioned", which is what it means. `CONDITION_TO_API` was also a `Record<string, string>`, so an unmapped label yielded `undefined`, dropped the parameter and fetched the unfiltered register — the same defect as the backend's, in the client, and it is now typed against the model's set. · Handoff 21 A; found by the verification of the assets controller, not by the brief.
- 2026-09-10 · ⚠️ **WITHDRAWN IN PART — handoff 24.** `[landed]` **`has_coordinates` and `has_deadline` had only a `true` branch, so `false` returned everything.** Report 05's inventory named five booleans; there are seven, and two of them are this shape rather than the fall-through shape. `?has_coordinates=false` is the survey-gap question and `?has_deadline=false` the unscheduled-work question, and both answered with the whole list. The negation of `latitude != 0 AND longitude != 0` is `latitude = 0 OR longitude = 0`, which partitions the register exactly. `tests/test_query_params.py` asserts the partition rather than only the 400, because a test that checked the refusal alone would have called both of these fixed. · Handoff 21 A.
  - **Withdrawn 2026-09-15 (handoff 24), on report 22 §5.** Two claims in this entry were measured false. **First, the partition does not partition.** `latitude`/`longitude` are declared with no `default=`, so an unset coordinate is **SQL NULL and never `0.0`** — the premise "plain Floats defaulting to zero" was wrong. Odoo renders `('latitude','!=',0.0)` as `(latitude != 0 OR latitude IS NULL)`, so every unmapped site landed on the *has-coordinates* side: on `jaizbankplc`, `true → 380 of 380, false → 0` where 250 of 380 sites are unmapped. After the fix, `true → 130, false → 250`. The De Morgan reasoning was sound; the premise was not. **Second, the test cited as proof never ran.** `_count()` returned `len(data)`, capped at 50, so the partition assertion was gated behind "only test endpoints smaller than fifty rows" and never executed on `/api/v1/assets` at all — silently, nothing skipped, nothing printed (report 22 finding 4). Both repaired in handoff 22; see that handoff's own entries, dated 2026-09-14.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **`active` on the employees and locations lists did not return everything — it silently returned the DEFAULT.** Report 05 classified it with the fall-through cases; its `else` branch appends `active = True`, so `?active=1` answered with the active roster to a caller who asked for the inactive one. Worth stating precisely because it is arguably the worse of the two failures: an unfiltered list is conspicuously large, whereas a plausible active-only roster invites no second look. Absent and empty still mean `active = True`, which is published behaviour; only a value that is present and unreadable is a 400. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **Clamping and refusing are different answers to different inputs, and `limit` gets both.** `?limit=100000` is still silently clamped — the ceiling is per-credential, so a caller cannot know it in advance and refusing them for asking would be unhelpful, and that is the published contract. `?limit=all` is now refused: it is not a number, and serving fifty rows to a caller who believes they asked for everything is the same false statement the filters used to make. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **`cadra_endpoint` catches `BadFilter` and answers 400 WITHOUT a rollback.** Every list route also catches it around its own domain builder; this is the floor under those, so a route written next year answers 400 rather than the 500 a bare exception produces — and a 500 tells an integration to retry a request that can never succeed. The absent rollback is the point rather than an omission: the exception is raised at the door, before the request has touched the database, and rolling back a clean cursor is the exact behaviour that ruled the existing helper out of the read path. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **`.gitlab-ci.yml` is deleted, and a guard now covers CI configuration the way one already covered Dockerfiles.** It pinned `SERIES_BRANCH: '15.0'`, named a `registry.crnd.pro` image and a GitLab that is not a remote of this repository, and had not been touched since an assembly bot wrote it in March 2023. Report 20 left it as inert; inert is exactly the state `Dockerfile.odoo.dev` was in when it was the thing the prod overlay pointed at. A pipeline definition nobody runs is what somebody wires up when they next want a pipeline. The guard reads first-party CI files only — `crnd-19/.gitlab-ci.yml` is vendored and theirs — and skips YAML comments, because `.github/workflows/ci.yml` explains the 15-to-19 history in one and a guard that flags its own explanation gets satisfied by deleting the explanation. · Handoff 21 B1.
  - **Annotated 2026-09-15 (handoff 24).** The deletion landed and stands; **the clause "a guard now covers CI configuration" was not true when it was written.** The guard skipped any line without `odoo` in it, and the pin it existed to catch — `SERIES_BRANCH: '15.0'` — has no `odoo` on that line; its mutation proof passed only because it wrote `ODOO_SERIES_BRANCH`, a variable that never existed (report 22 finding 3). Restoring the real file produced **0 offenders**. Repaired in handoff 23 A, which took the restored file to **3 offenders** — see that handoff's entry.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **A credential carrying a line break is refused by name, before anything is written.** `config/prod-entrypoint.sh` appends three values to an INI file with `printf '%s\n'`, and an INI file is line-oriented: everything after a break in a value becomes a directive Odoo obeys, so `ODOO_MASTER_PASSWORD=$'x\nworkers = 0'` writes a real `workers = 0` into the running config with nothing saying so. A stray `\r` from a `.env` saved on Windows lands in the same place with no intent at all. **Refusing the input, not hardening the `printf`** — there is no legitimate credential with a line break in it, so the narrow rule is the whole fix. The value is never echoed: a credential that has just been shown to contain something the operator did not intend is the last thing to put in a container log. · Handoff 21 B2, report 20's open item.
- 2026-09-10 · **`kanban_state`, `has_deadline` and five foreign keys were accepted by the requests domain builder and published by nothing.** A parameter a route acts on but does not declare is invisible to Swagger, to a generated client and to any inventory built from the annotations. All are now declared; the guard reads both directions — every declared parameter needs an inventory entry, and every parameter a `*_domain` function reads must be declared — so the drift cannot reopen. · Handoff 21 A.
- 2026-09-10 · **The guard is a pinned inventory because the judgement it encodes is a person's.** An AST reader can see the route decorator, the parameter list and the `parse_*` call; it cannot see whether a free-text `q` *should* have a closed set. So `INVENTORY` names every parameter and its kind, a waiver has to carry the reason it is waived, and a stale entry for a parameter nobody declares any more is a failure — a waiver list nobody prunes is how a waiver list stops meaning anything. Both mutations were measured on the real tree: deleting a live `parse_bool` call fails it, and adding an undeclared parameter fails it. · Handoff 21 A; the same shape as handoff 13's and handoff 20's, and right both times.
- 2026-09-10 · `[landed]` **`pg_isready` answering yes once is not readiness, and the bootstrap no longer asks twice.** CI on this branch died with `postgres ready after 2s` and `FATAL: postgres did not become ready` a hundred milliseconds apart, and the same commit passed on the next run. The postgres image runs a TEMPORARY server during initdb and shuts it down before starting the real one; `pg_isready` says yes to it. `scripts/bootstrap.sh` broke its wait loop on that first yes and then ran a **second, independent confirmation probe with no retry**, which landed during the shutdown. A check that passes followed by the same check failing — the identical shape as the inline prod start-up shell that refused every correct configuration, and it gets the identical treatment. Readiness must now HOLD for three consecutive probes (`CADRA_PG_READY_STREAK`), the loop's own outcome is carried forward instead of re-probed, the reported second is when readiness was CONFIRMED rather than first hoped for, and the failure says how close it got and dumps the database log. The assignment is written longhand rather than as `check && pg_ready=1`, which is the form that kills a `set -e` script on the branch that SUCCEEDS. `tests/test_bootstrap_pg_wait.py` extracts the shipped block between two markers and RUNS it against a stubbed `docker`, needing neither docker nor a database; restoring the original loop reproduces the CI failure deterministically. **The three k8s init containers share the single-probe latch but not the double-probe bug** — they `exit 0` on first success, so the worst case is a pod that restarts into a working database rather than a red build. Named, not fixed, on a branch about query parameters. · Handoff 21, found after the PR was opened.

- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **Group membership becomes a recorded fact, and the record starts now.** `res_groups_users_rel` is two integer columns, `(gid, uid)`, with `ON DELETE CASCADE` on both foreign keys: a grant writes nothing that outlives it and a removal deletes the only evidence it happened. Handoff 23 measured six places it could have been recorded and found all six empty; that is a property of the schema and no query can work around it. The reconstruction of PAST grants remains NRS's decision (INT-10 §2.10b), but the forward record is not that decision, and every week without one is a week that can never be recovered. `myservice.api.access.change` is append-only on the model rather than by ACL — `write` and `unlink` raise unconditionally so the guard holds under `sudo()`, and `create` refuses without the flag the single writer sets. · Handoff 24 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **The handoff's list of membership writers was wrong in three places, and a `res.users.write` override would have recorded about half of them.** Verified against Odoo 19 core, not assumed. **Group implication writes no membership rows at all** — `res.groups.all_implied_ids` is declared `compute=` with no `store=True`, so adding an implication grants every member of a group the rights of another while `res_groups_users_rel` is untouched; the handoff expected "implied rows an auditor will ask about" and there are none. **`res.config.settings` group toggles are that case too**, not membership: `_apply_group` writes `implied_ids` and never reaches `res.users`. **Reified group fields are gone from 19** — no `sel_groups_*`, no `in_group_*` — so the admin user form submits the real field and an ORM override does catch it. The hooks are therefore on `res.users` write/create/unlink and on `res.groups` write for BOTH `user_ids` and `implied_ids`. · Handoff 24 A; the fourth time a claimed single writer has been checked and the third time it was wrong.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **The hooks diff real membership instead of reading `vals`.** `(6, 0, ids)` does not say what it replaced; `_default_groups()` puts a user in a group the create never mentioned; `auth_signup` creates by `copy()`, where groups are cloned and appear in no vals; and a `Command.link` on a row that already exists must not produce a record. The diff is the only thing that reports what actually moved — and it is what keeps the eight security XML files that re-apply on EVERY module upgrade from filling the record with noise. Measured: upgrading all eight tenants produced **zero rows**. · Handoff 24 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A change nobody can attribute says so, rather than naming somebody.** `uid 1` is an upgrade, a cron or the command line; the row carries the actor `__system__` with `attributable = false` and no `changed_by_id`. Report 23 measured 100% of this estate's ACL stamps as `uid 1`, and a record that rendered those as "the system administrator granted this" would invent a person — worse than no record, because it is actionable and wrong. · Handoff 24 A property 1.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **The record knows when it begins, per tenant, by measurement — and a period before that still answers "cannot answer".** `fidelity.record_starts_at` is read from the earliest row held, at request time, and is `null` until the first change is recorded. It is deliberately NOT the migration date: a tenant upgraded and left alone has a record beginning when something first happened on it, and claiming the upgrade instant would assert coverage of a period in which nothing was being written down. `summary.group_changes_in_period` stops being a structural zero; a third coverage value `recorded` joins `none` and `partial`, and which one a caller gets is decided by where the period sits relative to the start. A period ending before the record began is `none` however much the record now holds. · Handoff 24 A property 2.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **An implication change is counted, not folded into the grants.** It moves what a group MEANS rather than who is in it, and it names no user — so forcing it into `data.events`, which the controller pages by user id, would either drop it silently or break the page contract. `summary.implication_changes_in_period` carries it instead. · Handoff 24 A.
- 2026-09-15 · **The test suite writes into a client's register and nothing stops it.** `CADRA_API_BASE` is the only thing choosing the target database — no allowlist, no refusal, no confirmation — and the only tenant carrying a full credential pair is `jaizbankplc`, a live client's replica. The suite has no teardown by documented policy and could not have one: the API publishes exactly one `DELETE` route. Measured after ~154 sessions since 2026-08-26: **88% of that register's assets, 73% of its custody rows and 84% of its PUBLIC homepage location dropdown are test fixtures**, plus six fabricated approved disposals in the auditor-facing report. Nothing ever reached the production host and no real asset carries a fabricated approval. · Handoff 24 B.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **The fabricated approvals are marked, not un-approved.** Disposal approval is deliberately irreversible — no `action_unapprove_disposal`, a published 409 on a second approval, approver fields read-only over the API — and correctable only by a human on the Odoo form under tracking. Fabricating a reversal would be the same defect as the fabricated approval, one step on. `scripts/mark_test_fixtures.py` discloses them and brings the `Handoff23 Approver` credential into the repo's own `is_test_credential` convention, which it had bypassed. **The script is owner-run: the Builder's permission layer refuses a committing write to a client-derived tenant.** · Handoff 24 B.
- 2026-09-15 · `[ruled]` **`Finance Officer` / `finance_viewer` is `group_auditor` under another name and will not be built as a second group.** "Asset financial data" resolves to four blocks of the asset serializer — `financial`, `sap`, `disposal`, `provenance` — plus `GET /reports/depreciation` and `GET /settings/depreciation`, every one reached by `assets:read`, `dashboards:read` or `settings:read`, all three of which `group_auditor` grants. **There is no field-level access control anywhere in Cadra**: `grep "groups=" --include=*.py` across every first-party module returns nothing, `serialize_asset` takes no scope argument and emits all four blocks unconditionally, and every route runs `sudo()` so an Odoo group cannot subtract. The finest cut available is route × scope, and financial fields are not a route. A `finance_viewer` built today would be `group_auditor` minus three NON-financial scopes — a narrower auditor, not a finance role. Noted separately: specification §3.4/§3.5 cast Finance as a disposal APPROVER, which is `approvals:write` and a different question. · Handoff 24 D; the deliverable is a line in INT-10 §4.3, not a group.


## 2026-09-15 — handoff 25

- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **Safety is a property of the DATABASE, recorded IN the database — and absent refuses.** `cadra.database_class` is a system parameter a person sets deliberately (`test` / `client` / `production`); both test suites refuse to start against anything but `test`, and a database nobody has classified is refused exactly as a production one is. Every other candidate was rejected for the same reason: **the name** (`test19` reads safe and `cadra19` does not, and both are wrong the day a client dump is restored into one), **the environment variable** (`CADRA_API_BASE` is the control that just failed), **a path, a hostname or a branch** (none survives a restore either). **There is no bypass flag and no override variable, deliberately** — a flag added for one legitimate afternoon becomes how everyone runs the suite by the end of the month. The refusal is loud, names the database and its class, and prints the exact command that marks a database; it is a failure and never a skip, because report 23 §3 established that a skip is a guard that passes. · Handoff 25 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **The guard reads the parameter row with SQL, NOT `get_param` — found by watching it leak.** `ir.config_parameter.get_param` is `@ormcache`d per worker, and under the committed `workers = 2` the invalidation does not reliably reach a worker between requests. The first cut used it, and during its own proof run the route reported `database_class = "client"` for a database whose parameter row had been **deleted**, while the suite — pointed at a database carrying no mark at all — ran **24 writing tests to a green pass**. A guard served from a stale cache says yes about a state that has stopped being true, which is the whole failure class the guard exists to close. One row, by primary key, once per session or test class. `tests/test_source_guards.py` fails if the cached reader returns, and its mutation proof exercises the exact code that leaked. · Handoff 25 A; found by proving the guard rather than by writing it.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **Both suites converge on one question, asked at the first point each can ask it.** The HTTP suite asks through `GET /api/v1/meta/database` and **`token` depends on the guard**, not the other way round — every writing path reaches the API through `client`, and `client` through `token`, so there is no way to authenticate around it. It is deliberately NOT an `autouse` fixture: that also fires for the static suites (`test_source_guards.py`, `test_deployment_profile.py`) which are documented to run with no instance up, and gating authentication covers strictly more anyway, since an unauthenticated test cannot write. The in-registry suite asks in `setUpClass` on a shared base, because **there is no earlier point that has an environment**: Odoo runs a module's tests from inside `load_module_graph` (`odoo/modules/loading.py:279`) immediately after that module is upgraded and long before `_register_hook` at STEP 9 — so the obvious one-line `_register_hook` guard runs after every test in the registry has already finished. · Handoff 25 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A test class that escapes the guarded base escapes the guard, so the discipline is itself a test.** All 39 first-party Odoo test classes across 13 modules now derive from `CadraTransactionCase` / `CadraHttpCase`, and `tests/test_source_guards.py` fails on any class deriving straight from a core base — matched on the AST and on all three spellings (`TransactionCase`, `common.TransactionCase`, `odoo.tests.common.TransactionCase`), including a core base sitting second in a multiple-inheritance list, which the first pass missed on two real classes. · Handoff 25 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **A fixture may not mint a credential that lies about itself.** `is_test_credential` is what makes a fixture identifiable afterwards — it drives `nrs.asset.custody.source = 'test'`, which is why 352 custody rows self-label with nobody curating them (2026-09-15; 330 when handoff 24 measured it). Handoff 23 minted `Handoff23 Approver` with a direct `create()` and the flag defaulted off, and its six approved disposals are the ONE class of artefact in `jaizbankplc` a reader cannot identify from the record itself. There is now one path — `create_api_client()`, which **forces** the flag rather than defaulting it — a source guard that fails on any direct `create()` of that model in a first-party test, and a session guard that refuses the whole HTTP run if the credential it authenticates with is unflagged. A test needing an unflagged credential to prove the flag's own behaviour toggles it on a record it already holds, which is what `test_custody_is_not_capped.py` already did. · Handoff 25 A.
- 2026-09-15 · `[landed]` **CI is stamped, not exempted — and `bootstrap.py` never guesses `test`.** There is no CI branch anywhere in the guard; an exemption is how a guard acquires its first hole. `scripts/bootstrap.py` records the class from an explicit `CADRA_DATABASE_CLASS`, and CI sets `test` when it builds its throwaway database, so the statement is true rather than waived. **This script is also the production deploy path** (`CADRA_PROFILE=prod scripts/bootstrap.sh`), so stamping `test` unconditionally would have marked every client's live database as safe to write fixtures into — the exact inversion of the control. Failing an explicit value, a `prod` profile stamps `production` (the one inference that cannot weaken the guard) and anything else writes nothing at all.
