- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **A maintenance request's reference is published in the asset history to a holder of `requests:read`**, and `detail.reference_withheld` says when it was not. The scope rule was extracted from `require_jwt` into `_missing_scopes`, so the gate and the shaping ask one question in one place rather than each keeping a copy of the `admin`/`*` wildcard.
- 2026-09-21 · **`sap_reconciliation_state = exception` is live, and is wired as a review status.** Measured: 34 rows on `nrs19`, 184 on `test19`, 593 on `jaizbankplc`, none elsewhere — **and every one of the 811 is a demo or a test fixture**, because it takes an affixed tag with no SAP code and only demo and test assets have been tagged on these databases. That is not the same as dead: it is a stored compute with no clock, and it fires the day a real asset is tagged and finance has not booked it. The three statuses handoff 29 deleted could never be reached; this one is reached by the most ordinary act in the programme.
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **Location is not backfilled.** Report 28 §3's measurement is the argument. Recorded as decided.

## 2026-09-21 — handoff 31 (one answer per rule)

- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **Custody and movement adopt `nrs.asset.append.only.mixin`. The mixin, not the rows.** Their own copies of `create`, `write`, `unlink` and `_resolve_source` are gone; each keeps its context keys, the sources a caller may claim, and the wording of its refusals. No migration, no id changed, no published name changed. The three `myservice_api` overrides that were supposed to be one derivation — and were three different pieces of code — are ONE, on the mixin (`myservice_api/models/append_only.py`), so a fourth append-only record cannot be written without it.
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **Custody's and movement's ROWS are NOT brought into `nrs.asset.event`, and this is not to be reopened on aesthetics.** `nrs.asset.custody` and its ids are named in some twenty files under `docs/` (nineteen when report 30 counted; this handoff's own documents have added to it), among them `INT-10-audit-access.md`, the contract an external auditor reads; `custody_id` and `movement_id` are published response keys; every history event carries `record: {model, id}`. Merging two sequences into one table changes every id on every tenant, and a migration is a rewrite of the two tables whose whole value is that nobody rewrites them. **Tidying our schema is not worth a breaking change to the audit contract.**
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **A body field a route does not accept is a 400, and nothing is written. BREAKING, announced, no deprecation window.** `400 invalid_request`, `details.rejected_fields`, `details.writable`; ELEVEN routes, one helper (`refuse_unwritable`), called before any write. **Report 30 counted ten, by searching for routes that REPORTED a dropped field in `_warnings` — so `POST /asset-categories`, which dropped fields and reported nothing, was invisible to the search.** It answered 201 to `{"name": …, "method": …}` and set no policy: the depreciation bug that justified this ruling, at the one door the count missed. Found by the test author reading the file. Still not covered, and not ruled: the ACTION routes (`/move`, `/verify`, `/tag-affixed`, `/assign`…) read the keys they know and ignore the rest. The `PATCH` whose every field was refused — 200, having written nothing — goes with it. **The argument was the consumer's, read from its source:** Vendra already dead-letters a rejected field, but only after Cadra had created the asset, incomplete, under an idempotency key that replayed the same 201 for ever. The forward-compatibility argument is noted and overruled: a consumer sending a field we do not accept is wrong about our schema, and the sooner it is told the cheaper the repair.
- 2026-09-21 · **A refusal does not burn the idempotency key — checked, because the opposite is the obvious fear.** Vendra's key is deterministic across retries, so a STORED 400 would have blocked a corrected mapper for ever. `cadra_endpoint` stores a response only when it is not an error. Acted: bad body 400 · same body 400 · corrected body, SAME key, 201 · again, 201 and the same asset.
- 2026-09-21 · **What the 400 uncovered: `POST`/`PATCH /requests` dropped a request's location for the life of the route — and BOTH of the Builder's first two readings of it were wrong.** The request model's location field became `generic_location_id` in the move to Odoo 19; `v1_requests` went on asking the model for `location_id`, refused the key because the model did not define it, and answered 201. The endpoint reference's canonical example, the OpenAPI example, the Cadra app's request form (which has a location picker), the test suite's fixture and the smoke test all sent one for the life of the API; `jaizbankplc` holds 460 requests and none has a location. `?location_id=` on the list was ignored the same way and answered with everything, and `GET /requests` published `location: null` whatever a request held. **The first reading was "the register cannot hold a request's location"** — reached by checking `ir_model_fields` for `location_id` alone — and on it the Builder ruled the app's picker removed, the fixture's location dropped, and wrote that conclusion into five documents. It was overturned two hours later by a comment the front-end author had left in the form: *the model's field is `generic_location_id`*. The independent sweep had named that field too, and its meaning was missed. **Fixed in the API instead**: the published key is the contract, and translating it onto the model's field is the controller's job (`_location_field`). Saved, published as `data.location`, a working filter; the picker stays; the fixture and the smoke test now FAIL unless the location comes back, which is the assertion nobody had ever made. **Then the correction over-corrected.** The rewritten documents said a request's location had *never been saved, by anyone*, that no earlier request had one and that none could be recovered — and cited *"`nrs19`: zero requests with a location"*, which was vacuous: `nrs19` has zero requests. The verification pass ran one SELECT. `POST /home/quick-submit`, the PM scheduler, fleet maintenance and the Odoo form had all written `generic_location_id` correctly all along; `cadra19` holds 80 requests and all 80 have a location, which the API read back as `null`. So the fix is also a READ-side change — those locations now appear — and it is announced as one. Two absolutes in one afternoon, each reached by stopping at the first query that agreed. It is the depreciation-method bug one layer down — a published name and the thing underneath answering to another — and it was very nearly "fixed" by deleting the feature.
- 2026-09-21 · **`approve-disposal` refuses ANY body key, and refuses it before it looks at the asset.** It reads no body, so `details.writable` is `[]`. A stray key on an already-approved asset is therefore a 400 where it was a 409: a malformed request is malformed whatever state the record is in, and the approver-claim refusal has always come first for the same reason. Both of that route's refusals now carry the same two keys.
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **The MODEL stamps a disposal — date and condition — for every writer** (`nrs.asset._stamp_disposal`). `/condemn` being the proper door did not close the others. The two `action_condemn` overrides that each stamped the date are deleted: three answers to one rule became one. **Two judgements inside it, both mine:** a `condition` sent beside `state: disposed` is OVERRIDDEN, because `decommissioned` is what disposed means here and a disposed asset in `good` condition is how the condition tiles came to count things that are gone; and **`create` is NOT stamped** — an asset registered as already disposed was disposed of on a day this system never saw, and stamping today would replace a finding the disposal report can show (no date) with a date that is wrong, which it cannot.
- 2026-09-21 · **What `no_disposal_date` now means: a legacy row, an asset registered as already disposed with no date given, or a date somebody later cleared.** Measured the same day across eight databases, before this handoff's tests ran: TWO undated disposals existed — `pytest-h29-undated-disposal` on `test19` and `H23 disposal probe` on `jaizbankplc`, both our own probes. One door remains and is named rather than shut: `disposal_date` is writable, so a `PATCH {"disposal_date": null}` on a disposed asset un-dates it.
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **Verification of a disposed asset stays ALLOWED.** *A disposed asset somebody physically finds on the premises is an audit finding, and a register that refuses to record "I saw it, today" is suppressing the one fact that matters.* The Odoo form hid Verify on anything but an active or missing asset while the model, the API and the app all allowed it — two surfaces, one rule, two answers. The form now offers it in every state. **Do not tidy this away.**
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **`POST /assets/<id>/mark-found`, a reason required.** *Where it turned up* is as much an audit fact as *why it went missing*, and an asset that reappears through a bare PATCH records nothing about the most interesting event in its life. 409 unless the asset is missing. It does not verify: whoever found it may not be whoever checks it.
- 2026-09-21 · `[ruled]` **A disposed asset is not on a leaver's clearance list.** `action_flag_retrieval` skips it, and `has_pending_retrieval` ignores a flagged asset that is later disposed — which, since disposal became terminal, could otherwise never have been cleared. No asset on any of the eight databases was flagged, so there was nothing to repair.
- 2026-09-21 · `[open]`, **NRS's — what `transferred` means.** Published, filterable, selectable, and nothing writes it. Not removed on our judgement and no producer invented. The question, and what the register does today if somebody sets it by hand, are report 31 §5. If the answer is that it means nothing, it is removed under this release's announcement.

- 2026-09-22 · **The sweep is a guard, not an afternoon** (`myservice_api/tests/test_published_names_resolve.py`, 12 tests). Every published key in the API against the model field it claims to write, both directions, **driven by something that cannot miss a case**: routes come from the routing attribute Odoo stamps on every routed method — not from `_ROUTE_REGISTRY`, which the nine pre-envelope routes never joined, and not from a search for allow-lists, which is why handoff 31 found eleven routes and there were thirteen — and the read side comes from parsing every controller. A route or a guarded read the tables do not name FAILS rather than being skipped. **The write side found one mismatch and it was the known one** (`location_id` → `generic_location_id`); the read side found twenty-two defensive field checks and all twenty-two resolve. **The tables read the gates themselves rather than copying them** — `SAP_FIELD_MAP`'s twelve translations are imported, not transcribed, because a second copy of a rule is the defect this series is named after.
- 2026-09-22 · **The first draft of the sweep could not see the helper it was written about.** `_location_field` spells its check `for name in ("location_id", "generic_location_id"): if name in Req._fields` — loop variable, not literal — so a parser that reads string constants is blind to it. Caught by the table's own staleness check reporting two entries the parser had stopped finding. A dynamic read is now reported as its own kind and must be named, and `_location_field`'s pair is asserted by hand **in both directions**: that fallback is correct only while the OLD name is the absent one, so the day `request.request` gains a `location_id` again it starts writing the wrong column silently.
- 2026-09-22 · **A key you can write and can never read back is the same asymmetry as the depreciation-method bug, with nothing to compare against.** Four found, all pre-existing, none fixed here because publishing a key is additive and renaming one is breaking: `salvage_rate` (published only as the derived `salvage_value`), `verification_notes` (published only in the verify response), `mobile`→`mobile_phone` (published by nothing), and six asset keys accepted under one name and published under another — `depreciation_method`→`financial.depreciation.method`, `model_name`→`model`, `location_id`→`current_location` among them. A screen that GETs an object, edits it and PATCHes the whole thing back is refused on every one of those six, with a 400 since handoff 31. Named in `docs/GO-LIVE.md`; not touched.
- 2026-09-22 · **`hr.employee.email` and `.phone` are NON-STORED relateds to `user_id.email` / `user_id.phone`** — the linked Odoo user's login address, not the employee's. The SAP feed sends `email` and writes `work_email`, which is right; writing `email` would rewrite a user account, and for the employees who have no user account it would write nowhere at all. Found by the new guard on its first run, recorded with that reason rather than waived, because the same-named field is exactly what a future maintainer would "correct" it to.
- 2026-09-22 · **`CLAUDE.md`: never write "zero have it", write "zero of how many".** Three defects in three handoffs came from a count with no denominator — handoff 27's "1,146 tracked moves" (one seeding script), handoff 30's "811 `exception` rows" (every one a fixture) and handoff 31's "a request's location has never been saved, by anyone" (from `nrs19`, which has zero requests; `cadra19` had eighty and all eighty had one). Knowing about it has demonstrably not prevented the next one, which is why it is a rule rather than advice.
- 2026-09-22 · `[ruled]` **A disposed asset's `disposal_date` cannot be emptied.** It was the one remaining way to manufacture the disposal report's `no_disposal_date` finding against a NEW disposal, on a register whose real opening balance for it is zero. Correcting the date to another day is a proper act and is untouched. **Implemented as a COMPARISON, not a presence check**, which is a deliberate deviation: an asset that is ALREADY undated may still be sent an empty date without a refusal, because refusing on presence would make every legacy undated disposal uneditable from the Odoo form, which re-sends every field it displays. That is the same argument the three refusals beside it already turn on — naming a protected field is not changing it.
- 2026-09-22 · `[ruled]` **Only a date arriving in the SAME write as the disposal is the caller's date.** `_stamp_disposal` read `self.disposal_date` back after the write, which cannot tell a date sent WITH the disposal from one PATCHed months before it: `disposal_date` is writable on a live asset, so an asset given last year's date while still in service and disposed of today was dated last year and the register said it left a year before it did. It now takes the write's `vals` — a REQUIRED positional argument, so a second caller has to decide rather than inherit the old behaviour by leaving it out. **The reading was available and was proved rather than assumed**: one call site, `vals` in lexical scope, no `write` override on `nrs.asset` injecting the key before `super()`, `create` never reaching it, and every door into `disposed` walked. **This reverses a test handoff 31 shipped with a stated justification** — *"paperwork written up before the act: the date was agreed at the board of survey and the status follows a week later"* — and the cost is real: that board of survey now loses its agreed date to today unless it sends the date in the same write or corrects it afterwards, and nobody is told at the time. `disposal_date` is tracked, so the previous value survives in the record.
- 2026-09-22 · **What `no_disposal_date` means after this: no first-party door can produce it on a new disposal.** A row carrying it is one of exactly three things — a legacy row disposed of before handoff 31's stamp existed; an asset REGISTERED as already disposed with no date, because `create` is deliberately not stamped and the system never saw the day it happened; or a column written outside the ORM, which is how `test_disposal_report.py` builds its legacy fixture and it says so.
- 2026-09-22 · **The twelfth door was the twelfth and the thirteenth**, and a fourteenth is left open with a ruling asked for. `POST /home/quick-submit` and `POST /integrations/sap/employee-sync` create records by handing a body to a function taking `**kwargs`; both now name what they accept and refuse the rest with the same 400 as the other eleven, before anything is written. **A batch is refused whole** — `employee-sync` takes a list, and refusing the eleventh employee after ten have been created would leave a partial sync under an idempotency key that replays the same answer on every retry. Quick-submit's bare `except (ValueError, TypeError): pass` and unchecked `.exists()` are gone too, as `details.unusable_fields` — deliberately NOT `rejected_fields`, because those keys ARE accepted and the repair is to fix the value, not to drop the key. **The rule lives once, in the delegate**, because the same Python answers the public homepage form; what differs is the rendering — a sentence for a visitor, `details.rejected_fields` for an integration. **`myservice_identity/controllers/sap_webhook.py`'s `auth='none'` twin is NOT closed**: measured first — `nrs.sap.sync.log.sync_type` is `webhook` on 0 rows across all eight local databases and `api` on 163, all of them on databases our own suites write to — so nothing here is evidence either way, and a refusal on an unauthenticated door a live upstream may be pointed at is a decision about an outage rather than a tidy-up.
- 2026-09-22 · **`status` on the SAP employee feed was published by four places and read by none.** The OpenAPI `SapEmployee` schema published it with an enum — `active`/`inactive`/`terminated`, of which `inactive` is not a value this system has — the endpoint reference's canonical example sent it, the demo card sent it, and this repository's own `tests/test_integrations.py` had been sending it since it was written. `nrs.sap.sync.log` reads `employmentStatus` or `sap_status`. So every integrator following Cadra's own documentation sent an employment status that went nowhere, under a 200. **Third instance of the same shape** after the category's `depreciation_method`/`method` and the request's `location_id`/`generic_location_id`. Per rule 4 the model wins and the documents are corrected; `status` and `officeCode` are now a 400. **The same was true of quick-submit's own documented example** — `name`, `email`, `phone`, `subject`, four of five keys the route has never read — and `tests/test_home.py` had been sending them since it was written, because the test was written from the example.
- 2026-09-22 · **The verification pass found two ways past the new guard, and both were the guard failing its own rule.** Its module list was a hand-written dict of ten while its read side walked the controllers directory — so a NEW controller file would have had its reads parsed and its routes missed entirely, and a route handing an unfiltered body into `create()` could be added with all twelve tests passing. **A maintained list beside a mechanical one, inside the file written against exactly that.** Both halves now list the same directory, proved by adding a real controller with a real route and watching the census name it. Second: a route declaring no `methods` was read as GET and skipped, when Odoo accepts every method on one — latent today, but the nine pre-envelope routes use bare `@http.route`, which is where the next one would be written. Such a route is now reported as `ANY` and must be classified.
- 2026-09-22 · **`nrs.asset.disposal_date` IS tracked, and the verification pass's headline finding that it is not was wrong** — worth recording because it is this handoff's own lesson pointed back at us. Its evidence was sound and its conclusion false: `models/asset.py:156` declares the field with no `tracking=True`, and `asset_disposal.py`'s module docstring calls it *"an untracked `disposal_date`"*. But `asset_disposal.py:285` declares it a SECOND time on `_inherit = 'nrs.asset'` — the same model — with `tracking=True`, which handoff 16 §B2 added for precisely this reason, and the docstring describes what INT-10 measured before that module existed. Proved by acting: a stale date PATCHed onto a live asset, the status written afterwards, and the chatter row reading `disposal_date old=2025-01-31 new=2026-09-22`. **A field declared in one file and answering to a second declaration elsewhere, and a conclusion drawn from the file that was opened first.** One file is not a search.

## 2026-09-22 — handoff 34 (warranty)

- 2026-09-22 · **Cadra accepts the warranty keys from `myservice_api` 19.0.3.12.0 (`myservice_asset_register` 19.0.1.18.0), and Cadra goes first — there is no order in which Vendra sends before Cadra accepts.** Since handoff 31 a key Cadra does not accept is a 400 for the whole body, so a Vendra that sent warranty fields to an older Cadra would dead-letter every INT-03 hand-off. The change is additive: nothing renamed, nothing that was accepted is refused, so a Cadra carrying it is harmless to today's Vendra. **The runtime signal is `openapi.json`**: `warranty_months` appears among `POST /api/v1/assets`'s request properties on a Cadra that accepts it — the same kind of check Vendra used to open `provenance_fields_enabled()`.
- 2026-09-22 · **A duration and a start date, and the expiry derived from them — a contradicting expiry is REFUSED, not overwritten.** The handoff's reasoning, kept: a derived date published without its derivation cannot be checked, argued with or corrected. So the block publishes `basis` (*"24 months from 2026-03-12"*) beside the date. Refusing rather than silently preferring one: two statements of the same fact that disagree are a question for whoever sent them. The same date re-sent is accepted, because a form that re-sends what it displayed contradicts nothing. Half a basis is refused; a bare expiry with no basis stays accepted, and says so. Calendar months via `relativedelta`, clamped to month end; the expiry day itself is covered.
- 2026-09-22 · **`warranty_expiry` keeps its name, type and published key; it is `_resolve_warranty` in `nrs.asset.create`/`write`, not a stored compute, that derives it.** A `compute=…, readonly=False` field would have silently let the basis win over an explicit date sent in the same write — exactly the quiet overwrite refused above.
- 2026-09-22 · **The warrantor is a NAME, not a partner link** — Cadra holds no vendor record for an asset arriving from Vendra, and the custody chain's rule is the precedent: the name as it stood when the record was made.
- 2026-09-22 · **The published block uses the WRITABLE names** (`warranty.warranty_months`, not `warranty.months`), so a client can send back what it read — UNCHANGED. Edit the duration or the start and the expiry that was read now contradicts them, so it is left out; that refusal is the rule above working, not a round-trip defect. Chosen the week handoff 33 §B2 named a GET→PATCH round trip refused on six renamed keys as a defect we had introduced; a new block should not be born with it. The three derived keys — `status`, `status_as_of`, `basis` — are the server's and are refused on write like any read-only key.
- 2026-09-22 · **A warranty change IS an event** (`nrs.asset.event`, type `warranty_changed`, one row per field that actually changed, the derived expiry included). A warranty is a contractual fact somebody could benefit from altering — lengthened after the laptop broke, shortened to justify a replacement — and the person holding the broken asset needs the record that stood on the day. **The cost is small and was checked:** an event is a CHANGE, so an asset CREATED with a warranty — every INT-03 receipt carrying one — writes none and is not pinned; only a later edit pins it. The five fields are NOT chatter-tracked, deliberately: the event is their record, and tracking would have put them into the register snapshot's roll-back contract (`test_every_tracked_field_is_rolled_back`), which warranty is not part of.
- 2026-09-22 · **Three states, not two, judged on a Lagos day.** `none` — no warranty recorded — is the honest third and almost every asset; folding it into `expired` would assert a lapsed contract nobody has seen. `warranty_status` is a NON-stored compute with a search method applying the same rules on `snapshot_today()`, so the filter and the published status cannot disagree and no job has to push it at midnight. `?warranty_expires_within_days=N` is today..today+N inclusive and never includes an expired warranty.
- 2026-09-22 · **A warranty is not the asset's health.** Nothing about a warranty moves `state`, `condition`, `is_verified`, a pill, a default filter or a working-stock count; the model test and the API test both assert it by writing an expired warranty and reading the rest back.
- 2026-09-22 · **Adding a start date and a duration to an asset that holds only a bare expiry REPLACES that date with the derived one**, without a refusal. It is the one path where a caller's earlier value is overwritten without being named in the write — accepted because the caller has just supplied the working, and the old date survives as the `from_value` of a `warranty_changed` event.
- 2026-09-22 · **The verification pass found a write that re-entered the chain.** The first draft resolved a multi-asset warranty write by calling `record.write(vals)` per asset, which starts again at the TOP of the override chain — so `myservice_workflow`'s SAP lifecycle trigger ran once for the set and once per asset, and a disposal written to two assets beside a warranty field queued two `asset.disposed` events for each. Now `write` hands each resolved record to `_write_recorded`, below the overrides. **Proved to fail first**: the new `test_a_disposal_beside_a_warranty_field_is_announced_ONCE_per_asset` against the old code — `1 failed of 1 tests`, `2 != 1`. No caller did this yet.
- 2026-09-22 · **`none` means no EXPIRY recorded, and the asset page no longer says "nothing has been entered" beside a warrantor or a certificate number.** The model accepts either without a date, and the first draft of the card hid exactly the two facts a person holding a broken asset needs. Found by the verification pass.

## 2026-09-22 — handoff 35 (nobody moves an asset alone)

- 2026-09-22 · **A move or a custody change needs a second person, and the rule is enforced in `nrs.asset.write` — not in the two routes the app calls.** The handoff asked for every writer to be enumerated before the rule was written, and the enumeration is the reason: **twelve** production writers of `location_id` and **thirteen** of `custodian_id`, against the one the previous guess would have named. A route gate would have covered `/move` and `/transfer-custody` and left nineteen writers alone, including a freely editable `custodian_id` on the Odoo asset form (`asset_views.xml:107` — `location_id` two lines above got `readonly force_save` in handoff 28 and custodian never did). The full table, with the argument for each row, is report 35 §A.
- 2026-09-22 · **Approval is about a PERSON DECIDING, which is what makes the exemptions principled rather than convenient.** `create` is never gated — a registration is not a move, and that is the INT-03 door Vendra delivers through. The bulk importer, the three demonstration seeds and the departure clearance declare `cadra_approval_exempt=<reason>` from a closed set of five; **an unrecognised value is no exemption at all**, so a typo cannot open a door a blank cannot. Five call sites, each a row in §A's table. **Two writers cannot be gated at all and are named rather than implied**: deleting a location or an employee empties the column through `ON DELETE SET NULL` with no ORM write. The guarantee is Python, as it is for the three append-only recorders.
- 2026-09-22 · **ONE model for both kinds of change — `nrs.asset.change.request` — and `nrs.asset.transfer` was deliberately NOT reused.** The case was made both ways before the code (report 35 §B). Against reuse, four measured facts: both location columns are `required=True` with a constraint forbidding equality, so it cannot express a move of an asset that is nowhere (**1,920 of `jaizbankplc`'s 4,164 — 46%**) or a custody change at all; `group_asset_manager` holds `1,1,1,1` on it and `asset_id` is `ondelete='cascade'`, and report 27 §13.2 ruled that *an approval recorded on a row an asset manager can delete is not an approval record*; and its `approved_by` is already in the INT-09 payload SAP receives, meaning something else. **Nothing was stranded by the decision**: measured 2026-09-22, all 267 transfer rows on all EIGHT local tenants are in `done` (`test19` 161, `jaizbankplc` 103, `gtb19` 2, `nrs19` 1; `cadra19`, `nrs`, `lasg`, `gtb` nil) and not one is in `draft` or `approved` — the machinery has never been used. **The transfer becomes the RESULT**: an approved location change creates one carrying the real approver, so `approved_by` — set on 1 of those 267 — stops being always-empty.
- 2026-09-22 · **The request is a state machine, not a fourth append-only record, and that is a distinction not a compromise.** `nrs.asset.append.only.mixin` refuses every write unconditionally, which is right for a completed fact and wrong for a record that legitimately moves `pending → approved | rejected | withdrawn`. What is taken from the mixin is the SHAPE: the transition methods set a context flag on themselves and `write` refuses without it, so the state machine is enforced by the model rather than by who holds which group. **`unlink` is refused always; a DECIDED request is frozen to everything including `sudo()`.** A mistaken request is WITHDRAWN, which is a state and leaves the record standing. `test_nothing_but_these_three_adopts_the_guard` still asserts exactly three adopters and still passes.
- 2026-09-22 · **`approver_id` (who is being asked) and `approved_by_id` (who clicked) are two columns and are never the same one**, per instruction, and the nomination confers nothing: any eligible person may decide, the requester may not, whoever was named. That split is what makes it safe to let a requester choose their own approver. Who decided is taken from the token exactly as `approve-disposal` takes its approver (handoff 23); a body naming it is refused 400 rather than ignored.
- 2026-09-22 · **Self-approval is refused in the MODEL.** `_assert_may_decide`, with no path to the state change round it — proved refused under `.with_user()`, under `sudo()` and as SUPERUSER. A hidden button is a door closed on one screen, which is what report 30 found when a disposed asset could be un-disposed through a route nobody had looked at.
- 2026-09-22 · **`cadra.approval.required` is read with raw SQL and never `get_param`, and absent means ON.** The handoff named `database_class` as the precedent and the substance is the ormcache, not the style: handoff 25 watched one worker answer `client` for a database whose row had been deleted while another answered `test`, and twenty-four writing tests passed. A worker serving `off` from a cache for a tenant whose row says `on` is a control that has stopped applying with no symptom at all. Absent, blank, misspelt and unrecognised all resolve to *required*. Proved with the value genuinely absent on `test19`, which has never had it set.
- 2026-09-22 · **The switch is READ-ONLY over the API, and it is the only settings block on that controller without a PATCH twin.** `assets:write`, `approvals:write` and `settings:write` are all granted by the single group `group_asset_manager`, so a PATCH would let exactly the people the rule constrains switch it off, act, and switch it back on. An administrator sets it in the database; the command is printed inside the refusal. `GET /settings/approvals` publishes `configured` beside `required` because *nobody has ever set this* and *somebody deliberately turned it on* are the same behaviour and very different facts.
- 2026-09-22 · **At most one PENDING request per asset, by PARTIAL unique index** (`models.Constraint` builds table constraints and cannot express one). Not tidiness: each request records the world it was raised in, so if two are pending and the first is approved the second's premise is broken by definition and it could only ever be refused. **The index alone was not enough** — the probe showed it raises a raw `UniqueViolation` that aborts the transaction, so the route checks first and answers 409 naming the blocking reference.
- 2026-09-22 · **A request records its PREMISE — what the asset was when it was raised — and cannot be approved once that stopped being true.** Read from the asset at creation and never accepted from the caller: a premise the requester can write is one they can choose, and catching the case where what they believed is no longer true is the entire use of the columns. `premise_holds` is **computed on every read and never stored**, because the thing that breaks a premise — somebody else moving the asset — touches no field on this record, and an approver reading a stale `true` is the silent failure the handoff forbade. One method answers for both the display and the refusal, so the queue and the approve can never disagree. A broken request is NOT auto-cancelled: it stays, shows why, and offers no button.
- 2026-09-22 · **A decision is taken once; there is no reversal route and no way back to `pending`** — the same ruling handoff 30 made for disposal, and for the same reason. Somebody who changes their mind raises a new request, which leaves both records standing. **Rejection requires a reason** (`/condemn`, `/mark-missing` and `/mark-found` all do); **withdrawal is the requester's alone and needs `assets:write`, not `approvals:write`** — otherwise a requester who cannot approve could not cancel their own request either, leaving a dead row in a queue for ever.
- 2026-09-22 · **The notification is Odoo's own `mail.activity`, and this is the first first-party model in the repository to schedule one.** `mail.activity.mixin` was already on seven models and on `nrs.asset`'s form and nothing had ever called `activity_schedule`. The approver gets an activity (*you must do something*); the requester gets a `message_post` to their partner when it is decided (*this happened*). **Neither is the record** — `asset_custody.py`'s docstring says why, and both calls are wrapped and LOGGED rather than swallowed so a failed doorbell cannot roll back the request it announces.
- 2026-09-22 · **BREAKING: `POST /assets/<id>/move` and `POST /assets/<id>/transfer-custody` no longer move the asset on a tenant that requires approval**, and gain a required `approver_id`. **The status stays 200 and the body carries it** — `pending_approval: true`, the `request`, and `asset` UNCHANGED read back in the same response, with `movement_id`/`transfer`/`custody_change` explicitly null. 202 would have been the better-looking answer and was rejected on a real cost: this API has no per-call success status, and adding one would have to be carried through the idempotency cache too or a replay would answer 202 once and 200 afterwards — and a byte-exact replay is the property handoff 18 built and handoff 31 protected. **Cheap to overturn** (one field in the cache) if the Architect prefers 202.
- 2026-09-22 · **`PATCH /assets/<id>` refuses a CHANGING `location_id`/`custodian_id` with a 400 naming the route to use, and still accepts an UNCHANGED one.** Refusing the mere presence of the key would have broken every save of the app's edit form, which re-sends every field — the trap `CLAUDE.md` names as *a screen that PATCHes back the object it GET-ed*. A 400 rather than a raised request because a PATCH carries a whole object, and turning part of it into a request while writing the rest would leave the caller with a half-applied save and no way to tell which half.

## 2026-09-22 — handoff 35, deployment ruling

- 2026-09-22 · `[ruled by the owner]` **Approvals go live ON for EVERY tenant on `cadra-dev`, including the two live client registers.** The switch is absent everywhere and absent means on, so `upgrade-19` (image `cadra-odoo-19:dev-b280bc4`, `scale_down=true`, all five tenants) makes a move or a custody change need a second person on `staging`, `nrs`, `gtb`, **`jaizbankplc`** and **`lasg`** from the moment it completes. The alternative — setting `cadra.approval.required = off` on the two live tenants first, so nothing changed for either client until they had been told — was put to the owner with that reasoning and **declined**. Recorded because it is a change to how a client OPERATES rather than a deployment detail: Jaiz Bank and Lagos State Ministry of Health staff cannot move an asset or reassign a custodian without a second person agreeing, and neither was warned. **Reversing it is one command per tenant** (`ir.config_parameter` → `cadra.approval.required = off`), and the refusal a blocked user sees prints that command.
- 2026-09-22 · **The upgrade is a schema change and was run with `scale_down=true`.** A new model and table (`nrs.asset.change.request`), two `ondelete` changes from `restrict` to `set null`, and a partial unique index created in `init()`. `upgrade-19`'s own help says scale-down-off is safe only for asset/data-only upgrades. Every tenant was upgraded before the image rolled, for the reason that workflow's header gives: one Odoo registry serves them all, so a tenant left behind raises `UndefinedTable` on `/move`, `/transfer-custody` and `/approvals` the moment the image moves.

## 2026-09-27 — handoff 36 (the control we cannot prove)

- 2026-09-27 · **Approvals are NOT off-by-default-by-accident on any tenant, and that was the question worth answering.** Measured across all eight local tenants: every one has 2–3 Odoo users in `group_asset_manager`, and every one has locations. `scripts/bootstrap.py`'s `REQUIRED_ADMIN_GROUPS` puts `admin` in that group on every bootstrap, and `_may_decide` accepts a system administrator explicitly anyway. **Proved by acting**: on `nrs19`, `Administrator` — a plain login holding no API credential — approved a request raised by the `vendra` machine credential. The narrower gap is real and is about MACHINES: four of the eight tenants hold no API credential that can approve, and `nrs19`'s existing one is `test_client`, whose secret has been unrecoverable since handoff 04. Closed on `nrs19` only, because a credential nobody uses is a credential nobody rotates.
- 2026-09-27 · **The approving identity deliberately holds no `assets:write`.** `nrs_approver` on `nrs19` carries `assets:read approvals:write`, so it is structurally incapable of being both halves rather than merely refused when it tries — proved: `POST /move` as that credential is `403 Missing required scope(s): assets:write`. A separate `res.users` from `vendra` (uid 12 against uid 11), which is the whole of the control. Not reused from `test_client` because that credential is flagged `is_test_credential`, so every row it writes is relabelled `source: test` — an approval on a demo register filed as a fixture looks like evidence and is noise.
- 2026-09-27 · **Both people are in the history, and `actor` was NOT redefined to do it.** An approved move recorded the approver and lost the requester — half of a two-person control missing from the artefact the control exists to produce (found by Vendra's report 43). Fixed ADDITIVELY with an `approval` block carrying `requested_by`, `approver_asked` and `approved_by` with their as-recorded names. `actor` stays the decider on all fourteen event types, because it is true — the approver's authority is what moved the asset — and because redefining a published key on one event is the defect `test_published_names_resolve.py` exists to catch. **Graded `append_only` in band and the grade is argued, not inherited**: a DECIDED `nrs.asset.change.request` refuses `write` and `unlink` for everyone including `sudo()`, which is word-for-word what that grade publishes. **Null, never absent**, on a change that took one person.
- 2026-09-27 · **`nrs19` was never short of locations, and the fix was a scope.** It holds 1,154 real ones (*Ikom MSTO*, *GBTO Calabar*, *FCT*). The `GET /api/v1/locations` reported as "an empty list" was a `403 Missing required scope(s): locations:read` — the `vendra` credential holds `assets:read assets:write` and nothing more. **`vendra` gained `locations:read`** (additive, read-only) rather than the tenant gaining seeded locations it did not need. A partner that must quote a `location_id` on every INT-03 receipt and cannot list locations is the real blocker; revoking it is one command if the Architect would rather Vendra saw no location tree.
- 2026-09-27 · `[for the Architect]` **Handoff 33 is not moot and should be re-queued REDUCED, after this handoff.** Nothing in it is done; nine of ten items are untouched. But three of its instructions now conflict with decisions 34 and 35 made deliberately: **§B2** would add write aliases on `location_id`/`custodian_id`, the only two fields `PATCH /assets/<id>` gates, and the second spelling would slip past the route's literal check and surface as a `UserError` instead of the designed 400 — and 34 already ruled the opposite convention in writing (publish under the WRITABLE name). **§D2**, widened naively, would fail on the gated-write refusal that deliberately prints an `odoo shell … set_param` command. **§A1**'s premise is stale — handoff 17 already gave that route a required `X-SAP-Webhook-Secret`, so it is unvalidated rather than unauthenticated, and the "authenticated sibling" it says to copy is `auth='user'`, which SAP middleware cannot post to at all. Full item-by-item table in report 36 §4.
- 2026-09-27 · **PRE-EXISTING and unfixed:** `myservice_asset_depreciation.TestPolicyInheritance` fails two tests (`'fully_depreciated' != 'disposed'`), proved pre-existing by stashing every change on this branch and re-running — `2 failed, 0 error(s) of 15 tests`. Both the test and the model were last touched in handoff 31. Out of scope for this handoff and named because no report has named it.

## 2026-09-29 — the landing page at cadra.ng

- 2026-09-29 · `[ruled by the owner]` **The landing page lives at `https://cadra.ng/`, on the existing cluster, with the design's copy unchanged.** The owner was shown, before deciding, the claims on the page that the code does not support (WhatsApp intake, SAML/OIDC, the Oracle/M365/Okta/Ariba/Workday connectors, SDKs, mTLS, *"every read and write is recorded"* — which handoff 23 established is not true — and a shared-SaaS edition that contradicts one-client-one-server) and the Federal Ministry of Works named in the mock-ups, and ruled to leave the copy as is.
- 2026-09-29 · **Its own Ingress and certificate, not two more hosts on `cadra`.** The tenants share one SAN certificate; a host on it whose HTTP-01 challenge fails fails the whole certificate, and with it renewal for the two live clients. A landing-page DNS mistake must not be able to reach them.
- 2026-09-29 · **No image of our own.** Four static files (~75 KB) in ConfigMaps under the stock unprivileged nginx. A `cadra-landing` image would add a build and a repository to a container registry whose plan caps the count, for a page with nothing to build.
- 2026-09-29 · **The demo form does not claim a request it did not deliver.** The design showed *"Request received"* on submit with nothing sent anywhere. The page ships with no destination configured (`CONTACT` in `landing/main.js`) and says so when submitted; it shows *received* only on a 2xx from a configured endpoint. The destination is the owner's to give.

## 2026-10-01 — handoff 38 (before the demo)

- 2026-10-01 · **Lookup precedence is tag number → asset code → serial number, each exact, and the order is for determinism rather than from evidence of a collision.** Measured on all eight local tenants before the code was written: 0 serials equal to any tag number, 0 equal to any asset code, 0 tag numbers equal to an asset code. A scanned tag can still never lose to a serial that reads the same. **The serial rule**: strip the query, compare case-insensitively and EXACTLY (`=ilike` with `%`/`_`/`\` escaped — Odoo 19 renders it as a bare `ILIKE` with the value passed through, so Postgres's default backslash escape applies). Internal whitespace and leading zeros are significant: `nrs19` has 574 serials with a space inside and ten beginning `0`. No stored serial on any tenant carries padding (0 of 11,704), so stripping the query is the whole rule.
- 2026-10-01 · **A serial on more than one asset is a miss that says how many, never a silent pick.** `nrs19` carries 1,291 serial values on more than one asset (3,455 rows) — model names typed where a serial should be, `PROBOOK` on 94 of them. Resolving one to the first row would put a green card on the wrong asset. 404 `not_found` with `details.ambiguous: true` and `details.matches`.
- 2026-10-01 · **The tag-number path is NOT widened: a pending or scrapped tag still does not resolve by its number** (handoff §A.4). An asset found by code or serial carries its active tag, or — when it has none — its `physical_tag_id`, so a label whose replacement is pending shows as *replacement pending* rather than *untagged*. **BEHAVIOUR CHANGE on `/barcode/lookup`:** `tag_id` is null (and the tag strings empty) for an asset with no tag at all; before, such an asset was a miss. Announced in `docs/api/CHANGELOG.md`.
- 2026-10-01 · **A tag replacement is two-person, on the tag model's OWN state machine, not on `nrs.asset.change.request`.** `[for the Architect]` The handoff said *use the pattern handoff 35/36 established rather than inventing a second one*; this reads "pattern" as the conventions (a nominated approver, the decider from the token, self-approval refused in the model, reasons recorded, decisions frozen) and applies them to the state machine the tag model has carried since the station was built — it is completed, not invented. Reusing the change request was mapped first: it is hard-wired to location and custody in more than fifteen places (`change_type`, premise columns, `_premise_check`, `action_approve`, `_approval_clears`, the activity text, the API's `CHANGE_TYPES`, the queue page), and its ONE-PENDING-PER-ASSET index would make a pending label block a move on the same asset — a change to the model that gates moves, the day before a demonstration. The cost, said: tag replacements do not appear in `/approvals`; they have their own queue (`GET /tags/pending-replacements`, the station's card). Who may decide is ONE rule — the tag model reads the change request's `APPROVER_GROUP`. If NRS wants one queue, the reuse path is the six-layer list in report 38 §B.
- 2026-10-01 · **The reason is recorded by the request, not left to a form field.** `action_request_replacement` used to write the state and the requester only; it now refuses without a reason (one of the five selection values) and without a named, eligible approver who is not the caller — and the Odoo form gained `approver_id` and `decision_reason` so the back-office buttons still work when the fields are typed first. **The legacy back-office station's one-click *Replace* now refuses** (`static/src/js/barcode_station.js` calls the method with no arguments); the SPA's station is the path a person uses, and the refusal names what is missing.
- 2026-10-01 · **Nine stamp fields are refused by `write` without `cadra_tag_replacement`, and a scrapped tag cannot leave `scrapped`.** `readonly=True` on the form is not a guard and the model is `1,1,1,1` to asset managers over XML-RPC — the same reasoning as `_CAPTURE_FIELDS`. `approver_id`, `replacement_reason` and `replacement_notes` are NOT guarded: they are what the requester types before pressing the button. A re-request after a rejection is a new slot and clears the old decision's stamps; the chatter keeps them.
- 2026-10-01 · **The old number never comes back, proved by doing it** — `_generate_tag` counts every tag on the number, scrapped included; two replacements in a row on one asset yield three distinct numbers, and `NRS-GEN-5565` on `test19` exists exactly once, scrapped.
- 2026-10-01 · **IR number format validation NOT built; the count was taken instead, and it changed the question.** NRS said *where it's supposed to be zero, sometimes we see O*. The field Cadra reads, `hr.employee.ir_number`, holds nothing on `nrs19` (0 of 11,386) — the real NRS extract's IR numbers are in a studio column `x_ir_number`, 11,385 of 11,386, ALL digits (5 long ×10,498, 8 long ×887), none containing a letter `O`, no duplicates. So an `IR` + digits rule — the shape one sentence of transcript suggests — would fail 100% of the real data, and a digits-only rule is one tenant's extract. The 193 IR numbers on the other seven tenants are all seeds or pytest fixtures. **Open for NRS:** what their IR number format is, and whether `x_ir_number` should be the column Cadra reads (`scripts/seed_nrs_assets.py` maps it onto `ir_number` on import, which this tenant's load evidently did not do).
- 2026-10-01 · **PRE-EXISTING and unfixed, named:** `pages/AssetRegisterDashboard.tsx` carries the same false comment the station had (*"a miss is a 200 with {found: false}"*) and a dead `!lookup.data.found` branch; `e2e/fixtures/api.ts` declares `twoLocations()` twice. Neither is in this handoff's shape.

## 2026-10-01 — handoff 39 (the tagger confirms)

- 2026-10-01 · **The screen never HANDS the expected tag number over — and "the screen" was wider than the dialog. One place shows it, by instruction, and that is the Architect's to rule on.** `POST /assets/<id>/tag-affixed` has carried the control since handoff 03: the number READ OFF the label is compared with the tag Cadra issued, and a mismatch is refused in a sentence naming both. Nothing in the app had ever called it; the only way it had been done was Postman. The station's *Confirm affixed* dialog holds no default, no placeholder, no suggestion and no copy button for the number, and the Playwright spec asserts it on the dialog's full markup. **The first screenshots showed the number anyway**: the scan box behind the overlay still read `NRS-ACS-716`, legibly, because when the lookup was BY TAG the scan box IS the number. So while the dialog is open the card's number line is replaced, the scan box is emptied (its query kept and restored on close) and the whole page is blurred (`blur-md select-none` — the text stays in the DOM, illegible and unselectable; the recent-tags table lists the number too). If the screen hands the number over, the check compares Cadra's number with Cadra's number and manufactures a record that says a human verified something no human verified; that is worse than no screen. **The exception, named:** after a WRONG scan the server's refusal — *Scanned tag X does not match the tag issued for this asset (Y)* — names the issued number, and the handoff requires that sentence verbatim, so a person who scans anything, reads Y out of the red panel and types it will pass. The refusal text is on the do-not-touch list and the handoff forbids paraphrasing it; the verification pass found the tension and it is reported in §F of report 39, not resolved here. `[for the Architect]` whether the server's sentence should stop naming the expected number (a change to published API text), or the dialog may redact it (a paraphrase), or this is accepted because a person who types a number they did not scan is lying rather than being misled.
- 2026-10-01 · **A repeat confirmation is told apart by reading the block before posting, not by a clock.** The server answers a second confirmation exactly as it answers a first — 200, the original `affixed_at`, nothing written, a chatter note (`_mark_affixed`, handoff 03) — and the handoff requires the screen to say *already confirmed on <date>*, never "done" and never an error. Comparing the response's `affixed_at` with the browser's clock would misread a fresh confirmation as a repeat on any client whose clock is ahead of the server. The dialog GETs the asset's `tagging` block, POSTs, and calls it a repeat when the block was already `affixed` with the same `affixed_at`. The POST is still sent, so the server's own record of the repeat is kept.
- 2026-10-01 · **The label's state is read from the asset's `tagging` block, not added to the lookup payload.** `/barcode/lookup` publishes the TAG's `state` (active / pending / scrapped) and not the LABEL's `label_state` (issued → printed → affixed), and the handoff shows the control only for an active tag whose label is neither `affixed` nor `scrapped`. The block already carries `label_id`, `label_state` and the four affixed fields; the station reads it for an active tag and compares `label_id` with the scan box's `tag_id`. Adding keys to the lookup route would have been additive and safe, and the day before a demonstration a second read costs less than a change to a published payload and its OpenAPI schema. Reversible.
- 2026-10-01 · **The serial field is offered — the route's second refusal is taken — and only when the record has a serial.** The route refuses a scanned serial when the record holds none to check against; a field the server will always refuse is a trap, so the dialog shows an explanation instead of the field for such a record (`hit.serial_number` from the lookup payload). With a serial on file the field is optional, and a mismatch is rendered in the server's words like any other refusal.
- 2026-10-01 · **The model's scrapped-label sentence is unreachable through the route, and this is named rather than changed.** `_mark_affixed` refuses a scrapped label by name, but the route resolves `rec.active_tag_id` first and a scrapped tag is never `active`. So through this screen a scrapped label in the tagger's hand is refused as *"Scanned tag OLD does not match the tag issued for this asset (NEW)"* — the new number having been issued on approval — and while the replacement is still pending as *"This asset has no active tag to confirm"*. Both are proved in the browser (spec 39, third test). Both are correct refusals for the situation; the handoff's do-not-touch list covers the route and the refusal text.
- 2026-10-01 · **The permission refusal is rendered, not proved in a browser.** A 403 lands in the same panel as every other refusal, in the server's sentence. Neither identity the suite can sign in as lacks `tagging:write` (`admin` holds `admin`, which satisfies it, `myservice_api/controllers/utils.py`; `GET /auth/whoami` for the `test19` service credential, read 2026-10-01, lists `tagging:write` among its 14 scopes), and there is no user-creation endpoint to make one that does. The HTTP 403 on this route is proved in the in-registry suite, `myservice_api/tests/test_auditor_access.py` (the Auditor is refused with `tagging:write` named); `tests/test_tagging.py` proves only that the OpenAPI document publishes the scope. The dialog also GETs the asset before it POSTs, so confirming needs `assets:read` as well — which the station's lookup already needs.
- 2026-10-01 · **"The station's counters follow" is invalidation, measured to change nothing visible.** None of the station's five tiles counts affixed labels (`total_assets`, `active_tags`, `pending_replacement`, `scrapped_tags`, `untagged`), and a confirmation changes none of them. The queries are invalidated anyway, with the pending list, the tag and the asset's history, so a later change to what a tile counts is not outlived by a stale screen.
- 2026-10-01 · **`formatIsoDateTime` appends `Z` to a zoneless `*_at`.** The API's datetimes are `isoformat()` of naive UTC values; parsed bare, a browser reads them as local time and shows the wrong hour. On `test19` the row says `17:30:41` and the card says `18:30` — Lagos.

## 2026-10-01 — handoff 40 (the camera in the dialog)

- 2026-10-01 · **A camera detection FILLS the field and does not submit; a handheld's Enter still does.** The two will read as an inconsistency and are not one. A trigger pull is a deliberate act — the person aimed at THIS label and squeezed — so report 39's form submits on the scanner's trailing Enter. A camera looks at the room continuously and reads whatever passes the lens: the neighbouring label, a box, the tag still on the desk. Filling the field lets the person see what was read before they commit, so a misread costs a glance instead of a false record — or a refusal that, by report 39's open tension, names the issued number. On detection the toggle flips back to Type and the cursor returns to the field; Confirm is one more tap or Enter. `[landed]` — `ConfirmAffixedDialog.tsx`, the reasoning in the file comment by instruction.
- 2026-10-01 · **The station's `BarcodeScanner` is reused as it is, and the toggle is a copy, not an import.** The scanner holds no module state and stops its own stream on detection and on unmount, so a second mount is safe without a change (the handoff's condition for stopping). The station's `ModeButton` lives in `BarcodeStation.tsx`, a page that imports the dialog; a component importing its own page back is a cycle for ten lines of markup, so the dialog carries its own `ModeToggle` in the same classes, a size smaller. One camera at a time, keyed by field (`cameraFor`), because two live streams and two detect loops for one pair of hands is a battery problem and a confusion. Default Type, as the station; not auto-selected on a small screen — being clever the night before a demonstration is how demonstrations break.
- 2026-10-01 · **The dialog is a column whose middle scrolls, because the preview pushed *Confirm* off a 720px viewport.** The spec's own geometry check found it on the first run. A sticky footer was tried first and floated over the form in the screenshot, with the serial field visible beneath it; the attempt is not kept and the reason is not claimed. So the content is `flex flex-col` capped at `calc(100vh - 2rem)`, header and footer `shrink-0`, the form `min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`. With the camera off nothing is tall enough to scroll and the layout is report 39's. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-01 · **The browser proof uses a real decode through Chromium's fake capture device, not a stubbed detector.** A stubbed `window.BarcodeDetector` would prove the dialog's wiring and nothing about the thing the handoff asks a tagger to rely on. Playwright's Chromium on macOS has the detector, `*.localhost` is a secure context, and the fake device plays an `.mjpeg` that is re-read each time a stream opens — so the spec writes the label Cadra prints (`code128Svg`, the printer's own encoder, imported from the app) and the platform reads it back. `docs/ENVIRONMENT-FACTS.md` carries the recipe and the race it has (the scanner stops itself before Playwright can see the Stop button when a label is in front of the lens). Not portable to a Linux runner; the browser suite does not run in CI.
- 2026-10-01 · **The station's own camera is not stopped when the dialog opens — observed, not changed.** If the station is in Camera mode with the stream running and *Confirm affixed* is clicked, the station's scanner keeps running behind the blurred page, and a detection there would run a lookup under the dialog. In practice a camera lookup switches the station back to Type on detection, so the stream is running only if somebody turned it on and then clicked Confirm on an earlier hit. The station's scan box is on the handoff's do-not-touch list. `[open]` — a one-line fix (`setMode("type")` when `affixOpen` becomes true) for a handoff that may touch the station.
- 2026-10-01 · **A detection already in flight when the camera is switched off still lands — observed by the verification pass, not changed.** `BarcodeScanner` checks its running flag before each `detect()` and not after it resolves, so a frame decoded during the one call in flight (about a quarter of a second) when the person toggles to Type or closes the dialog still calls `onDetected`: the field fills after the camera is gone, and if the dialog had closed the value sits in its state until the next open. It fills, never submits, and the value is what the lens saw, never the expected number. The station has the same shape. The component is on the handoff's do-not-touch list. `[open]` — a running-flag check after the `await` in `BarcodeScanner.tick`.

## 2026-10-02 — the mail relay on Odoo 19 (owner request, not a handoff)

- 2026-10-02 · `[landed]` **The HTTPS mail relay overrides `_connect__`, because that is what Odoo 19 calls; the `connect()` override it carried from 15 was dead for the whole life of the 19 stack.** `mail.mail.send()` opens one SMTP session per batch and hands it to `send_email()` for each message; on 15 the opener was `ir.mail_server.connect()`, and `myservice_email_relay` short-circuited it with a placeholder session so the relay's `send_email()` was reached. Odoo 19 renamed the opener `_connect__` (`odoo/addons/base/models/ir_mail_server.py:394`, called at `odoo/addons/mail/models/mail_mail.py:719`), and the August port (`8e45ec9`) changed only the manifest version — so on every 19 tenant the core opener ran, found no SMTP server, and marked every mail `exception` with *Unable to connect to SMTP Server* before the relay's own code could execute. Proved from the running server on `test19`: with the relay configured and a deliberately wrong secret, a `mail.mail` sent through `send()` now reaches `https://dev-api.akraa.io/api/v1/public/email/send` and comes back `exception` with the relay's own words on the row — `Email relay returned HTTP 401 / Invalid or missing secret` — which is the whole path, minus the secret. The in-registry test reads the source of `mail.mail.send` and asserts it still calls the name this module overrides, so the next rename fails a test instead of silently disconnecting mail again. **This is the fourth published-name-versus-thing-underneath defect in the record** (after `location_id`/`generic_location_id`, `status` on the SAP feed, and `_sql_constraints`): a method we override is a name the framework owns, and a framework upgrade is where names change.
- 2026-10-02 · `[landed]` **One POST per recipient, not the first To and a warning.** The relay accepts a single recipient per request; the 15-era module sent to the first `To` address and logged that it had dropped the rest, which is a mail silently not delivered. Every address across `To`, `Cc` and `Bcc` is now its own request, de-duplicated and in header order, and a failure after a success says *after N of M recipient(s) were delivered* on the row — a half-delivered message is a different fact from an undelivered one.
- 2026-10-02 · `[landed]` **The relay obeys `_disable_send()`, the same switch SMTP obeys.** Core sends nothing during a test run or registry init; the relay used to consult only its two config parameters, so an in-registry suite on a tenant with the relay configured would have mailed real people. The test proves a configured relay posts nothing under test.
- 2026-10-02 · `[landed]` **No staging tenant ever had the relay module, because the cluster's module list and `.env.example`'s disagreed.** The first invitation sent after PR #96 was merged and deployed failed with `SMTPDataError 553 Sender is not allowed to relay emails` from the server named *Cadra Default SMTP* — core's SMTP path, not the relay's. The traceback said why before any query did: `ir_mail_server.py:837 smtp.send_message` is core code the override never reaches when it is active. `k8s/dev/11-configmap-bootstrap-19.yaml` listed 16 modules and `.env.example` listed 16 with `myservice_email_relay` in place of `myservice_theme`; every staging tenant was bootstrapped from the first list. The module is now in the cluster list, and `set-email-relay.yml` (modelled on `set-turnstile.yml`) writes `cadra.email_relay_url` and `cadra.email_relay_secret` on a list of tenants from the GitHub variable and secret, prints the module's install state beside the readback — because a parameter nobody reads is inert — and says so when the module is missing. `_get_param` is `@ormcache`d per worker, so odoo-19 must roll afterwards; `set_param(key, False)` deletes a row where `""` stores an empty string. **The 553 itself is a separate fact:** Zoho refuses a From address that is not the authenticated account, so the SMTP fallback will go on failing for invitations whose sender is the inviting user, and the relay — which takes no From at all — is the path that does not have that problem.
- 2026-10-02 · `[ruled, owner]` **The relay is live on no tenant until the owner sets the secret.** `cadra.email_relay_url` and `cadra.email_relay_secret` are set on 0 of 8 local databases; the module is installed on 1 of 8 (`test19`); the dev relay refuses a request without a secret (`Field validation for 'Secret' failed on the 'required' tag`, HTTP 400) and refuses a wrong one (HTTP 401). The cluster path is `CADRA_EMAIL_RELAY_URL` / `CADRA_EMAIL_RELAY_SECRET` through `scripts/bootstrap.py`; locally it is two `set_param` calls per tenant. Note that `set_param(key, "")` on 19 STORES an empty string rather than deleting the row — the module strips and treats empty as unset, so the effect is the same, but a reader counting rows will see two.

## 2026-10-01 — handoff 41 (who has what)

- 2026-10-01 · **A human tagger holds Asset User + Tag-Issuing Unit; the pairing is documented, and no `ir.model.access` row is added for the Tag-Issuing Unit.** Driven, not reasoned: a person holding ONLY the Tag-Issuing Unit (`h41_tagger` on `test19`, scope `tagging:write`) is refused on every station read at the SCOPE gate before any access rule is consulted — `/barcode/kpis`, `/barcode/recent-tags`, `/barcode/untagged`, `/tags/pending-replacements` on load and `/barcode/lookup` on scan, every one `403 forbidden: Missing required scope(s): assets:read` (report 41 §A, the drive log verbatim). No access rule grants a scope, so a read rule on `nrs.asset.tag.label` for that group would leave every one of those refusals standing; the group's own comment says it grants `tagging:write` and nothing else, and a row would make that false. The option that LOST: a new `ir.model.access` row for the group — it answers the sentence the owner saw and not the station, and the sentence has a different cause (next entry). With Asset User beside it (`h41_tagger_paired`) the station loads, the scan resolves, and *Confirm affixed* opens. `[landed]` — the comment on the group in `asset_security.xml`, and `docs/GO-LIVE.md` §18.
- 2026-10-01 · **The four `/barcode/*` reads delegate under sudo, as every other route does; the scope is the gate.** The paired tagger still got one refusal on a station that otherwise worked: `/barcode/recent-tags` → `403 forbidden: You are not allowed to access 'Location' (generic.location) records…` — an ORM refusal naming an Odoo group in the published envelope. Four wrappers in `v1.py` (`lookup`, `kpis`, `recent-tags`, `print-url`) hand `request.env` — the token's user — to `myservice_barcode_station`'s delegates, where every other v1 route runs `sudo()` (handoff 09's ruling for the dashboards, `SCOPE_GROUPS` in `utils.py`). The sentence the owner hit, *You are not allowed to access 'Asset Barcode Tag' records*, is the same defect for a user holding a group that grants `assets:read` without the tag label's read rule (Auditor, Identity Manager); a tagging-only token cannot reach it. `request.update_env(su=True)` in one named helper before each delegation; `uid` is kept so stamps still name the person; the back-office station's own `auth='user'` JSON routes are untouched because there the access rules ARE the gate. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-01 · **On Odoo 19 a group reaches the user form through a `res.groups.privilege`, not `category_id`; two privileges, not one, and the Auditor gets a third.** `res.groups` has no `category_id` on 19 — the field the handoff names does not exist. The form widget renders one SELECTION per privilege, its groups as mutually exclusive options under a "No" placeholder, grouped by the privilege's category; a group with no privilege is shown only under *Extra Rights* and only in developer mode (`res_user_group_ids_field.js`: `odoo.debug ? this.getExtraGroupsArch() : ""`), which is exactly what the owner found. One privilege holding all three would make Asset User and Tag-Issuing Unit mutually exclusive on the form and the pairing above impossible to set, so *Asset Register* holds User and Manager (one choice, Manager implies User) and *Tag Issuing* holds the Tag-Issuing Unit alone. A privilege creates no implication and no rule; the group's isolation is unchanged. **Widened by one:** the Auditor in `myservice_api` had the same defect and is one of the five roles the handoff's own vocabulary names, so it gets *Audit* in the same category. The seven other first-party groups (`myservice_security` ×3, `myservice_staff_bus` ×2, `myservice_workflow`, `myservice_identity`) are the same defect and are NOT touched — `docs/GO-LIVE.md` §18. **Side effect, named:** `res.groups.full_name` now reads *Asset Register / Asset Manager*, so the access record's `group_name` snapshot and the access-changes report's `groups` list carry the privilege prefix on rows written from now on; rows written before read *Asset Manager*. A snapshot is a snapshot. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-01 · **`GET /api/v1/users` publishes `roles_direct` and `roles_effective` as two keys, and the screen LEADS with direct.** On 19 implication is computed at read time and materialises no rows (`access_change.py`, case 4): *the groups this account is a member of* (`group_ids`) and *the groups it holds* (`all_group_ids`) are different questions, and Asset Manager implies Asset User. The screen leads with direct because that is what somebody granted and what handoff 42 will act on — removing an implied role from a user is not an operation that exists; the implied roles are shown beside them, visibly different, and the scopes are derived from the EFFECTIVE set because that is what the token carries. The role vocabulary and the scopes per role are read from `GROUP_SCOPES` (`/api/v1/roles`), so nothing restates the table; the suite recomputes every person's scopes from `/roles` and fails on drift. An API credential's `scopes` are the credential's own, with `scopes_source: "credential"`, because its token is not minted from groups. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-01 · **The access-changes link lands on a read-only page that did not exist.** The handoff says *link to the existing report rather than re-query it*; the report existed as `GET /reports/access-changes` and nowhere in the app. A link to JSON is not a screen an administrator can use, so `/admin/access-changes` renders the existing route for a period and filters client-side by `user_id` — no new data, no new server filter, no second record. `[landed]`

## 2026-10-02 — handoff 42 (the grant, and the refusal)

- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect: no `base.*` group is grantable over the API — only Asset User, Asset Manager, Tag-Issuing Unit and Auditor — and nobody adds a role to their own account.** A caller may remove their own roles. The reasoning is the handoff's: `admin` scope lets a machine run the register and must not reach the authority to execute arbitrary server actions; and granting yourself access is the same shape as approving your own move. Both landed in the model, not on the route. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The guard tells our API from the system by the one thing only our API sets: an authenticated request carries `request.jwt_claims`.** Every `@cadra_route` passes `require_jwt`, which sets it; nothing else does. The back office, XML-RPC, `odoo shell` (bootstrap), migrations, `post_init_hook`s, security XML on install and upgrade, `auth_signup`'s template copy, cron — none has it, so none is guarded and nothing has to be switched off to install the product. **No context key, deliberately**: a key is a way round that an RPC caller could set, the reason `_assert_disposed_is_terminal` has none. The option that lost: `install_mode` / `env.su` / `uid == 1` as the system marker — `_provision_client_user` runs from the back-office form as a logged-in user with sudo, the 19.0.3.2.0 migration runs as uid 1 with no `install_mode`, and a UI-started upgrade runs under a bound request; every one of those markers both over- and under-matches. The JWT matches exactly the population the rulings are about. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **Diff-then-raise, after `super()`, like the recorder.** `vals` cannot be parsed honestly for membership (`access_change.py` lists the four ways), so the guard snapshots before and after and raises `AccessError` on the diff. An exception rolls the transaction back, recorder rows included, so nothing is written on a refusal; the route maps it to `403 forbidden` carrying the model's sentence verbatim. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **An account created over the API may hold what Odoo gives every new internal user, plus the four.** `res.users.create` without a `group_ids` key lands the user in `base.group_user` and whatever `base.default_user_group` implies; the guard allows exactly `_default_groups()` ∪ the four on create, so handoff 43 can create a person without the guard being loosened and without a person being created as a portal user to dodge it. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **Over the API a group cannot be created, deleted or re-meant, and an account cannot be deleted.** `implied_ids` moves what a group means for every member at once and the access record stores it as its own event; deleting a group or a user cascades membership away. None of those is an operation this product offers a caller, so they are refused outright rather than left to whichever route first reaches them. Deactivation is handoff 43's and is not membership. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The preview is a real write inside a savepoint, rolled back.** `dry_run` performs the change and reads the row the way `GET /users/<id>` would, then raises a private exception to undo the savepoint. So the effective roles shown before applying are what Odoo computes, implication included, and a change that would be refused is refused on preview — the screen shows the sentence before anybody clicks Apply. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The route validates the vocabulary; the model decides what is grantable.** `POST /users/<id>/roles` rejects a key that is not a role at all (400, `allowed_values`) and forwards every real key to the write, so `system` reaches the guard and comes back as the model's 403. That is the point: the refusal the handoff wants driven is the model's, not a courtesy copy on the route. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The seven remaining first-party groups are on the user form.** Security (Officer / Manager / Administrator, one choice, each implying the last), Staff Bus (User / Manager), WorkSpace Integration (its own category — the module does not depend on the register) and Identity (in the register's category — the module does). 11 of 11 first-party groups now carry a privilege. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The sign-up door on the cluster is shut, measured before handoff 43 asks.** Anonymous `GET /web/signup` answers 404 on `staging.app.cadra.ng`, `jaizbankplc.cadra.ng`, `lasg.cadra.ng` and `gtb.cadra.ng`; `/web/login` and `/web/reset_password` answer 200. WHY it is shut, and whether `auth_signup.invitation_scope` reads `b2c` there as it does locally, is handoff 43's §0 and is not claimed here. `[verified]`

## 2026-10-02 — handoff 43 (the person, and the door)

- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect: this product is not a service people sign themselves up to. `b2b` — invitation only — set deliberately, by us, in a way a module upgrade cannot quietly undo.** Landed as a CONSTANT: `res.users._get_signup_invitation_scope` returns `b2b` (`myservice_api/models/invitation.py`), and a migration sets the two rows that the Settings screen reads to `b2b` so the screen stops saying "Free sign up" about a shut door. The option that lost: setting the parameter. It was `b2c` on 8 of 8 local tenants and had never been read, because the `website` module overrides the same method with its own per-site field (`b2b` by default) — which is also why report 42 found the door shut; and one click in *Settings › Customer Account* would have opened it. A row is a thing a screen can flip; a constant is not. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The letters are the tenant's, through ONE override of the method every path ends in.** `res.users._action_reset_password` is what the user form's button, `res.users.create` (which invites by itself), `/web/reset_password` and `POST /api/v1/users` all call; ours renders `data/mail_templates.xml` with the brand `cadra_brand_<client>` put in `cadra.brand_*` (the same parameters `/api/v1/brand` serves the app from), the organisation's name from `res.company`, and no Odoo anywhere. Options that lost: writing into `auth_signup.set_password_email` (a `noupdate` core record — whether a later upgrade keeps or reverts the edit depends on who loads it) covers only the invitation; inheriting the `reset_password_email` QWeb view covers only the reset. `tests/test_user_lifecycle.py` reads core's source and fails the day `create` or `action_reset_password` stop calling the name we override — the relay's `_connect__` lesson, applied before it costs anything. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The link points at the host the request came in on — when the caller is authenticated.** An anonymous `/web/reset_password` keeps core's base: under `proxy_mode` the request host is `X-Forwarded-Host`, and a proxy that does not overwrite it would let a stranger choose the host a victim's reset link points at (verification pass, report 43 §F; the dev proxy now sets the header itself). Core builds it on `partner.get_base_url()` → `web.base.url`, which core rewrites from WHATEVER host the last system administrator signed in on, and which on this machine lacked its port on 8 of 8 tenants because the dev proxy passed `$host`. `res.partner._get_signup_url_for_action` asks core for the relative URL and joins it to the bound request's `url_root`; with no request bound (a cron, a shell) it does exactly what core did. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **A letter that cannot be sent takes the account with it.** The create and the send share one savepoint; a `MailDeliveryException` answers `500 server_misconfigured` naming the cause and nothing is written. `send_invitation: false` is the explicit way to create a person silently and invite later. The option that lost: create, then report that the letter failed — a person who exists and was never told is the silent degrade rule 3 forbids, and core's own `create` does exactly that (it swallows the delivery failure). A missing sender address is NOT refused: the From falls back as core's own reset path does — the company's address, then the sender's, then the recipient's own — because the first draft refused it with a `UserError`, which would have broken every back-office user create on a tenant whose company has no address (`nrs19`), where core had always created the user and swallowed the delivery failure (verification pass, report 43 §F). `[landed]`
