- 2026-10-02 · **Deactivation is not membership, and the guard does not judge it.** The three refusals it needs are in the route, in words: not your own account (core refuses this too, but against `env.uid`, which under `sudo()` is still the token's user, so core's sentence would have been right — ours is clearer), not the user behind an API credential (an integration breaking silently is worse than a refusal naming the credential), not the last active system administrator WHO IS A PERSON — counting the credentials' users, which bootstrap also puts in the group, would have let the last human be deactivated on `test19` and `nrs19` (verification pass). And `require_jwt` now reads `active` on every request: a deactivated person's live token is refused on its next call, not at `exp`. Nothing is deleted, on purpose and by instruction. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The invitation's state is derived from two stamps and the partner's live token, not recorded as a row.** `cadra_invitation_sent_at` / `_sent_by_id` / `_sends` are stored on `res.users` and written under every path; `state` is computed (`never` / `outstanding` / `expired` / `redeemed` / `cancelled`), a sign-in since the send tested FIRST because the token carries `login_date` and one sign-in kills it whatever `signup_type` still says. The token itself is stateless (`hash_sign`, expiry baked in), so `expires_at` is arithmetic. The option that lost: reading `mail.message` for the letter — `auto_delete` and the relay make that unreliable. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **A deactivated nominee is refused at nomination; what to do about a request already stranded on one is asked, not built.** `_resolve_approver` now refuses an archived `approver_id` — a line, as the handoff allowed: the picker hid them and the gate accepted them, the two-things-that-should-agree shape. The queue row still publishes `approver: {id, name}` with no sign the person cannot sign in, and a pending request cannot be re-nominated (the model freezes everything but a transition); both measured in report 43 §2 and left for the Architect. `[landed]` / `[open]`
- 2026-10-02 · **Odoo's *unregistered users* reminder cron is switched off**, on upgrade and on install. Five days after an unredeemed invitation it mailed the INVITER Odoo's own letter — the third Odoo-branded email, found by the verification pass after §1b asked about the first two. Switched off rather than rebranded: the inviter can see the same fact on the Users page (`invitation.state`). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The logo in a letter is a URL, not a `data:` URI.** `GET /api/v1/brand/logo` serves the decoded `cadra.brand_logo`; a tenant with none gets a wordmark in the brand colour, never a broken image. Gmail and Outlook strip inline-data images. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The sign-up door was shut on every tenant before this handoff, by accident.** Anonymous `/web/signup` → 404 on 4 of 4 cluster hosts and 8 of 8 local tenants; cause the `website` row's `b2b` default, created the same day as each database (so no self-made account can exist); no partner holds an outstanding sign-up token on any of the 8 (0 of 1,221 / 11,397 / 73 / 146 / 1,219 / 72 / 72 / 80 partners on `cadra19` / `nrs19` / `gtb19` / `test19` / `nrs` / `lasg` / `gtb` / `jaizbankplc`; `test19`'s one is this handoff's own fixture) and 0 of 6 / 11 / 12 / 52 / 5 / 12 / 12 / 19 users are share users beyond the two structural ones. And on Odoo 19 the sign-up path copies the PORTAL template (`base.template_portal_user_id`), not `base.default_user`, which no longer exists — a self-made account would have been a share user holding `base.group_portal`, invisible to `/users` and holding no scope. `[verified]`
- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the owner: the cluster's `nrs` database is a leftover, not a tenant.** It has no Cadra module, no host and one user; `upgrade-19` had been reporting *upgrade succeeded: nrs* as a no-op on every run because the default `target_dbs` named it. Out of the default list; the database stays until the owner drops it with `drop-db`. `[landed]`

## 2026-10-02 — handoff 44 (the credential that is already root)

- 2026-10-02 · **What an API credential is entitled to, written down for the first time.** A credential's power is its SCOPES, carried in the token and checked by `require_jwt` on every route; that is the whole of the gate. The `res.users` underneath it exists so the record can say WHO acted (*Tag-Issuing Unit*, not *Administrator*) and so the few ORM paths that need an internal user find one. It holds exactly `utils.groups_for_scopes(scopes)` — `base.group_user` plus the register groups its scopes name in `SCOPE_GROUPS` — and nothing else, ever: **no `base.*` group beyond `base.group_user`, and never `base.group_system`, whatever the scopes, `admin` included.** `admin` is a wildcard over the API, not a role in Odoo. A system administrator can execute arbitrary server actions, so a credential holding the group makes every rule we put in the model advisory for it — handoff 42's guard among them. **The consequence to know:** the MODEL's own checks ask for groups, not scopes — deciding a change request or a tag replacement asks for Asset Manager (`_may_decide`) — so a credential holding `admin` alone passes the route's scope gate and is refused by the model. That is right (a wildcard over the API is not a person who may approve), and `approvals:write` is the scope that says so; the owner script names any such credential before stripping it. Any group added to a client user by hand is erased by the next scope edit (`_provision_client_user`, `(6, 0, …)`), so the scope table is also the only DURABLE source of a client user's groups; a credential that genuinely needs more needs a scope and a `SCOPE_GROUPS` row, reviewed in a commit. Asked for by handoff 44 §2; the content is the Builder's, on the drive below, and is `[landed]` in `bootstrap.py` and `SCOPE_GROUPS` — for the Architect to rule on as written or correct.
- 2026-10-02 · **Nothing provisions a credential as a system administrator any more — and there were TWO grant sites, not one.** `scripts/bootstrap.py::ensure_test_client` granted it on every run, and the `19.0.3.2.0` migration that gave each credential its own user (2026-09-07) granted it to any client holding `admin` — which is why the three holders are exactly the three `admin`-scoped credentials. Report 42 named the first; the second was found by searching every first-party writer before building on the claim, as `CLAUDE.md` requires. Both removed; `tests/test_source_guards.py::test_no_provisioning_path_grants_a_credential_system_administrator` fails on either shape, proved to fail first on the old code naming both lines. The migration matters beyond history: a 15-era client database cut over to 19 runs it again. Both were granted on the stated reason that the dashboard routes do not `sudo()`; they have since handoff 09. Stripped on `test19` and driven: the 37 calls of the targeted drive as that credential (`44-evidence/drive-test19-*.txt`) answer the same status with and without the group, and the HTTP suite as the stripped credential is in report 44 §A, as is the employee xlsx import, whose background worker runs as the superuser. Bootstrap does NOT take the group off a credential that already holds it — it is the production deploy path, and that would change every live tenant on the next deploy without anybody deciding to. `scripts/strip_credential_system_group.py` is that decision: dry run by default, measured in a savepoint, touches only credential users and only their direct membership, recorded in the access record. Locally 3 of 12 credentials hold it (the `admin`-scoped `test_client` on `cadra19`, `nrs19`, `test19`); `test19`'s is stripped by this handoff, the other two and the cluster are the owner's to run. Vendra's and the Tag-Issuing Unit's credentials never held it on any tenant measured. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect: one name per role. The alias moves to the model.** `models/role_names.py` holds the one table (`base.group_system` → *System Administrator*); `/users`, `/roles`, the guard's refusals, the access-change record's `group_name` snapshot and the access report's roster all read it, and every other group keeps the name each place already used. The record changes for rows written from 2026-10-02: a grant or removal of the group read *Role / Administrator* before and *System Administrator* after — a snapshot is a snapshot, and the old rows are not rewritten (they are append-only). `tests/test_source_guards.py::test_a_role_alias_is_chosen_in_one_place` fails on a second alias table anywhere in first-party code, proved to fail first. `[landed]`

## 2026-10-02 — handoff 45 (the first five minutes)

- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect: Odoo is invisible to everybody who is not a developer.** The letter's link opens the APP's page (`/app/accept-invite?token=…`, `/app/reset-password?token=…`), not Odoo's `/web/signup`; the back office redirects every non-administrator to the app. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The token is core's, unchanged; only the page that takes it moved.** `res.partner._get_signup_url_for_action` (already ours since 43, for the host) now also rewrites core's `/web/signup?db=…&token=…&redirect=…` to `/app/accept-invite?token=…` (and `reset_password` to `/app/reset-password`), carrying ONLY the token. Three anonymous routes in `controllers/v1_signup.py` do what the Odoo form did — `password-token/inspect`, `password-token/redeem`, `password-reset` — bare `http.route` because `cadra_route` always requires a bearer token, like `/auth/login`. Redemption is core's own `res.users.signup(values, token)` followed by `authenticate`, which is what stamps `login_date` so the invitation reads `redeemed`, and the response is `/auth/login`'s own `data` block so the app stores it identically. The option that lost: a token of our own — a second secret, a second expiry rule, and a second answer to "is this link still good" beside the one `_cadra_invitation()` already publishes. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **Letters already sent keep working — they are redirected, not re-sent.** Every letter before this change carries `/web/signup?…token=…`; `controllers/backoffice.py` answers a GET there (and on `/web/reset_password?token=…`) with a 303 to the app's page and the SAME token. Nobody invited yesterday is stranded and nobody gets a second letter. The token-less `/web/reset_password` form goes to the app's *Forgot password?*. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **A link's five states, told apart.** 43's vocabulary — `outstanding`, `expired`, `redeemed`, `cancelled` — plus `invalid` for a token that was never ours. Core's `verify_hash_signed` returns `None` for an expired token and a forged one alike; the token carries its expiry in clear and its HMAC is checked separately (`v1_signup._read_token`), so a person who simply waited too long is told the link EXPIRED rather than that it is not valid. A newer letter (a reset after an invitation) and a deactivation both read `cancelled`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **Password rules: the ones Odoo enforces, which on these tenants is almost none — measured, and the minimum is the Architect's to set.** On Odoo 19 `signup()` hashes whatever it is given, the empty string included; `auth_password_policy` (which adds a minimum length) is installed on 0 of 9 local databases. So the routes refuse exactly: an empty or all-space password, a confirmation that does not match, and — if the policy module is ever installed — anything below its `auth_password_policy.minlength`; `/inspect` publishes the rules (`password_rules`) so the page shows them before anybody types, and the page's own check is the server's, worded the same. A Cadra minimum would be a rule we invented; it is not set. `[landed]` / `[open]`, Architect (`docs/GO-LIVE.md` §22.2).
- 2026-10-02 · ***Forgot password?* answers the same sentence whether or not an account matched.** Core's own `reset_password` says *"No account found for this login"* — a way to learn who has an account. Ours sends the letter to exactly one active internal account matched by login, then by email (core's order), and otherwise sends nothing and says the same thing; a delivery failure is logged for the operator and not told to an anonymous caller. Turnstile applies as on sign-in (the hook is shared, `src/lib/useTurnstile.ts`). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The back office: the interface is shut, the plumbing is not — and the split is clean because the app holds no Odoo session at all.** Enumerated by driving the app as a non-administrator in a browser, recording every request it makes (report 45 §B, the Playwright spec's own list): its only non-API request to Odoo is `/report/barcode` (the QR on a printed label, `auth='public'`); labels are composed in the browser and never go through `/report/pdf`. `Home.web_client` (`/web`, `/odoo`, `/odoo/<path>`, `/scoped_app/<path>` — one handler on 19) sends an anonymous visitor to `/app/` and signs a non-administrator out of Odoo and sends them there; `/web/login` stays (system administrators' door, and the k8s probes' target) but a non-administrator who signs in on it is sent to the app; GET and POST of `/web/signup` and `/web/reset_password` go to the app's pages. **And the doors a person does not walk through are shut one level down:** the verification pass held an Odoo session as a non-administrator through JSON-RPC `/web/session/authenticate` and the two-factor step, and reached `/web/dataset/call_kw`, `/web/tests` and `/my` with it — so `ir.http._authenticate` (`models/backoffice_session.py`) ends a non-administrator's session on every `auth='user'` route before it runs, an `http` route answering a redirect to the app and a JSON-RPC route core's *"Session expired"*. Not `ir.http._dispatch`: `auth='none'` (every `/api/v1` route) and `auth='public'` routes (`/report/barcode`, assets, `/web/login`) never see it. Consequence, accepted: the back-office barcode station is now an administrator's tool; its two in-registry tests run as one. The options that lost: closing each door in its controller (the shape that missed three); a proxy rule (`/web` is shared with assets, images and the probes); `_login_redirect` alone (an explicit `redirect` wins over it). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect (G.1a): the approval queue publishes whether the person asked can sign in.** `approver` on the row is `{id, name, active}`; the screen says *"— X cannot sign in"* on a pending request. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect (G.1b): a stranded request is fixed forward by re-nomination, recorded; deactivation is never blocked.** `nrs.asset.change.request.action_renominate(new_approver, reason)` is the ONE new path past the model's `write` refusal, through `_transition` — `write` still refuses `approver_id` from anybody, `sudo()` included. Who may: the requester, or an Odoo administrator (`_is_admin()`, the same rule as deciding), while pending; the current approver and other approvers may not, because they can already decide it. A reason is required. The act is a row of `nrs.asset.change.request.renomination` — append-only through `nrs.asset.append.only.mixin`, the fourth record to inherit it rather than copy it (`CLAUDE.md`'s rule) — carrying from, to, by, when and why; the inbox activity moves to the new approver. `POST /api/v1/approvals/<id>/renominate`; the row publishes `renominations` and `can_renominate`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **`[ruled]` by the Architect: `sent_by` keeps naming the credential.** *"Sent by Smoke Test (API client)"* reads oddly because it is true. Nothing changed. `[ruled]`
- 2026-10-02 · **A deactivation and a reactivation are recorded, under every writer of `active`.** `myservice.api.access.change` gains `deactivated` / `reactivated` (source `user_active`), written by the recorder's `res.users.write` from a before/after diff exactly as group changes are — so the API routes, the back office's Archive, core's portal self-deletion and a shell are all on the record, a no-op write is not, and a user created inactive is not a "deactivation". The access-changes report publishes them as events with their own counter (`account_state_changes_in_period`) and their own coverage start, because that half of the record began later than the group half. Cancelling an invitation is not a separate act in this product — it happens inside the same `write` — so no event was invented for it. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-02 · **The relay's plain-text part is opt-in by parameter, not sent blind.** The relay service is not in this repository and nobody here knows whether it accepts a plain-text field, or under what name; a strict validator rejecting an unknown key would stop EVERY letter on the cluster. So `cadra.email_relay_text_field` names the field (absent = today's five keys, byte for byte); set it once the relay's operator confirms the name. `[landed]` / `[open]`, owner.
- 2026-10-02 · **The sender address is set where the brand is set — and on the cluster it changes nothing until the relay accepts a From.** `GET/PATCH /settings/branding` gains `sender_email`, the main company's `email`, validated before any write. Measured on the way: a tenant with no company address is NOT refused (report 43 said it was) — the From falls back to the sender, then to the RECIPIENT's own address; and the cluster's relay ignores our From entirely, sending as its own account. `[landed]`

## 2026-10-03 — handoff 46 (everyone sees their own job)

- 2026-10-03 · **Demo personas are marked by the repository's own derivation, not by a third convention.** Handoff 46 asked for one account per role with realistic names on every tenant, client tenants included, and for the marking to be "in the data, not only in the name", reusing `is_test_credential` / `is_test_fixture`. The personas are created BY an inert credential — `cadra_demo_personas`: `is_test_credential=True`, archived at birth, no secret, no scopes, so `/auth/token` cannot find it (401, measured) — so `cadra.test.fixture.mixin` stamps each persona's partner exactly as it stamps anything else a test credential creates. Nothing sets the flag and nothing can. Two further marks, each independently checkable: logins on `demo.example` (RFC 2606 — can never be delegated, so never anybody's address), and an external id `__cadra_demo__.persona_<key>` (`ir.model.data`; a double-underscore name is not a valid module, so no upgrade adopts or deletes it). The remover acts only where all three agree. The options that lost: a new `is_demo` boolean (the third convention the handoff ruled out); a name prefix (a convention, and the owner ruled realistic names); stamping through a context flag (the mixin's whole guarantee is that it has no settable path). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **An account that is itself a fixture is a test ACTOR.** `myservice.api.client._is_test_credential_user(uid)` — the one derivation behind `is_test_fixture` on 15 models and `source='test'` on the four append-only records — now also answers yes for a user whose partner is labelled. Without it the persona is marked and what the persona DOES on a client's register is not: measured on `test19`, the Asset Manager persona's move request (2111) is `is_test_fixture=true` and the movement it caused reads `source='test'`. It also labels what the suites' invited officers do, which was a gap since handoff 43. Safe under the ormcache: the partner's flag is stamped at creation and `write()` refuses to move it. Nothing a real credential or a real person creates changes; proved both ways in `myservice_api/tests/test_fixture_label_is_derived.py`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Persona names are checked against the tenant's staff, and the handoff's own example failed.** *Aisha Bello* is four real employees on `nrs19`, one on `jaizbankplc`, `gtb`, `lasg` and `gtb19`; *Ibrahim Musa* is seven on `nrs19`. A realistic Nigerian name on an 11,386-person Nigerian staff list collides. The six chosen (Halima Danjuma, Chukwudi Anyanwu, Nkechi Obiora, Babatunde Olaniyan, Zainab Garba, Oluwaseun Ajayi) match no employee and no contact on any of nine local databases, and the seed refuses to create a persona whose name matches either, case-insensitively as a substring, on the tenant it runs on. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **A persona is never an employee.** Odoo 19 creates an `hr.employee` for a new user only when asked (`create_employee` defaults to False; `addons/hr/models/res_users.py:100`), and the seed does not ask. So a persona has no IR number, cannot be a custodian and is on no staff list: 0 employees linked to or named like a persona, on every tenant measured (`46-evidence`). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Removal deletes where it can and archives where a record names the persona.** A move request holds requester, approver and decider with `ondelete='restrict'` (handoff 35), and that request is a record of what was asked and decided, so it is not the remover's to delete. Such a persona is archived (cannot sign in, still named on the record) and the output says which and why. The decision is a trial delete in a savepoint, in the dry run too, so the dry run is the database's answer. The seeding credential goes only when no persona remains. The remover accepts `production`, because removal is what a live tenant needs at go-live; the seed refuses it, and refuses an unclassified database like every writing script. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **A sidebar item declares the scope the SERVER requires for what the page is for; hidden ⇔ refused.** `lib/navigation.ts` holds the table (moved out of `Sidebar.tsx` so the landing rule reads the same scopes), `scope` is required on every item, all-of. Read pages declare their read scope; pages whose purpose is a change declare the write scope — the three Settings screens declare `settings:write` because their reads are served under `assets:read` for other screens, so the thing withheld is the change. The rule was not "what the role's job is": an item hidden from a person the server would still answer is courtesy pretending to be a permission, so where a scope admits a page that is not the role's job, that is recorded as a finding about the role model (handoff 46 report §E) and not repaired by inventing a scope. `tests/test_nav_scopes.py` fails on an item without a scope or with one the server does not know; `e2e/tests/46-…` signs in as each persona and asserts, item by item, drawn ⇒ answered and hidden ⇒ 403. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Access changes moved to an Audit group and opened to `identity:read`.** The server has always served `/reports/access-changes` under `identity:read`, which the Auditor holds; the app showed it to administrators only, so the person whose job it is could not find it. It is now drawn for whoever the server answers — which includes every Asset User, because Asset User also holds `identity:read` (finding E.2). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Landing: the asset register dashboard, or the best page the person can open.** `landingPath` tries `/assets`, `/assets/list`, `/locations`, `/hr/employees`, `/dashboards/fleet` against the nav table's own scopes and falls back to `/no-access`, a page that says in words that the account opens nothing and what it holds. Measured by signing in: five personas land on `/assets`; the Tag-Issuing Unit alone lands on `/no-access`. The tagger lands on `/assets` per the owner's rule, though the barcode station is their job — a one-line change if the Architect prefers it. `[landed]` / `[open]`, Architect.
- 2026-10-03 · **The verification pass's findings, and what was done with each.** (D1) the spec's in-page check waited on a button inside the gated bar and swallowed the timeout, so a blank page passed for everybody without `assets:write` — it now waits on the workflow strip every reader is drawn, with no catch. (R2) the remover deleted any claimed `res.users` row other than a persona on the strength of the external id alone — it now takes the seeding user only if it IS the seeding credential's own user with the expected login, and refuses an unrecognised `__cadra_demo__` name. (R4) deleting a persona blanked the actor on any event, movement or custody row it was named on (`ondelete='set null'`) — such a persona is now archived, like one a move request pins; measured on `test19`, where the administrator persona approved a move. (R5) the landing rule ran only at sign-in — `/` now sends a stored session that cannot open the command centre to its landing page (a hidden URL typed elsewhere still renders and is refused; only the front door redirects). (N8) the seeding credential's own user was left active — archived at creation, and archived once on the six tenants seeded before the fix. (N6) the name check was one contiguous substring — every word of the name, in any order. The remaining two are recorded, not changed: **(R1) an account a persona creates is labelled a fixture for good**, because the persona is a test actor — a real colleague created from a persona's session would carry it; the listing now names such accounts, the remover never touches them; do not onboard real people from a persona's session. **(R3) the access record keeps the personas' role grants for good** — 14 rows per seeded tenant, the invented System Administrator among them; it is append-only by design (handoff 24), the `user_login` snapshot on `@demo.example` says what they were, and removal cannot and should not take them off. `[landed]` / `[open]`, Architect for R1 and R3.
- 2026-10-03 · **The next tag number was found by walking every number already issued.** `_generate_tag` took the highest number under a prefix by a TEXT sort, under which `NRS-GEN-999` outranks `NRS-GEN-8305`; past 999 labels of one prefix every new asset started at 1000 and issued one COUNT per taken number — 410 queries for one asset with 401 labels past 999 (`myservice_asset_register/tests/test_tag_number.py`, failing first on the old code), ~7,300 queries and ~11 s per `POST /assets` on `test19`. Real tenants were paying it too: one `nrs19` prefix and eight `jaizbankplc` prefixes are past 999. Now the numeric maximum by SQL, plus the existing free-number check. The numbers issued are the same where they are contiguous; a number above 999 that was never used is no longer back-filled — no number is ever issued twice either way. Found because the HTTP suite was running four times slower than handoff 45's. Not asked for by the handoff; fixed because it was impeding the handoff's own verification and was costing every client tenant on every registration. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **A local `.env.local` compiles a token into this machine's bundle.** `VITE_API_TOKEN` (from July, gitignored) is the dev fallback in `lib/auth.ts`, so a fresh browser on the local `/app/` counts as signed in with no scopes until its first request is refused 401 and the app signs out. It is why the first version of the front-door redirect sent every visitor to `/no-access` before they had typed a password; the redirect now acts only on a session the app stored. Not removed — it is the owner's file. `docs/ENVIRONMENT-FACTS.md`. `[verified]`
- 2026-10-03 · **`[ruled]` by the owner: the four cluster tenants are `client`.** `staging`, `gtb`, `jaizbankplc` and `lasg` carried no `cadra.database_class` (measured through `db-query`, 4 of 4 absent), so the persona seed refused them. The cluster is the demo estate, not the production topology (`CLAUDE.md`), and the local replicas of the same tenants are already `client`. Consequence, accepted: the two owner-run cleanup scripts accept them too. `[ruled]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Demo personas on the cluster, with the passwords encrypted out.** `.github/workflows/demo-personas.yml` pipes the same three scripts into `odoo shell` in the running `odoo-19` pod (`set-email-relay.yml`'s pattern). A GitHub log and an artifact are both readable by anybody with read access to the repository, so the passwords never leave the runner in clear: `scripts/demo_personas_ci.sh` registers each as a log mask, prints everything else, and encrypts the block (AES-256-CBC, its key RSA-OAEP-encrypted) to `scripts/demo_personas_recipient.pem`, whose private half exists only on the owner's machine (`docs/api/.personas.key.pem`, gitignored). `scripts/demo_personas_fetch.sh <run-id>` decrypts into `docs/api/.personas.cluster.<db>`. Classification is an input, `classify_absent_as=client`, and writes ONLY where no class is recorded — an existing class, `production` included, is printed and never changed. Proved locally by running the workflow's own run step (only the `kubectl exec` line swapped for `docker compose exec`) against an unclassified scratch copy: classify, seed, remove, seed, fetch, decrypt. The options that lost: masking alone (a mask does nothing for an artifact, and one stray echo is the whole leak); passwords as a repository secret (a credential stored where every workflow can read it, to solve a problem that only needs a one-way trip). `[landed]`

## 2026-10-03 — handoff 48 (what this tenant has)

- 2026-10-03 · **Which sidebar items a tenant shows is a setting, and it is presentation, not permission — said on the screen, in the payload and by a guard.** Handoff 48 asked for a per-tenant switch answering *"does this organisation have it at all?"*, beside handoff 46's *"may this person see it?"*. An item is drawn when the tenant shows it AND the person's scopes open it; neither axis consults the other. Nothing on the server reads a switch but its own route, and the app's route table does not consult them (`tests/test_sidebar_catalog.py` fails on either), so an item switched off still opens by its address and its data comes back — measured on `test19` (`/fleet` → `GET /api/v1/fleet` 200) and `boot44` (`/assets/list` → 200 assets), as the administrator and as the Store Officer. The Sidebar screen leads with *"This changes what is shown, not what is permitted"* and the server's `statement`; the payload carries `is_a_permission: false`. A tenant that must not have a module needs it uninstalled. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Keyed on a declared literal, not the label, the path or the scope.** Each item and group carries a `key` in `lib/navigation.ts` and in `SIDEBAR_GROUPS` (`myservice_api/controllers/v1_sidebar.py`). The label is reworded, the path re-routed and the scope re-ruled (handoff 47 is about to change several); a key derived from any of them would switch an item a tenant had turned off back on the day it changed. The key changes only when somebody edits the key, and the static guard holds the two tables equal (keys, grouping, order) and holds every key published on 2026-10-03 in the table or in `SIDEBAR_RETIRED`, never reused. The options that lost: the path (the 46 spec already reads `data-nav-path`, but a route move would silently un-hide), the label (two items are both called *Depreciation*). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **One `ir.config_parameter` row per switch, absent = on, never deleted.** `cadra.sidebar.item.<key>` / `cadra.sidebar.group.<key>` = `on`/`off`. Per switch, not one row holding a list, because the configuration-change report can only name who LAST wrote a row: with a list, the second administrator would be reported as the author of the first one's change. Absent means on, so no tenant lost anything the day this shipped (0 rows on 9 of 9 local databases before the first switch) and an item added later appears. Switching back on writes `on` rather than deleting, because a deleted parameter leaves no tombstone in that report; switching on what nobody switched off writes nothing, because a row there would be a "change" that changed nothing. The report already reads every parameter, so nothing was added to it; none of the 38 keys trips its credential redaction. Not the access record: that record is for permissions, and this is not one. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **A group is a master switch.** An item is shown when its own switch and its group's are both on; switching a group off hides its items and leaves their own switches untouched, so switching it back on restores exactly what was there (`tests/test_sidebar_settings.py`, both directions). The alternative — an item switched on overrides its group — makes a group switch that sometimes does nothing, and the screen could not say in one sentence what it does. An item hidden only by its group says so on the screen and keeps its switch on. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **The lockout: the Sidebar screen and its group cannot be switched off — refused at the door AND ignored on read.** (Consequence, accepted: an administrator can switch off the Administration group, after which Users opens only by its address until it is switched back on from the Sidebar screen — which is still drawn.) The switches live on `/settings/sidebar` (key `sidebar_settings`, `admin`, under Settings); with it or the Settings group off, nothing could be switched back on from the app. The PATCH refuses either, `400`, before anything is written — a legitimate switch in the same body included (measured: the row set in Postgres identical before and after). The GET reports both as `locked` and `enabled` whatever a row says, so a row put there by the back office or a shell hides nothing. **Boundary:** guarded is exactly what draws the screen; everything else — Users and the Administration group, the other three Settings items — can be switched off, because each can be switched back on from the screen while it is drawn. The guard holds the locked key to the item whose path IS `/settings/sidebar`, so moving the screen moves the lock. Not guarded, and not this handoff's to guard: an administrator removing their own `admin` scope — that is the role model (handoff 42 refuses self-grants, not self-removal). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Who last changed a switch is readable by every signed-in caller.** The GET is open to any token because the sidebar needs it on every screen for every role, and it carries `changed_by` (id and name) — so an integration credential (Vendra's, the Tag-Issuing Unit's) can read the names of the administrators who changed the sidebar, and the browser's persisted cache holds them for up to six hours. Accepted rather than split by caller: the names are of this tenant's own administrators, the same people `/users` names to its readers; a payload whose shape changed with the caller's scope would make `configured: true, changed_by: null` mean two different things. Recorded because the verification pass rightly said nobody had written it down. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **`landingPath` prefers a page the tenant shows, then a page the role opens.** After sign-in — by the sign-in page, or by an invitation or reset redeemed — the app reads the switches WITH THE NEW TOKEN BEFORE STORING IT (`login(…, beforeStore)`, `tenantHiddenWith`): storing it swaps the sign-in page for the app, and the first version, which read them after, rendered `/` in between — the command centre and its four reads for most people, a second `/no-access` for the Tag-Issuing Unit, caught by the handoff 46 spec's record of every URL passed through. The app and lands on the first page in the landing order the role opens AND the tenant shows; failing that, the first the role opens regardless — never `/no-access`, which would tell somebody their role opens nothing, which is false. An unreadable answer hides nothing. `/` (the front door) is unchanged: it redirects only on scopes. `[landed]`



## 2026-10-03 — handoff 47 (a role is a job)

- 2026-10-03 · **A scope that means two things gets split — `identity:read` and `dashboards:read`, each in two.** `[ruled]` by the Architect on report 46 §E. `access:read` is the record of who granted whom which role (`GET /reports/access-changes`); `identity:read` keeps the employee roster. `operations:read` is the fleet, staff-bus, FM and security dashboards; `dashboards:read` keeps the asset register's own figures (`/dashboard/kpis`, `/dashboard/map`, `/reports/depreciation`). Both are BREAKING for credentials provisioned under the old name, announced in `docs/api/CHANGELOG.md`. The Auditor grants `access:read` (fixing both halves of the 46 finding: every Asset User could read the access record and the Auditor could not find it); the Security Officer and Bus User groups grant `operations:read` and no longer `dashboards:read`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **`operations:read` is the name, and it is NOT the sidebar group key `operations`.** The scope covers fleet + staff bus + FM + security, so `fleet:read` would lie on three of its four consumers; the published switch key `operations` (the Locations screens' group, handoff 48) is a different namespace and is never reused as a scope. Both tables say so at the declaration site.
- 2026-10-03 · **Minting and replacing a tag is tagging, not asset-writing.** `[ruled]` ruling 2. `POST /barcode/generate` and `POST /tags/<id>/request-replacement` take `tagging:write` (BREAKING — they took `assets:write`, so every Asset Manager could print a label while the Tag-Issuing Unit could not). Deciding a replacement stays `approvals:write`: the second person, from `APPROVER_GROUP`, as on a move. `POST /assets` still mints its tag server-side, so registration is unaffected. Measured consequence, accepted: an Asset Manager no longer mints or replaces labels. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **The Asset Officer — the role between reading the register and running it.** `[ruled]` ruling 3, a new ROLE and no new scope: `group_asset_officer`, Asset User's reads plus `assets:write`, implying Asset User and implied by Asset Manager (the ladder User < Officer < Manager, both of Manager's implications spelled out in the XML). Deliberately not `approvals:write` (handoff 35's second person), not `settings:write`, `integrations:write`, `locations:write` or `ingestion:write`. Grantable over the API — the fifth entry in `API_GRANTABLE_XMLIDS`, which handoff 42 said must be a decision with a name on it; this is that decision. `SCOPE_GROUPS[assets:write]` now maps a client to the Officer group, not the Manager: a credential that registers assets (Vendra, INT-03) is doing an officer's job, and `approvals:write` still maps to the Manager so a deciding credential keeps `APPROVER_GROUP`. No ACL rows: every `/api/v1` route sudo()s and the back office is shut to non-administrators (handoff 45) — the TIU pairing's reasoning, re-applied. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-03 · **Staff Bus and Identity (clearance) leave the sidebar; the code stays.** `[ruled]` §1b on report 48 §C: both render sample data built into the app and ask the server nothing, so with default-on every new client was shown two screens of invented content. Keys retired in `SIDEBAR_RETIRED` (never reused); routes still serve by address. NRS is owed a REAL clearance screen — their offboarding question is what the mock pretended to answer; the mechanism exists in the back office (report 43 §G.2) — recorded as owed in `docs/GO-LIVE.md` §25, not dropped.
- 2026-10-03 · **The "All systems operational" pill is deleted.** `[ruled]` §1b: a hardcoded badge on the front door reporting a thing it does not check — the defect the programme is named after, in the most visible place. Either it measures something or it goes; report 47 records what making it real would take (a server-side health answer: registry reachable, scheduler live, outbox not backed up — none of which exists as a route today).
- 2026-10-03 · **IT Assets Command should become a saved view on All Assets, not a menu item.** `[ruled]` §1b: it is the register filtered to one category (69 of 11,951 on `nrs19`, 0 on five of eight tenants). It stays in the sidebar for now — the tenant switch handles it — but it is not a product line, and the next person should not treat it as a module.
- 2026-10-03 · **Fleet is the owner's question, untouched.** All four fleet screens stay exactly as they were (behind `operations:read`); whether fleet is in scope for any client is for NRS, not engineering. The real register holds 0 vehicles.

## 2026-10-03 — handoff 37 (the doors, and the two red tests nobody named)

- 2026-10-03 · `[ruled]` **One name per field, published and writable — handoff 34's convention wins over 33 §B2.** Where a published key and the writable field already differ, the fix is to PUBLISH THE WRITABLE NAME, keeping the old published name as a **read-only output alias** where a consumer already depends on it — **never as an accepted input**. The reason is report 36's: a second accepted spelling would slip past `PATCH /assets/<id>`'s approval gate, which tests `f in ("location_id", "custodian_id")` literally, and surface as the model's `UserError` instead of the designed 400 — a convenience that opens a hole in a control is not a convenience. That "accept both" is undefined for `current_location`, which publishes an object, settles it twice. Recorded so 33 §B2 can be re-queued honestly; the renaming work itself is NOT in handoff 37.
- 2026-10-03 · `[ruled]` **The identity door retires; SAP gets `POST /api/v1/integrations/sap/employee-sync`.** The third option report 36 found, closing 33's items 1, 2 and 4 at once: a validated, authenticated door replaces an unvalidated one rather than gaining a second guard. Two conditions, both met in 37: the old route is NOT removed and its behaviour at the route level is unchanged (retiring a door SAP may be posting to is an outage), and the census was re-taken before being quoted — **0 `webhook` rows of 287 sync-log rows across all eight tenants, 2026-10-03** (286 `api`, all our suites; 1 `csv_import`), the secret set on **0 of 8** tenants and provisioned by nothing in the repo, so no caller can even reach it. The figure had already moved 163 → 179 → 286, which is why quoting an old count was forbidden.
- 2026-10-03 · **The legacy door was measured side by side with the v1 route, and it cannot do its own job on Odoo 19.** Driven with the same bodies on `test19`: every employee create AND update through `/api/sap/employee-sync` fails — `hr_version.last_modified_uid` is NOT NULL on 19 and an `auth='none'` environment has no user to stamp — reported per-row as `status: "error"` under a top-level `"success": true`, and the list-of-employees shape its own docstring offers is refused by Odoo 19's JSON-RPC dispatcher (`Invalid JSON-RPC data`) before the route runs. So the door has been inert as a writer for the life of the 19 stack, which is independent confirmation that nothing uses it. The v1 route answered every body the old door can actually accept, against the same register, same per-row `results[]` schema.
- 2026-10-03 · **A payload key the SAP feed does not read is refused ON THE MODEL, whole, for all six callers at once** (33 §A2/§A4 as re-issued by 37 §2B, with report 36's correction: four siblings, not three — `scripts/load_employees_xlsx.py` is the fourth). `process_sap_payload` answers an unknown key with an `error` log row naming the keys and the accepted set, nothing written to the employee — a RETURN, not a raise, because the webhook has no row handler. The v1 route's whole-batch 400 (handoff 32) stays and fires first for its callers; what the pre-flight buys is batch atomicity, not the rule. The CSV wizard until now passed every unrecognised spreadsheet column straight through as a key; a misspelt column is now an errored row instead of a silently incomplete import.
- 2026-10-03 · **Quick-submit's `required` pair is enforced — the schema stops being fiction** (33 §C as written). `type_id` and a non-blank `description`, refused in the delegate so both doors agree: the v1 route answers the 400 with `details.missing_fields` (third refusal key, beside `rejected_fields` and `unusable_fields` — three refusals, three repairs), the public twin answers a visitor's sentence in words. The first-active-type fallback and the *"Quick facilities request"* placeholder title are deleted with it — an empty body no longer creates an empty record, which on the public twin was unauthenticated. BREAKING, announced in `CHANGELOG.md`; the homepage form has enforced both in JavaScript since it was written, so no live caller sent what is now refused.
- 2026-10-03 · **The two red depreciation tests were a defect in the BEHAVIOUR, not the tests — and they were red for one hour a night, which is why six handoffs of green suites never named them.** `nrs.asset._stamp_disposal` stamps `disposal_date` as the LAGOS day (`snapshot_today`, handoff 32); `_apply_depreciation` measured `as_of` with `context_today` — the UTC day for any user with no timezone, which every API credential's user and the test runner's superuser are. Between 23:00 and 24:00 UTC the stamp is tomorrow relative to `as_of`, the engine's `as_of >= disposal_date` check misses, and — disposal truncating the schedule, so every remaining period has ended — the state falls through to `fully_depreciated`. Reports 31, 32 and 34 ran the suite green with these tests in the denominator (775, 796 and 820 tests, CI's module list) — and the frozen clock proves a green run can only happen OUTSIDE the window, so those runs were. Report 35 never ran this module's suite at all (it named `asset_register` and `api` only, 432 + 282). Report 36's session is the first on record to land inside the window — its drive stamps read 22:49–22:50 UTC, minutes before midnight Lagos, and its suite runs followed. **Nothing read past a failure; the failure was not there when the green runs ran.** Fixed by giving `_apply_depreciation` the same definition of today the stamp uses (`snapshot_today`), proved by a frozen-clock regression test that failed on the old code at any hour (`'fully_depreciated' != 'disposed'`, 1 failed of 1) and passes on the fix. The depreciation suite IS in the CI gate (`ci.yml` upgrades `myservice_asset_depreciation` with `--test-enable`), so the gate was never the gap — the clock was.
- 2026-10-03 · **Found beside the drive and left, named:** `process_sap_payload`'s `skipped` ("No changes detected") status is unreachable in practice — `sap_last_sync` is stamped `now()` on every call, so an identical body re-sent one second later reports `success` with a one-field change. A silent instrument in the small: the status exists, reads well, and nothing can reach it except two calls in the same second. Not fixed here; it is a reporting nicety on a feed whose real door is the v1 route.

The screen states the register's size BEFORE the click, shows a preparing state while the one-piece body builds, and afterwards the file's name, row count, bytes and elapsed time; a truncation header renders in amber naming cap and total. The spec drives 1990, where `test19` holds 0 of its 2,0xx disposals. ## 2026-10-04 — handoff 50 (the consequences)

- 2026-10-04 · **The export is its own permission — `export:read`, granted by exactly the Auditor.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 50 ruling 1) on report 49 §A: reading a page of the register and taking a copy of all of it are different acts, and `assets:read` meant both — the same one-name-two-meanings defect handoff 47 split twice. Both export routes (`GET /assets/export`, `GET /assets/export.csv`) take the new scope; the `admin` wildcard covers administrators; no register group gains or loses anything else. Named `export:read`, not `assets:export`, because `test_auditor_access.py` pins every Auditor scope to end in `:read` — the pin encodes that the Auditor changes nothing, an export changes nothing, and a name that forces an allow-list hole in a guard is the wrong name. BREAKING, announced in `CHANGELOG.md`; per-tenant counts of who loses it are in report 50 (session users are the real surface — an API credential's export was already capped at 100 rows). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-04 · **The register dashboard's map gets what the register dashboard reads: locations.** `[ruled]` handoff 50 §2.1, closing GO-LIVE §25's open hole in handoff 47's own fix. Every first-party consumer of `GET /dashboard/map` except one read only `locations`; the one — the Fleet Command Center, an `operations:read` screen — read the vehicle rows, so the map's fleet half was a fleet screen wearing the register's scope and MOVED (`GET /fleet/map`, `operations:read`). `recent_requests` went with it: read by nothing, documented wrongly, and gone. The back-office twin `/myservice/dashboard/map` keeps the full shape — it is `auth='user'` and the back office is administrators-only since handoff 45. Measured before changing: 0 geocoded vehicles of 3,338 vehicles on all nine local tenants, so the leak was the shape, never yet a row; the proof used a seeded evidence vehicle on `test19`, read as a Store Officer before (plate and driver in the body) and after (403 on `/fleet/map`, no `fleet` key on the map). BREAKING, announced. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-04 · **Any render error is a sentence, not a blank page.** `[ruled]` handoff 50 ruling 2 on report 49 §E.2. One `RouteErrorBoundary` (class component — React still requires one for error boundaries) wraps the page area INSIDE the shell, keyed on the location so navigating away clears it, and wraps the shell-less public pages too. It shows what happened in words, what the person can do, and the error's message so there is something to report; the sidebar stays alive. Proved by deliberately feeding `/assets` a map payload whose `locations` is a string — the boundary rendered, the sidebar stayed, the next page drew (spec 50, `test19` and `boot44`). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-04 · **The attribution rule is mechanical.** `[ruled]` handoff 50 ruling 3, after reports 37 and 49 both shipped a Step-0 commit carrying the tool's default trailer. `scripts/check_commit_messages.sh` refuses a message matching any AI-attribution pattern; `scripts/git-hooks/commit-msg` runs it locally (`git config core.hooksPath scripts/git-hooks`, set in this clone); CI job `commit-messages` runs it over exactly the NEW commits of a push or PR — never full history, because 206 of 209 commits reachable from `dev` predate the mechanism and stay as the owner ruled. Proved both ways: a live `git commit` carrying the trailer was refused (no commit created) and the clean Step-0 range passed. `--no-verify` skips any hook; the CI half cannot be skipped. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-04 · **`mobile` stops being accepted; the other two write-only keys are published.** Handoff 33 §B1's "measure which", executed: 0 of 15,780 employees across all nine tenants hold a mobile number (nrs19's real extract included), 0 of 321 sync payloads ever carried the key — nothing sends it, nothing wants it, so it is removed from the model-level accepted set for all six callers (BREAKING, announced; the feed is live on no tenant). `salvage_rate` (10,364 of 11,173 on `test19` carry one) and `verification_notes` (193 of 11,173) are real data someone wrote and could never read back — published on the asset GET, and their removal from the census's `_WRITE_ONLY` waiver list makes the read-back guard prove the fix. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-04 · **The two red browser specs are named, not fixed, and neither is handoff 37's defect.** Handoff 50 §3's question answered with the first recorded failure texts (they had never been captured): spec 13 is a fixture-selection defect in the SPEC — it takes the first computed-method asset without checking it has a cost or useful life, and the product's honest "No schedule yet" empty state fails its assumption; spec 14's shape (the flip passes, the reload shows the old value, no refetch for 30 s) matches GO-LIVE §16.3's persisted-cache hypothesis point for point — handoff 29's family, not a depreciation defect. Both predate 37's one-hour-a-night window and fail at every hour. Evidence: `docs/handoffs/50-evidence/red-specs-13-14.md`. `[open]` — the fixes are a spec fixture and a cache-policy decision.

## 2026-10-05 — handoff 51 (the queue and the refusal)

- 2026-10-05 · **A route published as *pending* stays pending-only; the requester's question gets a sibling route.** `[ruled]` handoff 51 §1a, after a tag replacement was raised with an approver named and appeared on neither the approver's screens nor the requester's — the model carried every fact and the only screen displaying any of it was the tagging console. `GET /api/v1/tags/replacements` (`assets:read`) takes `state` (the label's own values), `waiting_on_me` and `raised_by_me`; `pending-replacements` is unchanged, because adding `state` to it would make one published name carry two meanings, and *the requester's question is not "what is pending", it is "what happened to mine"* — unanswerable by a pending-only endpoint however many filters it grows. The base set is labels that have ever had a replacement requested (`requested_by` set, which approval and rejection both preserve). The `/approvals` page shows tag replacements beside asset-movement requests, both kinds in the same tabs, each kind keeping its own query and its own honest count; the Barcode Station panel stays, because one screen answering the approver's and the requester's question does not mean one screen in the product. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **A refused affix leaves a record.** `[ruled]` handoff 51 §2. The wrong-tag refusal text names the expected number (handoff 39's ruling, which stands), so one wrong entry reveals the right answer and a second attempt with it passes — and that sequence used to leave exactly one trace: a clean affix record saying a human verified the label. Now the mismatch writes an append-only `nrs.asset.event` of the new type `affix_refused` (payload: the scanned value as entered; the expected number stays on the label row) before the `ValidationError` is raised. The row is written ON ITS OWN CURSOR in `_record_affix_refusal`, because the API decorator rolls the whole request transaction back on a `ValidationError` — measured both ways on `test19` (`51-evidence/same-cursor-probe.txt`: the same-cursor row exists after a flush, is invisible to every other connection, and is gone after the rollback; `51-evidence/affix-refusal-http.txt`: the own-cursor row survives the real 400 into a separate read). Under a running test the row goes on the test's own cursor instead, because only `HttpCase` patches `registry.cursor` — a plain `TransactionCase` would get a real second connection that blocks forever on the test's uncommitted asset row at the foreign-key check. It does not block, warn or score anyone; it writes down what happened. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **Spec 13's fixture selects a CHARTABLE asset; spec 14 is measured, not fixed.** `[ruled]` handoff 51 §3. The first computed-method asset is not the first chartable one; the spec now selects on every gate the engine's own `build_schedule` applies (computed method, cost > 0, useful life > 0, start date, salvage < cost, a rate for declining balance), and skips LOUDLY naming the reason when no such asset exists — a spec that quietly finds nothing to assert and goes green is the silent-instrument class in a test's clothing. Spec 14 gained the §3b measurement only: the persisted `cadra.queryCache` entry for `depreciation-settings` is dumped between the click and the reload and again after, with no settling pause (a pause would let the 1 s write throttle flush and measure a different spec) and with the expected value recorded beside every dump — the first dumps lacked it and were briefly misread. The measurement CONFIRMS GO-LIVE §16.3 item 8: the failing attempt restored a pre-click payload the throttle had preserved and never refetched it; the passing retry had no persisted entry and fetched fresh. The cache policy itself is deliberately untouched — that fix is a product-wide `staleTime`/throttle decision and is the Architect's, from the measurement. `[open]` — the cache-policy decision.
- 2026-10-05 · **Two of the Architect's own rulings withdrawn, in the repository.** `[ruled]` handoff 51 §§4–5, recorded in `docs/retired-screens.md` so the next person with either idea finds the answer already written: the IT Assets Command page is NOT demoted to a saved view (the per-tenant sidebar switch of handoff 48 is the right control, and it is shipped), and the Security Command Center source is NOT deleted (ships to nobody, already guarded against silent return, and the in-place source is what keeps the documented ~2-day port estimate honest). `[landed]`

## 2026-10-05 — handoff 52 (the Builder's list)

- 2026-10-05 · **The sidebar badge counts BOTH kinds of waiting work, and shows nothing rather than half.** `[ruled]` handoff 52 §1 on report 51 §I: the Approvals page had shown tag replacements beside movement requests since 51, and the number beside the menu item still counted only `/approvals` — `test19` held 214 pending replacements the badge read as zero of. `fetchApprovalsWaitingCount` now sums the two routes' `total_estimate`s (`/approvals` and `/tags/replacements`, both `state=pending&waiting_on_me=true&limit=1`, both `assets:read`); if either total is missing the badge draws nothing, because *3* when the truth is *3 plus an unknown* is the same under-report with a confident face. `invalidateTagQueries` invalidates `approval-count` so deciding a replacement moves the badge without a reload. Driven as the Asset Manager on `test19` (0 movement approvals waiting): badge **1** with one replacement naming them — a number the old badge could never show — and **not drawn** after approving it. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **The station panel says what it is showing; the honest cap won over paging.** `[ruled]` handoff 52 §2 offered either. The Barcode Station is a scan console and its pending panel a glance-list; the full, paged, filterable record is the Approvals page (handoff 51). So `fetchPendingTagReplacements` keeps one page of 100 but now returns the pagination block it used to throw away, and when `next_cursor` is non-null the panel ends with the truth: *Showing the 100 most recent — 114 more on the Approvals page*, the remainder computed from the server's own `total_estimate` (driven on `test19`, 100 + 114 = 214). A second scrolling queue at the station would be a second queue UI to keep honest for no one who stands there. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **The IT dashboard matches a word, not a substring — and the stable key lost on measurement.** `[ruled]` handoff 52 §3. `/IT|computer|laptop|server/i` was satisfied by the *it* in Furn**it**ure and Secur**it**y/CCTV; `jaizbankplc`'s page titled IT Assets Command reported *21 Furniture records*. `nrs.asset.category` does carry `code` and `domain` — but both are SEED values: on `jaizbankplc` the one IT-populated category, *IT Equipment* (21 assets), has neither, so matching `domain='it'` would have missed every real IT asset on the very tenant that showed the defect. The name is the only key every tenant fills in; the regex is now `/\b(IT|computer|laptop|server)\b/i`, and the page prints *Showing: <category> (N assets)* as its first line under the title — the furniture count was visible for weeks and nobody saw it, because the heading said IT and the eye believed the heading. The other two dashboards in the group checked: Fleet Command selects on `fleet.vehicle` (a model, not a name) and the Facility Command Center reads register aggregates — neither has the pattern. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **All three affix refusals leave a record, each naming its reason — and the err-path placement was measured, not inherited.** `[ruled]` handoff 52 §4. The scrapped-label raise in `_mark_affixed` and the route's serial-mismatch `err()` now call the same `_record_affix_refusal` as 51's number mismatch, with `reason` (`number_mismatch` / `scrapped` / `serial_mismatch`) in the event's typed payload — the existing `reason` column, no schema change. A NULL reason is a pre-52 row and publishes as `number_mismatch`, the only kind recorded then. The history sentence differs per reason, so a wrong label, a dead label and a label going onto the wrong box are three different lines. **The measurement** (`52-evidence/same-cursor-probe-err-path.txt`): on the `err()` path a same-cursor write SURVIVES into a separate read — the opposite of the raise path, because no exception means no decorator rollback — so either placement works there; the shared own-cursor recorder ships anyway: one mechanism, one test-mode branch, and a row that stays final if the `err` is ever turned into a raise. The no-serial-on-file refusal (`cannot be verified`) stays unrecorded, named rather than silently widened: it is a *cannot check*, not a mismatch, and the handoff scoped §4 to the two. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **Whatever is restored from the persisted cache paints immediately and is refetched immediately — restored data is never fresh.** `[ruled]` by the Architect, handoff 52 §5, on report 51 §3b's measurement. Implemented as `onSuccess` on `PersistQueryClientProvider`: the restore completes, every restored entry is invalidated, mounted queries refetch at once and the rest as they mount (`refetchOnMount: true` was already in place). The exclusion list is untouched, the 1 s write throttle is untouched, and no key was excluded to make a spec pass. Cost, measured (`52-evidence/page-ready-*.json`): content paint unchanged (~350–475 ms on all three screens), the refetch burst is +15/+3/+3 API calls per reload on dashboard / register list / approvals. **Spec 14's own race, found by the ruling:** the toggle flips optimistically, so the spec's first assertion passed while the PATCH was in flight, and its immediate reload ABORTED the write — the spec used to go green by reading its own restored sketch of a value the server never held. It now awaits the PATCH response (the act under test) before reloading, still inside the throttle window, and is green three runs in a row with the no-pause instrumentation kept; the post-reload dumps show the restored entry refetched (`dataUpdatedAt` moves). `tag-pending`'s cached shape changed (§2), so the buster is `v6`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **Two guards: the scope maps and the endpoint index cannot drift silently.** `[ruled]` handoff 52 §6. `tests/test_scope_maps.py` holds `use-scopes.ts` `SCOPE` and `Users.tsx` `SCOPE_WORDS` to the 19 scopes `utils.py` owns, both directions, denominators printed — the SCOPE map was carrying 17 of 19 (`ingestion:write`, `webhooks:admin`), now complete with a note on why entries nothing gates on still belong. `tests/test_endpoint_reference.py` re-derives the route census statically (AST over every first-party controller) and diffs it against the reference's endpoint index, both directions, with an exclusion table that demands a sentence per entry and a staleness check on each — the index carried 64 rows of 115 `/api/v1` routes and now carries all 115. Both guards were made red once by contrivance and green after (`52-evidence/guards-red-once.txt`). `[landed]`

## 2026-10-05 — handoff 54 (every time)

- 2026-10-05 · **The scanner's decoder is bundled, and it is THE engine — not a fallback.** `[ruled]` handoff 54 §1, after the owner found that some barcodes scan and some do not: the platform's `BarcodeDetector` exists on some devices and not others (not Safari, not Firefox, not Chrome on Windows/Linux), honours an unknowable subset of the formats it is asked for (`getSupportedFormats()` was never called), and made every failure a fact about whose phone it was. The engine is now `zxing-wasm` (3.1.4, the reader build) behind `src/lib/scanEngine.ts`, on every device; the platform detector is not kept even as an acceleration, because one engine is the point. Evaluated against the `barcode-detector` ponyfill the handoff named as the obvious candidate: the ponyfill lost because the handoff's own requirements (reticle-crop decode, format passed up, 1D two-decode agreement, throttle) rewrite the component anyway — "the change is the import, not the component" stopped being true — and the ponyfill is a second dependency wrapping the same zxing-wasm with its own API churn, while the corpus needs the inner package in Node regardless. The wasm (953 KB, 414 KB gzip) is served from our own origin and loads when the camera starts, never on app boot — proved by network trace in spec 54. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **A 1D value is accepted when two consecutive decodes agree; a 2D value on one; two different values in frame are a choice, never a guess.** `[ruled]` handoff 54 §§2, 4. The rules live in `src/lib/scanDecision.ts` as a pure function the e2e suite drives directly — proving the transient states (the "Reading…" between two 1D reads, the disagreement that replaces the candidate) through the fake camera would be a race, not a proof. Same-string-different-symbology is NOT agreement. The reticle is true since 54: the frame is cropped to the reticle's rectangle and decoded there first, the full frame only when the crop finds nothing — which is also what "prefer the one inside the reticle" means when a laptop's underside shows four codes. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **The refused-affix record says how the value was read; a successful affix stores nothing new.** `[ruled]` handoff 54 §§4, 7. `nrs.asset.event.scanned_format` (Selection over the 13 published names) is allowed only in the `affix_refused` payload; the route accepts `scanned_format`/`serial_format`, validates against the enum before anything is written, and refuses `serial_format` without a `serial_number` (contradictory input, handoff 34's rule). NULL publishes as null — a typed entry, a keyboard wedge, or a pre-54 row — never a guess. Announced in `docs/api/CHANGELOG.md`; additive, Vendra unaffected unless it sends the keys. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-05 · **The corpus gates on an invariant and a baseline, not on the handoff's literal must-pass bar — because the bar is missed by the LABEL.** `[ruled]` by the Builder in `e2e/corpus/run54.mjs`, stated for review: §6a demands Cadra's labels decode M of M at 0° and 90° undegraded, and the small and standard labels' Code 128 side scores 0 of 24 — a 15-character tag at ≤ 28.6 mm is ≈ 0.13 mm (≈ 5 mil) per module, below what a camera pipeline at 300-dpi-equivalent density resolves, exactly the label finding §6a itself anticipates ("a finding about the label, not the scanner"). The QR side reads 3 of 3 as printed on every size. So the script's exit gate is (1) every label size has at least one side the engine reads as printed, straight on, undegraded, and (2) no cell that passed at the committed baseline (`e2e/corpus/expected-pass.json`, 201 of 408) regresses. The must-pass column is still reported per row. **Open for the Architect:** whether the smallest labels should carry the QR larger or drop the Code 128 — the §9 numbers are in `docs/handoffs/54-evidence/corpus-table.md`. `[open]`
- 2026-10-05 · **The §6c real-device table cannot be produced by the Builder and is owner-blocked — a handoff defect, named.** Handoff 54 §6c requires driving the MacBook's built-in camera, an Android Chrome phone and an iPhone Safari with printed labels at measured distances, and its own rule is "no real device, no done" — but no "Needs the owner" block names it, and a Builder session cannot hold a printed label in front of a camera. The harness the owner needs is shipped (the station itself, plus the `scanner-granted` caption showing what `getSettings()` granted on the preview); the report carries the fill-in table and the §6c procedure. `[open]`
- 2026-10-05 · **A signature you grow is a contract every overrider holds.** The first full pytest run after `_mark_affixed` gained `scanned_format=None` failed 16 of 1320 — every affix 500'd — because `myservice_workflow` holds TWO overrides of it (the INT-05 and INT-06 event hooks) with the base signature spelled out, and nobody grepped for overriders before growing it. The mirror of the `_connect__` lesson (2026-10-02): there, core owned the name; here, our own module did. Both overrides now take `*args, **kwargs` and forward, with the comment saying why — an override that reads nothing but the transition has no business naming arguments it only forwards. `[landed]`

## 2026-10-07 — handoff 57 (the gates, tonight — SCAFFOLDING, replaced by handoff 58)

**Kept by handoff 58; see `docs/ADDENDUM-A-EVALUATION.md` §8.** (2026-10-12: §4.6 reversed — the candidate stays a state on `nrs.asset`; the entries below are the record of how it was built and stand as written.)

- 2026-10-07 · **The gate machine goes on `nrs.asset` for one night, knowingly against §4.6.** `[ruled]` handoff 57 §0: Addendum A's chain must be shown end to end by 07:00 Lagos, 8 October, and the correct build (a separate `nrs.asset.candidate`, evaluation §4.6) takes a week. One field (`register_state`, default `registered`, every existing row registered by migration), one table (`asset_gates.TRANSITIONS`) and one method (`_fire_gate`); `write` refuses the field without the flag `_fire_gate` sets. The top of `asset_gates.py` and `v1_gates.py` says, dated, that this is scaffolding and names 58. `[landed]` (Stream A)
- 2026-10-07 · **The register hides candidates in `nrs.asset._search`, not in a route helper — because there is no shared helper.** `[ruled]` by the Builder against §2d's literal "whatever shared helper builds the asset domain": the census found 22 routes that list, count, aggregate or export assets and only two (`GET /assets`, `/reports/asset-register`) share `_list_domain`; the dashboard strip, the export, the category and employee counts, the depreciation and disposal reports and the Redhubs export each build their own. A default in the model's `_search` is Odoo's own mechanism for `active` and is the one place all 22 pass. It steps aside when the domain names `register_state`, when the context carries `cadra_candidates`, and **when `active_test` is off** — the last found by the in-registry test, not reasoned: `BaseModel.fetch` re-reads records it holds through `_search([('id','in',ids)], active_test=False)`, so without it a candidate was unreadable (sudo included) once its cache dropped. `browse()` is not a search, so every by-id route reaches a candidate; the scan lookup, by-tag, the import's de-duplication, the SAP-number uniqueness constraint, the location/employee delete guards and the two owner-run scripts pass `cadra_candidates` explicitly. `[landed]` (Stream A)
- 2026-10-07 · **The label minted at `request-tagging` is numbered with the asset code.** `[ruled]` evaluation §7 / 4.3 (one identifier); handoff 57 allowed keeping today's number if it cost more than an hour — it cost minutes. A label is created with `name = asset_code`; `_generate_tag` is not called for a candidate, so no location-built number is minted. The scan box resolves it either way (tag number first). Registered assets and the switch-off path are untouched. `[landed]` (Stream A)
- 2026-10-07 · **The maker's typed SAP code is a code ENTERED BY HAND, and INT-06 still goes out once.** `[ruled]` by the Builder: `register` writes it to `sap_asset_number` with `sap_code_source = manual` — the honest label the dual-code control already has — and `_queue_sap_event` skipped a master create for any asset already carrying a number. The registration hook passes `cadra_gate_registration`, which lifts that one check only for a `manual` code; the payload gains `capitalisation.cost_centre` and `sap_asset_number_entered` (additive, null otherwise) and proposes the maker's capitalisation date. `[landed]` (Stream A)
- 2026-10-07 · **Query, resubmit, reject and return are not gates, but the rule checks them as the gate they act for.** `[ruled]` by the Builder, reading Addendum A §4 rule 1's list (receipt, verify, approve, tag request, tagging, register make, register check): a query is IAD acting (the officer's gate if the caller holds `vfm:verify`, else the approver's), a rejection is the approver's, a return is the checker's, a resubmission is Store's. Their events carry no `gate`, so they cannot block anybody later. Tagging IS a gate: the existing affix path refuses a candidate's affix by somebody who fired another gate on it, and records `tag_issued → tagged` as the tagger's. `[landed]` (Stream A)
- 2026-10-07 · **Five roles, grantable, each implying Asset User; Maker and Checker share one privilege on the user form.** `[ruled]` handoff 57 §2e; the shared privilege is the Builder's — it makes the two a single choice on the form, which is the addendum's default role exclusion (Register Maker with Register Checker) at zero cost; the API grant does not enforce it (the role-exclusion table is out of scope tonight). Added to `API_GRANTABLE_XMLIDS` because a role an administrator cannot grant from the Users screen does not exist in the room. Asset User and the Auditor gain `candidates:read`. `[landed]` (Stream A)

## 2026-10-12 — handoff 58 (the gates, kept)

- 2026-10-12 · **§4.6 is reversed: the candidate stays a state on `nrs.asset`.** `[ruled]` Architect, `docs/ADDENDUM-A-EVALUATION.md` §8 — report 57's probe showed the one property a separate `nrs.asset.candidate` was meant to buy (no list, count, report, export or dashboard shows a candidate) already holds through ONE filter in `nrs.asset._search`, failing safe. Handoff 58 takes the one-night labels off and makes it the product. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **The register filter is load-bearing (P8), and a tenant behind its code says so.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3 P8, placed by the Builder where it costs least: (1) `cadra_route` maps `UndefinedColumn`/`UndefinedTable` to `503 server_misconfigured` naming the missing column and the upgrade (`utils.schema_behind`) — every route, one `except`; (2) `GET /api/v1/meta/readiness`, **unauthenticated**, lists every stored column the registry expects and the database lacks, per module, and answers 200 `{ready: true}` otherwise. Unauthenticated on purpose: the person who needs it is checking a deploy, and on the cluster nothing scripted can sign in (Turnstile). It names tables, columns and module names, never data. The k8s probes are NOT moved onto it — a probe that fails on a missed upgrade would restart the pod into the same failure; it is a check for a person. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **THE helper for "an asset on the register" in the HTTP suite is `tests/gates.py`.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §2 ("pick one helper, name it in DECISIONS"): `receive(client, **body)` (POST /assets, filled in for the class when the gates are on), `on_the_register(client, asset)` (walks the seven gates as the seven demo personas, over HTTP — the no-two-gates rule makes one credential unable to), `ready_to_affix(client, asset)` (to `tag_issued`, for a test about affixing), `new_registered_asset`. With the switch off all are no-ops. Chosen over "create at `registered` the way the migration did" because over HTTP that has no honest door — a bypass flag would be a way round the gates in the contract — and walking the chain proves it end to end ~80 times a run (≈3 s a walk once the personas are signed in). A tenant with the switch on and no persona file FAILS, never skips. In-registry, the same job is `GateCrew` in `myservice_api/tests/test_receipt_gates.py`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **D3: a no-two-gates refusal at affix is a `409 conflict`, `details.reason = segregation_of_duties`, like every other gate.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3. The label override now raises the gates' own `GateRefused` (`_assert_two_gates`, which carries `details.earlier`), and `cadra_route` maps every `GateRefused` in ONE place (`utils.gate_refusal`) — so the gate routes, the affix route and the P5 refusals cannot drift. The sentence is unchanged; every other affix refusal keeps its 400. BREAKING for a client branching on the affix status (CHANGELOG). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **P4: SAP's number never silently replaces the maker's.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3. `_set_sap_master`: when the record's code is the maker's (`manual`) and SAP's INT-06 answer differs, the maker's number stays in `sap_asset_number`, SAP's goes into `sap_returned_asset_number` (+ `sap_discrepancy_at`), and the dual-code state is `exception` — checked FIRST in the compute, before presence. Equal (stripped, case-insensitive): `sap_code_source` becomes `confirmed` (*Entered by hand, confirmed by SAP*) and nothing else changes. **"The existing SAP reconciliation surface" is the dual-code state and its filter (`?sap_reconciliation_state=exception`) — there is no discrepancy record in Cadra and the handoff assumed one;** the discrepancy is therefore published as `data.sap.discrepancy` (both numbers, the maker and the checker read from the gate events, the time) and posted on the asset's chatter. Only the integration may write the new fields or the `confirmed` value (`_assert_sap_evidence_allowed`). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **P5: a candidate takes no custodian, no move, no transfer, no disposal — through any writer.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3. On the model: `_assert_on_the_register` in `nrs.asset._write_recorded` (after the terminal rule, BEFORE the approval gate, whose exemptions it must not inherit), `nrs.asset.create` for a `captured` receipt carrying a custodian, and the `create` of `nrs.asset.change.request` and `nrs.asset.transfer` — a request is refused when RAISED, because with approval on nothing touches the asset until a second person agrees. Plus explicit calls on `PATCH`, `/move`, `/transfer-custody` and `/condemn` so the candidate sentence speaks before any other refusal. Clearing a custodian or a location takes nothing onto a candidate and passes; naming an unchanged value passes. The sentence names whom the unit waits on. `GateRefused('not_on_the_register')` → 409. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **D6: `waiting_on` publishes seven values; the endpoint reference now says seven.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3 — `iad_approver` is served (§3 of 57 needed "waiting on IAD approver") and the reference listed six. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **P1 (kept, no code): query, resubmit, reject and return are not gates** — the 2026-10-07 entry above stands; their events carry no `gate`, so they block nobody later. `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3.
- 2026-10-12 · **D5 (kept, no code): with the switch off, responses are byte-for-byte as before except additive keys** — 57's `register_state` and `gates` on every asset and two null keys in the INT-06 payload, and 58's own additive keys (CHANGELOG 2026-10-12). `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3, §7.
- 2026-10-12 · **INT-05 (kept, no code): `asset.tag.affixed` still fires when a candidate's label is affixed** — the label IS on the box; the Tag-Issuing Unit's outbox records a physical fact, not a registration. `[ruled]` handoff 58 §3.
- 2026-10-12 · **Section 12: the class decides how a unit is identified; the unit keeps the mode it arrived with.** `[ruled]` owner, evaluation §7/4.1; built by handoff 58 §4. `nrs.asset.category.identification_mode` (`serial`/`tag_id`/`pool`, default `serial`) and `class_attributes_schema` (`{"mandatory": [...]}` over the POST /assets names `name, make, model_name, manufacturer, colour, material, dimensions`; empty = the mode's default — serial: make, model; tag_id: AD-32's six, description being the asset's `name`). Furniture (FM domain `furniture` or a name saying so) seeded `tag_id` by migration (D11); nothing defaults to `pool`, which is declared and refused at receipt. Every check applies to a GATED receipt only, so the switch-off path is unchanged (§7). The mode is stamped on the unit at receipt (`nrs.asset.identification_mode`, AD-31) — a class changed later does not reach a unit already in the chain. New attribute fields `colour`, `material`, `dimensions` on `nrs.asset`, writable and published. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Serial uniqueness did not exist in Cadra; it does now, at the gated receipt only.** `[ruled]` by the Builder against the handoff's "it already should, so prove it": no constraint, no route check, nothing — only Vendra refused duplicates.
