A constraint is impossible (`nrs19` holds 1,291 serials on more than one asset, handoff 38), so a gated receipt whose serial is already on ANY asset (candidates and archived included; stripped, case-insensitive) is refused naming the asset code; empty serials are never compared — the hundred chairs. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **"No serial, no receipt" in Cadra speaks Vendra's sentence.** `[ruled]` handoff 58 §4 — *"<class>: 0 of 1 serial numbers captured. Capture one serial number per accepted unit."*, Vendra's `_serial_count_errors` for one unit. Vendra already refuses a captured line without make and model (`_missing_capture_field_errors`), measured before the serial-mode schema was enforced, so the default schema cannot dead-letter a Vendra receipt on `staging`. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **`settings:classes` did not exist; it is new, granted to the Asset Controller AND the Asset Manager.** `[ruled]` by the Builder — the handoff said "already granted to the role"; no scope of that name existed anywhere. It changes a class's `identification_mode` and schema and nothing else (any other key from a `settings:classes`-only caller is a 403 naming `settings:write`); `settings:write` still changes everything. The Manager holds it too so the categories sidebar item can declare ONE scope (`settings:classes`) and be drawn for both people the server answers — a nav item's scope is all-of. `PATCH /asset-categories/<id>` checks the either-or in the route (the decorator's list is all-of). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **"The configuration record" is `ir.config_parameter`, so a class's mode and an exclusion write one there.** `[ruled]` by the Builder: the configuration-change report reads that table and nothing else (`audit_configuration.py`), and the handoff assumed a record of category and table changes that does not exist. A change of a class's mode or schema writes `cadra.class_identification.<category id>` (`<mode>; mandatory: …; class: <name>`), an exclusion's create or change writes `cadra.role_exclusion.<id>` (`on|off; A × B; reason`) — the row the report shows, with the person and the time. Nothing reads them back; the model fields are the truth. The alternative — a second source in the report — mixes two id sequences under one cursor and was not worth it for last-write-only rows. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Rule 2, the role-exclusion table: Store is the Asset Officer, not Asset User.** `[ruled]` by the Builder against §5's "Store (Asset User / Asset Officer)": every gate role IMPLIES Asset User (`asset_security.xml`), and handoff 41 RULED that a human tagger holds Asset User beside the Tag-Issuing Unit — an exclusion on Asset User would forbid every gate role to everyone who holds one. The Officer is the role that holds `assets:write`, i.e. fires the receipt gate. Eight seeded rows (`data/role_exclusions.xml`, noupdate): Officer × {IAD officer, IAD approver, Asset Controller, Tag-Issuing Unit, Register Maker, Register Checker}, Maker × Checker, IAD officer × IAD approver. Judged on EFFECTIVE membership, so a Manager (who implies the Officer) is refused the same pairs. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Rule 2 holds for every writer, not only the API — with two exceptions, named.** `[ruled]` by the Builder: a combination of roles is wrong whoever grants it, so the back office meets the refusal too (`AccessError`, the reason in words). Outside it: an API credential's user (its groups are derived mechanically from its scopes — refusing them would fail every scope edit; the no-two-gates rule still holds per unit) and module install/upgrade (`registry.ready` false — an upgrade must never be refused by a table an owner edited). Only a pair the write CREATES is refused; an existing pair is listed (`GET /settings/conflicting-grants`), never revoked. Measured before the seed: 0 people on 7 of 9 local tenants and on `staging` (0 of 17); 16 of 665 on `test19` and 10 of 37 on `boot44`, every one an INACTIVE e2e fixture holding Officer + Tag-Issuing Unit (spec 42's officer). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **The verification pass (a fresh agent) found three defects before they shipped; all fixed with a test each.** `[landed]` (1) Rule 2 refused a NEW API credential: `_provision_client_user` creates the user before the client row that names it, so on a fresh database `bootstrap.py`'s every-scope smoke client would have been judged a person and refused — the guard now also leaves out the `cadra_client_` login prefix. (2) `PATCH /asset-categories/<id>` checked its either-or scope after existence and the body (a caller with no settings scope got 404/400, and spec 46's "hidden ⇒ refused" broke for the Asset Controller), and its `scopes=[]` published it in `openapi.json` as needing no token — the check is first now and the spec entry is overridden (`security: bearerAuth`, `x-cadra-scopes-any-of`). (3) P5 was open at creation: a gated receipt could arrive `state: disposed`, be walked to the register and send SAP a master for a write-off — refused now; the four test fixtures that relied on it dispose AFTER registration (`gates.disposed_on_the_register`). Also fixed: a discrepancy could never close (correcting the entered number to SAP's now closes it as `confirmed`); the discrepancy's chatter post could escape the INT-06 drain and re-send the master (savepoint + catch); a mode-only category change re-derived depreciation for the whole class; a non-string role key answered 500; a repeat affix of a `tag_id` unit was not idempotent; the test fixture pinned a class's default schema (`class_attributes_schema: null` now resets it).
- 2026-10-12 · **Left by the verification pass, each with its reason.** `[ruled]` by the Builder. (a) Rule 2 does not judge a group IMPLICATION edit in the back office (making Maker imply Checker would give every Maker the pair) — the API already refuses implication changes outright (handoff 42), and in the back office it is a developer's act on the security model, not a grant. (b) The SAP discrepancy is held in two overwritable fields and the chatter, not an append-only `nrs.asset.event` — a new event type touches the history's kinds and four guard tables; recorded as a follow-up, GO-LIVE §29. (c) After a discrepancy, INT-09 lifecycle events still carry the maker's number, which SAP has said it does not hold — resolve the discrepancy before disposing such a unit; GO-LIVE §29. (d) A back-office *Duplicate* of a candidate yields a registered asset with no gate events (`register_state` is `copy=False`, default `registered`) — pre-existing from 57, the back office is an administrator's tool since handoff 45; GO-LIVE §29. (e) `GET /meta/readiness` answers without the envelope's `meta` block — deliberate, it is read by a person with `curl`.
- 2026-10-12 · **The browser suite has its own one helper, `e2e/fixtures/gates.ts`**, mirroring `tests/gates.py` (`receive`, `onTheRegister`, `readyToAffix`), and `ApiClient.createAsset` goes through it; it fails, never skips, when the gates are on and the personas file is missing. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Two app defects found by running the suite with the gates on, both fixed.** `[landed]` (1) The *Create person* dialog grew past a 720px window once handoff 57 brought the grantable roles to ten — Create sat below the edge with nothing to scroll (spec 43, "element is outside of the viewport"); it scrolls inside the viewport now. (2) The asset form had no colour, material or dimensions inputs, so with the gates on a `tag_id` unit (furniture) could be received only over INT-03, never in the app; the three inputs are on the form, beside make and model.

## 2026-10-12 — handoff 59 (what SAP already holds)

- 2026-10-12 · **A registration is refused at BOTH gates when its class has no SAP asset class.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §1. `nrs.asset._assert_sap_class`, called from `_gate_validate` for `register` (on the class the maker CONFIRMS in `asset_class`) and `approve-registration` (on the unit's class, which can have been cleared in between) — before anything is written, the sentence verbatim: *"<Category> has no SAP asset class, so SAP would reject this asset. An Asset Controller sets it under Settings → Asset Categories."* (400 `validation_failed`, `details.field = sap_asset_class`). With the gates off nothing is checked. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **INT-06 with the gates OFF still sends a blank class as null, and should not — but not in this handoff.** `[ruled]` by the Builder, asked by §1. Today: on affix, `_sap_master_payload` (`myservice_workflow/models/sap_asset_events.py`) sends `asset_class.sap_asset_class = null` for a class with none (requirements §5.1 — never guessed); the mock accepts and echoes a default, a strict SAP answers 422 (`mock_sap.py --mode reject`), and the dispatch row retries and is abandoned (`sap_posting_state = failed`, the dual-code state then `exception`). It SHOULD be held — a master Cadra knows SAP will refuse is a retry storm and a dead letter, not a delivery — released when the class is set. Not built: §5 requires gates-off responses and behaviour unchanged, and a hold changes INT-06 on every gates-off tenant; with the gates on, the refusal above means a unit with no class never reaches INT-06. Owner: us, before any gates-off tenant is pointed at the real SAP endpoint (GO-LIVE §29 item 5). `[open]`
- 2026-10-12 · **The SAP class is four digits, validated where it is WRITTEN, recorded as `cadra.class_sap_asset_class.<id>`.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §1. All ten classes in NRS's October export are four digits. Not a constraint over every row: `nrs19`'s *Desktop / Laptop Computer* carries `3100` (four digits, but NOT one of the export's ten — set 2026-09-21, flagged in report 59) and a stored value of another shape must stay readable until somebody edits it. Settable by `settings:classes` (the Asset Controller, the Asset Manager) or `settings:write`; the Store Officer is refused 403. The ten codes are a HINT on the screen, not a closed list — NRS may have classes the export did not use. The configuration record gets its own key (not folded into `cadra.class_identification`) so the report says which of the two settings changed. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **The class mapping is NRS's, so the proposal is in report 59 and nowhere else.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §1. No category on any tenant was given a SAP class by this handoff (the test fixtures `pytest-59 …` on `test19` aside, and the HTTP suite's walk, which gives a `test19` class with none `3000` as the Asset Controller — a test database). A wrong class books an asset to the wrong account; once INT-06 is live that is a finance error. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **`record_only`: the fourth identification mode, for what nothing can be fixed to.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §2. Label *"Identified by its SAP record — no physical label (land, buildings, software, works in progress)"*. At a gated receipt: a serial is REFUSED whoever sends it (Cadra does not depend on Vendra's follow-up), the class's attributes are required — default `name` + **`reference`**, a new `nrs.asset` field (writable, published; no existing field fit: `import_ref` is the importer's, `legacy_asset_code` is an old code). In the chain: the ONE `TRANSITIONS` table gains rows limited by `modes` (`request-tagging`, the labelled `register` and `return-registration` are `modes=_LABELLED`; a `record_only` `register` from `reg_ready` and `return-registration` to `reg_ready`); IAD's approval fires the system row `skip-tagging` (`vfm_approved → reg_ready`) whose event has NO `gate` — skipped, not fired, so nobody is recorded as having tagged it and the no-two-gates rule counts nothing — and the reason *"no label: <class> is identified by its record"*. Five people, not seven. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **A new register state `reg_ready`, not `tagged`.** `[ruled]` by the Builder: §2 says "straight to waiting on the maker", and the maker's state was `tagged` — reusing it would publish *Tagged* on a plot of land. `reg_ready` (*Ready for registration — no label*) waits on `maker`, so `waiting_on` never says `controller` or `tagging` for such a unit and the published `waiting_on` set is unchanged (seven). Additive enum value on `register_state` (CHANGELOG). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **No label is ever minted for a `record_only` unit — and with the gates OFF nothing changes.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §2, §5. `_generate_tag` refuses a unit whose received mode is `record_only` (after the candidate early return, so the receipt's own create path is untouched): the back-office station's Generate and a replacement cannot mint one either. The mode is stamped only at a GATED receipt (58's rule, AD-31), so with the switch off a unit of a `record_only` class is created exactly as before — label minted at creation — because §5 says gates-off is unchanged. Turning the gates on is what makes the class label-less. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Seed nothing to `record_only`.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §2. Measured by the upgrade's own line: **0 of 381 categories on 10 tenants** (9 local + `staging`) have a name suggesting land, buildings, software, licences, works in progress, construction or projects — Cadra holds nothing today that maps to SAP 3500, 6000, 8000, 9000 or 9300. Which categories are which is the §1 conversation with NRS. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Placeholder serials are a tenant list, one parameter, refused before the uniqueness check.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §3. `cadra.serial_placeholders`, JSON `{"exact", "patterns"}`, seeded by `data/serial_placeholders.xml` (noupdate) with the handoff's two patterns and eleven values; absent or unreadable means the seed (never an empty list, which would quietly accept every placeholder). Compared stripped, upper-cased, internal spaces collapsed; patterns case-insensitive. `GET` (`settings:read`) / `PUT` (`settings:write`) `/api/v1/settings/serial-placeholders`, the whole list each time; one row on the configuration record. At a gated receipt a placeholder is refused *"'XXXX' is a placeholder, not a serial number. Capture the serial printed on the item."* BEFORE the attributes and the uniqueness check. A `tag_id` unit carrying one is refused too (the handoff names `serial` mode; a placeholder is not a serial in either mode and storing one through the gates would seed the next collision) with a sentence that says it may come without one. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **"The uniqueness check ignores existing placeholders" holds by construction, and the code says so anyway.** `[ruled]` by the Builder. The check is reached only by a serial that is NOT a placeholder, and two values equal stripped and case-insensitively are placeholder-or-not together — so a legacy placeholder cannot be the clash today. The SQL rows are filtered through the list regardless, so a future change of comparison (collapsing spaces) cannot bring it back. The first draft loaded the list AFTER executing the clash query on the same cursor and fetched an empty result — every duplicate accepted; caught by its own test before it shipped. Proved on `nrs19` read-only (report 59). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Legacy placeholders measured, not cleaned: 0 of 25,659 serials on 10 tenants.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §3 — GO-LIVE §29 item 8 stays NRS's. SAP's export: 33,092 of 80,128 active serials under the seeded list (the reference doc says 33,096; the 4 are in class 3000 and its definition is not stated). `nrs19` holds near-misses the list does not catch — `N\A` ×3 and three-letter status codes (`DEL` ×53, `TOG` ×44, `BAD` ×13, `TRF` ×2) typed into the serial field; the list is the tenant's to extend on the screen. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **SAP asset numbers: both shapes accepted, stored and compared stripped and upper-cased.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §4. Measured before: the field is an unbounded `Char`, the maker's form is free text, and both the gate's clash check and `_check_sap_asset_number_unique` compared EXACTLY — so both shapes were accepted and `abc123` / `ABC123` were two SAP assets. Now the `register` gate stores the code upper-cased, its clash check and the model constraint compare `upper(btrim(…))` (raw SQL, candidates included; the constraint keeps its company-code + sub-number key). Measured on every local tenant before the change: 0 SAP numbers shared once normalised. No format is REFUSED: SAP's legacy codes carry `/` (3,299), `&` (97), `\` (73) and internal spaces (4), lengths 7–12 — a pattern would refuse SAP's own numbers. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **The class-digit warning knows class 3500.** `[ruled]` by the Builder, on the export: 146,165 of 146,224 eleven-digit numbers start with their class's first digit; the other 59 are every numeric number of class 3500, which start `5`. `SAP_NUMBER_FIRST_DIGIT = {'3500': '5'}`, and the sentence names the class a digit says (`5` → 3500, `9` → "9000 or 9300"). Returned as `data._warnings.sap_asset_number` on `register` and `approve-registration`, and kept on the `register` event's evidence — a warning, never a refusal. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **SAP sub-numbers are out of scope.** `[ruled]` handoff 59 §4: 53 rows of 211,699 carry one (5 main numbers appear twice because of it). The model already has `sap_asset_subnumber` and the uniqueness key includes it; the maker's form does not ask for one. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **Two changes reach gates-off tenants, against the letter of §5, on purpose.** `[ruled]` by the Builder, flagged by the verification pass. (a) SAP asset numbers unique once stripped and upper-cased, in the model rule every writer meets — §4 asks for the normalisation, and a uniqueness rule that only the gate obeyed would let INT-07 or an import create the pair the gate refuses; 0 such pairs on any local tenant. (b) A category's `sap_asset_class` is four digits from every writer — a value the gate then sends to SAP should not depend on which door typed it; 0 categories on 10 tenants hold another shape. Both are announced in the CHANGELOG as BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, every tenant. Reverse either by narrowing the check to the gate, if the Architect rules §5's letter. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-12 · **The verification pass (a fresh agent) found four defects; all fixed with a test.** `[landed]` (1) The new `PUT /settings/serial-placeholders` was missing from the published-names table, so `myservice_api`'s guard would have gone red. (2) A registered `record_only` unit would have sat in the station's *untagged* list for ever, and a batch *Generate* containing one would have failed whole (the `_generate_tag` refusal uncaught in the loop, rolling back every label minted before it) — it is now left out of the list and the count, and SKIPPED in a batch with `reason: record_only`. (3) The receipt rules guarded `create` only: a `PATCH` on a candidate could put a serial on a `record_only` unit, a placeholder on any, or clear its `reference` — `nrs.asset.write` now applies the same rules to a unit still in the chain whose mode was stamped at receipt. (4) The placeholder list could be saved empty (every placeholder then accepted) or with `.*` (every serial then refused) — both refused, and an unreadable stored list now reads `configured: false`. Left, with reasons: 58's serial-UNIQUENESS check is still receipt-only (a `PATCH` can give a candidate a duplicate serial — pre-existing, GO-LIVE §29); INT-06's master carries no `reference`, so SAP gets a land master with only its description (a payload change belongs with the real endpoint, §29 item 5); a registered `record_only` asset reads `is_tagged: false` for ever, which is true.

## 2026-10-15 — handoff 60 (seventy a day)

- 2026-10-15 · **A batch is N single-unit fires — the rule, held in the code's shape, not in a comment.** `[landed]` The four batch routes (`POST /candidates/gates/{verify,approve,register,approve-registration}`) call `nrs.asset._fire_gate` once per unit, each in its own savepoint inside one outer savepoint, with the same payload a single route builds. So every check a single fire makes — state, scope, the no-two-gates rule, the placeholder and SAP-class checks — runs per unit, every unit writes its own `gate_fired` event, and a refused unit rolls back its own savepoint and nothing else. The refusal of a unit is mapped by `utils.gate_refusal_parts`, the same function the single routes' envelope is built from, so a unit refused in a batch carries the status, code and sentence it would have had alone (the HTTP test fires the barred unit alone and compares the sentences). What is checked ONCE is what every unit shares and nothing about a unit: the checklist and the evidence (by `_validate_gate_evidence`, which is `_gate_validate('verify')`, so the words are the single route's), and the maker's cost centre and date. A batch that cannot be read is the envelope's 400 before anything is written; a batch that can is always 200 with a line per unit. Handoff 60 §2.
- 2026-10-15 · **A dry run is the batch run inside a savepoint that is rolled back — not a separate validator.** `[landed]` The maker's paste is previewed with `dry_run: true`, which runs every line for real and rolls the outer savepoint back. That is the only way the preview can see what the real run will: a SAP code duplicated INSIDE the paste is refused on its second line because the first line's write is visible to the uniqueness check — a validator judging each line against the database alone would have previewed both as fine. The flushing savepoint clears the cache on rollback (`sql_db._FlushingSavepoint.rollback`), so nothing the dry run wrote is read back afterwards. Handoff 60 §2d, the same pattern as handoff 42's role-change preview.
- 2026-10-15 · **Verification evidence is stored once and LINKED from each unit's event; the batch is told nothing it does not need.** `[landed]` One delivery note for twenty laptops is one `ir.attachment`, whose id every fired unit's `verify` event carries in `attachment_ids` — the shape a single verify writes, so the history of a batched unit reads like a single fire (§4.4, pinned by comparing the two events' keys). It is attached to the first unit awaiting verification; the evidence route reads the EVENT, not `res_id`, so every unit finds it. If no unit fires, the stored files are deleted: nothing would link them. A batch mixing GRNs is refused whole, a unit with no GRN counting as a receipt of its own — nothing says it shares a delivery note with anything. The event does not say "batch": §4.4 asks that the unit's history read like a single fire, and the actor, the checklist and the evidence are the same facts either way. Handoff 60 §2b.
- 2026-10-15 · **`waiting on me` and the badge are one query, and it is judged as the PERSON.** `[landed]` `nrs.asset._waiting_on_me_domain` reads `WAITING_ACT` (the act each waiting state needs, its gate and scopes — pinned against `TRANSITIONS` by `test_waiting_act_matches_the_table`) and, per state the person holds the scope for, removes the units on which they fired a DIFFERENT gate — the no-two-gates test `_two_gates_refusal` makes per unit, made as one SQL query per state. The badge is that list's `total_estimate` at `limit=1`, the approvals badge's cheap count. A person holding every scope is barred from everything they touched: the suite credential, having verified 17 units and received 3, waits on none of the 20 at approval (the HTTP test asserts exactly that). Queries, rejections and returns are not what a unit waits for, so they are not counted. Handoff 60 §2a.
- 2026-10-15 · **Oldest first needs a column: `register_state_since`.** `[landed]` `gates.state_since` was computed per row from the events, which cannot be sorted on or paged by. The stored, indexed column is set at creation and by `_gate_move`, nowhere else, and the 19.0.1.29.0 migration filled it on every candidate from its last gate event (registered rows are left empty: they never waited on anybody and inventing an instant for them is a claim the record cannot back). `paginate` gained an ascending keyset (`keyset_field`) — value then id, both in the cursor, a cursor from the other walk a 400 — because the id-only keyset cannot page a list ordered by anything else. `sort=newest` stays the default: the published order does not change; the app asks for `oldest`. Handoff 60 §2a.
- 2026-10-15 · **The maker's submission is published as `gates.registration`, not under `sap`.** `[landed]` The checker needs SAP code, cost, cost centre and class on the row; the cost centre and the maker's capitalisation date were published nowhere, and `sap.capitalisation_date` is SAP's own date (`sap_capitalisation_date`, written by INT-07), a different field. Publishing the maker's date under the same name would be the one-name defect ruling 7 is about. `null` until the `register` gate writes `cost_centre`, the field nothing else writes. Handoff 60 §2d.
- 2026-10-15 · **Ruling 7 landed: `reference` is `record_reference`, the model field and the published key together.** `[landed]` BREAKING, announced; no client reads it. The pre-migration renames the column, its `ir.model.fields` row and external id, and rewrites a class schema naming `reference` among its mandatory attributes. **One loss, on a test database, by my ordering:** `test19` had already taken 19.0.1.29.0 (for the new column) when the rename was added to the same version, so its pre-migration never ran there and Odoo dropped the old column with the field — 23 values, every one on a `record_only` fixture created by handoff 59's suites on 12 October (23 of 23 `is_test_fixture`). Every other tenant was still at 19.0.1.28.0 and carries its values over (the fan-out prints the count). Lesson: a rename goes in its own version, or before the first upgrade of the version it ships in. Handoff 60 ruling 7.
- 2026-10-15 · **A new asset carries its class's identification mode, gates on or off — stamped, not checked.** `[landed]` The mode was stamped only by `_check_receipt_identity`, which runs only for a gated receipt, so a gates-off chair published `null` (Vendra report 54 §5.3, asset 12016 on `nrs19`). `create` now stamps `category.identification_mode or 'serial'` when nothing else did. None of the receipt rules is applied — nothing is refused with the gates off that was not before (§3). Two consequences are the mode's own and are announced: a new `tag_id` unit is affixed with the attributes tick; a new `record_only` unit is minted no label at creation (`asset_tag.create` skips it; `_generate_tag` refuses one, which until now would have failed the creation). Records created before keep what they had: not a computed field, because AD-31 fixes the mode at receipt and a recompute would rewrite it when a class changes. Handoff 60 §1.2.
- 2026-10-15 · **Vendra's credential gains `candidates:read` by migration, the merge being the owner's authorisation.** `[landed]` `myservice_api` 19.0.3.32.0 adds it to every client named `Vendra (INT-03)` or with client id `vendra` (`=ilike`, as 19.0.3.3.0 does), archived ones included, through the ORM — so the client's user is re-provisioned to `groups_for_scopes` as on any scope change (rule 7), which moves the `nrs19` and `boot44` Vendra users off the pre-handoff-47 Asset Manager group onto Asset Officer, what `assets:write` grants today. Existing scopes are kept (`nrs19`'s hand-added `locations:read` included); `bootstrap.ensure_vendra_client` grants the same list, so a later bootstrap does not narrow it away — though a bootstrap run on `nrs19` would still strip `locations:read`, which is not in its list (pre-existing; recorded, not changed). Vendra must call `/auth/token` again: a client refresh family keeps the scopes it was issued with. Handoff 60 §1.1.
- 2026-10-15 · **INT-05 from `test19` was configuration: `test19` had no event target at all.** `[landed]` Every affix there logged *"no asset-event target configured on this database; asset.tag.affixed … not queued"* — 1,402 such lines — and wrote no dispatch row. `nrs19` has one and has delivered 29 `asset.tag.affixed` acknowledged. `test19` now has the same target (`http://vendra.network:8080`, the bench's own endpoint and bearer token, which is the same for every Cadra tenant the bench pairs with). One consequence, measured in report 60: every affix on `test19` — the suites' included — now queues an event to the bench, because INT-05 sends every affix, not only Vendra's units. Handoff 60 §1.3.
- 2026-10-15 · **The tagger reads the asset code off the label, not the screen — where the screen still shows it is said, not hidden everywhere.** `[landed]` Ruling on report 59's note: the dialog still never pre-fills the number, the Candidates `tagging` rows and the station's *Labels to fix* panel do not show the code, and the station card's *Asset code* field is hidden (not only blurred) while the confirm dialog is open. Report 60 lists every other place that still shows it — the Anybody and other gate tabs, the asset page, the station's recent-tags and untagged tables, the printed label itself. Handoff 60 §2c.
- 2026-10-15 · **The verification pass (a fresh agent) found ten defects; all fixed.** `[landed]` The weightiest: with the gates off a `record_only` unit — now stamped and minted no label — would never have sent its INT-06 master, which goes out on affix; it now goes at creation. Then: the evidence file's header (Odoo's RFC 6266 `content_disposition`); `register_state_since` truncated to the second because the cursor compares seconds (`test19`'s 716 sub-second values truncated by the same SQL; 0 elsewhere); a tampered cursor a 400; the stored evidence re-homed on the first unit verified; the paste's empty line and `=ilike` wildcards; the approver's checklist in the verifier's words; `state_since` published from the column it is sorted by; the endpoint reference's old name. It confirmed the batch rule held in every path. Left for the Architect, in report 60: who may read the evidence (`candidates:read` today), and four gates-off changes a user can see that each follow from something the handoff asked for.

## 2026-10-15 — handoff 61 (the same code, and the photo)

- 2026-10-15 · **D1, ruled (R2a): a code is *assigned at capture or creation, printed at tagging, active at registration, never reused*.** `[ruled]` by the Architect with the owner, on findings §7.1. The built behaviour is the ruling: `nrs.asset.create` assigns the asset code to every unit, gates on or off, and a candidate's code is hidden while it is a candidate (handoff 60 §2c). "Reserved at tagging request" in NRS's D1 row (`docs/reference/addendum-a-verification-gated-receipt.md`) is superseded by an appended note, NRS's text left as it was. The code ledger that comes with device code blocks (handoff 63, R2) records receipt codes as well as device codes, so "which codes exist" has one answer. Nothing built here. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **A replacement reprints under the same number (R3) — one row is one physical print.** `[landed]` `nrs.asset.tag.label` gains `reprint_count` (0 for a first print), and on the VOIDED row `voided_date` and `void_reason` (a stamp drawn from `replacement_reason`'s values, because that field stays editable on the form). `action_approve_replacement` writes the old row `state`/`label_state` `scrapped` with the void stamped, THEN creates a row with the same `name`, `replaces_id` set, `source` copied and `reprint_count` one higher — so `_check_unique_active` still sees one active row per number. `asset_tag` and `active_tag_number` do not change. `_generate_tag` is no longer called by the approval, which closes findings §5.6 (on a gated unit the replacement had been minted under a location prefix instead of the asset code, against ruling 4.3) and applies the same rule to location-prefix labels: the number on the asset does not change because a sticker faded. The handoff-46 comment in `_generate_tag` — reissuing a number "would make the replacement history ambiguous, two physical labels sharing one code" — is answered by the print count and the void recorded on each row; `_generate_tag` still never reissues a number for a NEW label. The new stamps are refused on `write` and on `create` without the approval's context. A number active on TWO assets (84 such numbers today: 68 `test19`, 15 `jaizbankplc`, 1 `nrs19`) is refused a reprint, which would print the other asset's label too. Approver unchanged (an Asset Manager, never the requester — handoff 38). The barcode station's Generate no longer lists or mints for an asset whose label is pending replacement (`physical_tag_id`, not `active_tag_id`), which was §5.6 by another door.
- 2026-10-15 · **A voided print cannot be told from its reprint by any scanner — so the check is in time, and the app now records its prints.** `[landed]` (Builder's deviation, with evidence.) The two prints encode the same value. The one case the server can reason about: a reprint still `issued` while the voided print is on the asset — a matching scan then can only be the voided label, and is refused (`voided_print`, recorded as `affix_refused` before the refusal) naming the void, its date and reason. That needs a print to be RECORDED, and the app draws labels in the browser and never reached the PDF funnel: 1 of 3,069 affixed labels on the 8 tenants had a `printed_date`. So `POST /api/v1/tags/printed` (`tagging:write` — not `assets:read`, which the Auditor holds and the Auditor writes nothing) stamps the app's prints through `_mark_printed`'s own rule. Consequence, announced: a label printed at the station reads `printed` before it is affixed, gates on or off — a read change §10 did not list. What printing `·R1` beside the code (outside the encoded value — handoff 55 §3) would add is human legibility only; not built, because four of the five app print paths carry no count, and marking some reprints and not others would make an unmarked reprint read as an original (report 61).
- 2026-10-15 · **IAD's evidence has its own scope, `evidence:read`, behind one guard.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (ruling 1 on report 60); scope name and placement by the Builder. Held by the IAD officer and approver, the Register Maker and Checker, and the Auditor (`GROUP_SCOPES`); `SCOPE_GROUPS` maps it to the Auditor — the one read-only role among the five — so a credential granted it never inherits a gate role's exclusions. NOT held by Asset User, nor by Vendra's credential (`assets:read assets:write candidates:read`). `utils.evidence_readable()` is the guard and `utils.evidence_count_only()` the answer to everybody else (*"IAD evidence: N items"*); it fronts `GET /assets/<id>/evidence`, its file route (`require_jwt` with the scope), the photo list's evidence items, and the asset history's verify events (attachment ids replaced by the count; the checklist stays — it is the record of what was attested, not the papers). Ends `:read`, so the Auditor-is-read-only pin holds. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **The last two two-click gates have batches: *Submit selected* and *Request tagging for selected*.** `[ruled]` ruling 5 on report 60. `POST /candidates/gates/submit` and `/request-tagging`, through `_simple_batch` — N single fires under 60's rules, `dry_run`, a mixed-receipt selection allowed for these two. The tag request stays the Asset Controller's act (AD-17); nothing fires it on IAD's approval. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **`GET /asset-categories`: one grouped count per counted field, not one per row.** `[ruled]` ruling 6 on report 60. `asset_count` and `asset_depreciating_count` compute with `_read_group` over the recordset (which goes through `nrs.asset._search`, so the register's candidate filter applies exactly as `search_count` did). `test19`, 160 categories: 2.8–4.2 s before (8.0 cold), 0.27–0.52 s after (`docs/handoffs/61-evidence/asset-categories-timing.txt`). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **`test19`'s INT-05 target and its copied bearer key stay — on local test tenants only.** `[ruled]` ruling 7 on report 60; recorded in `docs/ENVIRONMENT-FACTS.md` so the pattern is not copied to a cluster tenant. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **A candidate is not verified, marked missing or found, or given any other status (R10).** `[ruled]`. `_assert_on_the_register` (under every ORM writer) refuses a change of `state` and of `last_verified_date` on a unit not on the register, beside 58's custodian, move and disposal; the four routes ask first (`_on_the_register_or_409`) so a candidate is told what it is rather than "not missing". The `assets:write` routes checked, and what each does on a candidate: `GET /approvals/approvers` (a read), `POST /approvals/<id>/withdraw` and `/renominate` (a change request cannot exist on a candidate: its `create` is refused since 58), `POST /assets` (creation), `PATCH /assets/<id>` (receipt corrections allowed; custodian, location, status, verification date, disposal refused), `/move` and `/transfer-custody` (58), `/verify`, `/mark-missing`, `/mark-found` (61), `/condemn` (58, now asked first), `/gates/submit` and `/gates/resubmit` and the submit batch (the candidate's own path), photo upload (allowed, §5). `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **The default `tag_id` schema drops make and model (R9).** `[ruled]`. `DEFAULT_MANDATORY['tag_id'] = (name, colour, material, dimensions)`. Measured first: 13 `tag_id` classes on the 8 local tenants, 13 on the default (stored empty) and 0 customised — so all 13 change and no stored schema is touched. Vendra's own make-and-model question at its receipt is Vendra's rule (its handoff 54) and stays. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **Photos on an asset: append-only, 300 KB, 12 standing, JPEG/PNG by the bytes.** `[landed]` `nrs.asset.photo` inherits `nrs.asset.append.only.mixin` (the fifth thing that pins an asset; `asset_id` restrict). Caps from AI-NFR-07, which gives a size ("compressed to a maximum of 300 KB each on the device before sync") and no count — so 300 KB (307,200 bytes) is the server's cap and 12 per asset is the handoff's fallback, counting only photos not superseded (nothing is ever deleted, so a superseded photo frees its slot). Type judged by sniffing (magic number, then Pillow opens it as that format); a 50-megapixel header ceiling added by the Builder so a tiny file cannot declare a huge picture. Bytes are `Binary(attachment=True)` written with `image_no_postprocess` — without it Odoo re-encodes any image over 1920 px and the photo comes back as different bytes (the same flag now on IAD's evidence store, which had the same latent defect: 0 image attachments on any tenant so far). Superseding is an `nrs.asset.event` of type `photo_superseded` (`photo_id`, `reason`, the actor); the photo row is never touched. `source` is the channel the caller declares (`web` | `mobile`), not the credential relabel; the test label is `is_test_fixture`. The web app re-encodes a picture to ≤ 300 KB JPEG in the browser before upload, as the phone must. The multipart body is parsed once, in `utils.get_multipart`, inside the envelope — idempotency and the unknown-field refusal as for JSON. `[landed]`
- 2026-10-15 · **`updated_since`: a sync filter, and how to page with it — no cursor needed.** `[landed]` `write_date >= the instant`; the next sync starts at the first page's `meta.timestamp` minus a margin, so a row changed behind the cursor mid-sync is read next time (driven in `tests/test_the_same_code_and_the_photo.py`). A change to a related record (a photo, a custody row) does not move `updated_at`; said in the mobile team's map. Parsed in `v1_assets` because the query-parameter door imports nothing; waived there by name.
- 2026-10-15 · **Room codes (R8): `LOC-` + the hierarchy's zone codes, or the id; stable once stored.** `[landed]` `generic.location.location_code`, unique tenant-wide archived rows included (`models.Constraint` plus a Python check that refuses in words first), `[A-Z0-9-]`, ≤ 24 characters, always beginning `LOC-` so it can never equal an asset code or a tag number (0 of either begins so on 8 tenants; a typed code equal to one is refused too). Generated from the zone codes of the location and its ancestors where they exist (510 of 4,869 locations), else ending in the id — names are not abbreviated, because an invented abbreviation is not the hierarchy. A code does not move when the room moves (printed labels keep scanning); empty on edit means regenerate. Filled on every tenant by the 19.0.1.30.0 migration and by the install hook: 4,869 of 4,869, 0 collisions. The scan box tries a room code LAST, so nothing that resolved before resolves differently; `/tags/lookup` (the public portal's shape) is unchanged. Room labels print at medium and large only — and the medium Code 128 reads 0 of 24 in handoff 54's corpus as well, so a medium room label is read by its QR (GO-LIVE §30.2). Room labels are not asset labels and take no print-funnel record.
- 2026-10-15 · **Hosting is a known deviation from AI-NFR-04 (R7) — GO-LIVE §30.1.** `[ruled]` by the owner: the current servers for now; NRS accepts in writing before the first production photo. Nothing in the repository names the demo cluster's region (searched, report 61). `[open]`

## 2026-10-15 — handoff 62 (the state, told)

- 2026-10-15 · **Cadra 62 is the register-state event; the SAP mirror becomes 63 and the audit-capture path 64.** `[ruled]` by the Architect in handoff 62: it closes the gated receipt loop go-live needs, which the AI work does not. `docs/AI-CAPTURE-EVALUATION.md` §4 renumbered in the handoff's own docs commit. `[landed ca742d9]`
- 2026-10-15 · **`asset.register_state.changed` is built to Vendra report 56 §4, on INT-05's road, raised in `_gate_move`.** `[ruled]` by the Architect. The claim that `_gate_move` is the only writer was VERIFIED before building on it (CLAUDE.md's rule): `write` refuses `register_state` without `_GATE_WRITE_KEY`, which only `_gate_move` sets; `create` sets a birth state (`captured` or `registered`), not a change; the 19.0.1.26.0 migration's one-off `NULL → registered` backfill is history; no raw-SQL writer in first-party code, scripts or tests. `_gate_move`'s four callers cover every gate, query/reject/resubmit/return, the label issue, the affix and the `record_only` skip. `[landed 8c9f45e]`
- 2026-10-15 · **One event per ACT, for the state the act left — not one per internal move.** `[landed 8c9f45e]` (Builder's judgement, said in report 62.) Two acts move a unit twice in one call: `request-tagging` passes `tag_requested` on the way to `tag_issued`, and IAD's approval of a `record_only` unit passes `vfm_approved` on the way to `reg_ready`. Nobody can ever see a unit in the state it passed through, and `vfm_approved` there would tell Vendra the unit waits on the Asset Controller when it waits on the maker. So `_fire_gate` holds its inner moves back (context `cadra_register_state_one_act`) and raises one event when it returns; a `_gate_move` outside `_fire_gate` (the affix) is its own act. Seven events for a labelled unit's seven gates, five for a `record_only` unit's five, the counts the handoff expected. Sending every move instead is one line (drop the deferral) and adds `tag_requested` to the contract.
- 2026-10-15 · **`register_state_since` strictly increases per unit: a move in the same second as the last carries the last value plus one second.** `[landed 8c9f45e]` The handoff asked whether two moves can land in the same second: a datetime column holds whole seconds, and an in-registry walk lands ALL SEVEN in one second (measured with the guard removed — `62-evidence/t1-mut.txt`). Vendra keeps the newest by this value, so equal values would leave it to guess. The column, not a separate event clock, because `state_since` is published as the column (60) and the Candidates list sorts on it; the cost — a value up to a second or two ahead of the clock — is said in the contract.
- 2026-10-15 · **Only units Vendra sent: `import_source = vendra` AND a non-empty `grn_reference`.** `[ruled]` (the handoff's definition). 8 of 18 `vendra`-sourced candidates on `test19` carried no GRN (measured before the drive); they raise nothing, because Vendra has nothing to match them to.
- 2026-10-15 · **The event has its own endpoint on the INT-05 target, and a blank one queues nothing.** `[landed 8c9f45e]` (Builder's design.) `workflow.config.rest_endpoint_register_state`, routed in `_endpoint_for_event`; the body carries no `event` key (Vendra's table, no more), so the queue passes the row's `event_type` to the adapter for a body that does not name itself — every existing event is called exactly as before (`asset.tag.affixed` pinned by a test with the old three-argument adapter). Blank means not queued, rather than queued to the tag endpoint or dead-lettered: Vendra answers `401` for any path its path-scoped auth hook does not cover (measured; Frappe's `validate_auth` refuses an unauthenticated `Authorization` header before routing, `frappe/auth.py:627-628`), which is final on the first attempt, so queueing before Vendra's receiver AND its hook entry exist would dead-letter every move. Deploy order: Cadra, then Vendra 57 with the path in its `auth_hooks`, then the field. (First written as "a method it does not know"; corrected by the verification pass.)
- 2026-10-15 · **A day without Vendra is measured on the real ladder, and dead letters are retried by the selection.** `[landed 8c9f45e]` The default ladder (8 attempts, 60 s doubling, 3,600 s cap) gives up 2 h 3 min after an event; a working day's 490 events during a 24-hour outage are 490 dead, 0 late (`TestADayWithoutVendra`). Until 62 a dead letter could be re-sent only from its own form; the dispatch log's list now has *Retry Delivery* on a selection and a *Register-State Events* filter. Whether to lengthen the ladder for this event (30 attempts cover a day) is for the Architect — GO-LIVE §31.1. `[open]`
- 2026-10-15 · **`scripts/walk_units.py`: the one supported way to walk a unit outside the suites, local test tenants only.** `[landed 8c9f45e]` As the demo personas over HTTP, through `tests/gates.py` — which now imports without pytest (`pytest.fail` inside a pytest run, `GateWalkError` outside) and gains `walk_to`, `query`, `reject` — so there is one walker. Refuses, before touching a file or the network, any tenant not in `ALLOWED_TENANTS = ("test19",)` (`staging` and every client replica by name) and any host but `localhost` / `<tenant>.localhost`. A tenant joins the list by a commit with its reason.

## 2026-10-18 — handoff 63 (what SAP holds, in Cadra)

- 2026-10-18 · **The SAP mirror is `nrs.sap.asset`, a read-only reference table — not `nrs.asset`, not on the register.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 63 §1, evaluation §3). Its ORM `create`/`write` refuse without the importer's context and `unlink` refuses always; a record missing from a newer export is marked `in_latest_export = False` and kept. Measured with the October export loaded on `nrs19`: the eleven register routes of handoff 57's probe unchanged (`63-evidence/register-filter-probe-nrs19.txt`).
- 2026-10-18 · **The import is a cron job read in TICKS, not a thread.** `[landed]` (Builder's design.) A full pass of the 655 MB sheet costs ~52 s of CPU with the column skip (100 s without it, 72 s for a bare lxml pass), and Odoo kills a worker at `limit_time_cpu` — 60 s locally, 600 s on the cluster — per unit of work. The employee import's thread inside an HTTP worker shares that process's RLIMIT_CPU and would get the WORKER killed. So `_cron_process_imports` reads for ≤ 25 s of CPU, writes, commits the decompressed offset it reached, and re-triggers itself; resuming feeds the parser the sheet's own opening and skips to the offset (decompression is 0.8 s for the whole sheet). The five-minute interval resumes a killed tick. Proved identical to one pass over the real file (211,699 rows, two resumes) and in-registry at every batch boundary. Not Odoo 19's cron progress API: its `_run_job` loops a job at least ten times in one process, which is the CPU limit again.
- 2026-10-18 · **The header is read at upload; a missing column is refused there, and a refused file is not a run.** `[landed]` The 400 names the column and says nothing was imported; no run row is written (the handoff's "each run is recorded" is read as each IMPORT — a file refused at the door imported nothing). Required columns are what the mirror cannot do without (key, class, description, old tag, serial, capitalisation and deactivation dates); the second description, first acquisition, created-on and vendor are optional.
- 2026-10-18 · **Rows are written by batched SQL, not ORM creates.** `[landed]` 211,699 ORM creates is an hour; `execute_values` in batches of 1,000 makes the October file 100 s end to end. The ORM refuses every other writer, no recorder watches these rows, and the test-fixture label is stamped from the run.
- 2026-10-18 · **The link to the register is COMPUTED, never stored.** `[landed]` The handoff offered either. `sap_asset_number` has many writers (the legacy importer, INT-07, the Register Maker's gate, the back office); a stored link refreshed "on every SAP-number write" goes stale on the writer somebody forgets. One join per read, driven from the assets that carry a number. Its first form was planned as a hash of the whole mirror on every query (Postgres estimated 6,999 assets with a SAP number on `nrs19`, which has 2) — 370 ms a matcher call; the matcher now asks per candidate (`EXISTS`, an index probe) and the whole-table form is `MATERIALIZED` and driven by `LATERAL` from the assets: 1.1 ms for a tag lookup. A receipt candidate whose maker typed the number counts as linked: offering that record to an audit capture would be a second claim on it.
- 2026-10-18 · **The key is (asset number, sub-number), the company code compared only when the asset carries one.** `[ruled]` by the handoff. The October export has one company code (`FIRS`); a sub-number of `0`, `00` or blank is the main record. An asset with a company code matches only a record with the same one.
- 2026-10-18 · **The matcher also takes a `serial`.** `[landed]` (Builder's addition, additive.) The handoff's §6 counts confident links "by a unique old tag or an exact serial", so the serial is a matcher input: tried when no active record carries the tag, placeholders never matched (said in `notes`). Description matching is whole-word over both descriptions, scored by words matched, ties broken by the shorter description then the asset number; no fuzzy library (61 §12's baseline already settles three in four by tag).
- 2026-10-18 · **"Confident" excludes a SAP record two Cadra assets would both claim.** `[landed]` On `nrs19`, 3,404 assets rank first by a unique tag or serial but land on 2,894 records — the coverage report counts the contested ones apart and reports `uncontested` as the number a reconciliation could link without a person choosing between two of Cadra's own assets.
- 2026-10-18 · **The retry budget is 32 attempts, not the ruling's 30.** `[landed]` (Deviation, report 63.) Ruling 8 asked for "a full day plus margin: 30 attempts at the 3,600 s cap, about 26 hours". At the built ladder (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 minutes, then hourly) 30 attempts span 24 h 3 min — three minutes of margin; 32 span 26 h 3 min, which is what "about 26 hours" says. Migration 19.0.3.4.0 moves targets on exactly (8, 60, 3600) and lists the rest.
- 2026-10-18 · **A category save writes the class once, and the figures as data.** `[landed]` (Ruling 3.) Measured on `test19`'s class of 18,008: 162,072 ORM writes (nine per asset — one policy write each, four figure assignments inside it and four more after) over 208 queries, 68 s; mail tracking alone 25 s. Not one query per asset — one WRITE CHAIN per asset per field. Now one `write` per category for the policy, and the four derived figures (`accumulated_depreciation`, `current_book_value`, `depreciation_state`, `depreciation_end_date` — nothing computes from them, nothing records or tracks them) stored by batched SQL for the assets whose figures changed, after a flush. Only those assets move `write_date`.
- 2026-10-18 · **The unique index on active tag numbers creates itself where it fits.** `[landed]` (Ruling 2.) `nrs.asset.tag.label.init` creates `nrs_asset_tag_label_active_name_uniq` (`UNIQUE (name) WHERE state = 'active'`) on any upgrade of `myservice_asset_register` where no number is active twice, and logs per database how many stand in the way where some are. Nothing is cleaned automatically. Created on `cadra19`, `gtb19`, `nrs`, `lasg`, `gtb`; blocked on `test19` (68), `jaizbankplc` (15), `nrs19` (1).

## 2026-10-19 — handoff 63b (the faster loop)

- 2026-10-19 · **Both switch states are proved in CI, side by side.** `[landed]` The `bootstrap-and-test` job is a matrix `gates: [off, on]`; the switch row is written after the personas, in both legs, so each says which state it proved. The two legs run in parallel in the same ~13 min the one job took (run 39209513059: 12 m 55 s each). The gates-on pass on a laptop is no longer the proof.
- 2026-10-19 · **The bench gets a fresh test tenant on demand: `test19f`, restored from a template.** `[landed]` `scripts/fresh_test_tenant.sh test19f` — about 5 s from `cadra_test_template` (`CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` + the filestore), the template rebuilt by `--rebuild-template` in about 150 s exactly the way CI builds its database, plus the fixtures `test19` carries (`scripts/seed_test_template_fixtures.py`, which CI now runs too). Allow-list `test19f`, `test19`; an existing database must be marked `test`; `nrs19` and the cluster are refused by name. **The suites use `test19f` from now on**: 9 min gates off and 15 min gates on, against `test19`'s 14 min 42 s and 24 min 36 s, with 10 skips against 7 — and its results do not depend on what earlier runs left behind. `test19` stays as it is (the owner's call), the tenant for anything that needs history: the Vendra Builder's drives, the walk script, report-scale timings.
- 2026-10-19 · **`pytest -n` (xdist) is NOT adopted.** `[landed]` Measured on a fresh `test19f`: `-n 4` 30 failed, 2 errors of 1,498 — the suite shares state (one tenant-wide switch flipped by one test under another, counts over the whole register, and the tag-number race below). Stopped there as the handoff said; no test rewritten. The report hook collects in `pytest_runtest_logreport` so the day it is adopted the report is still written.
- 2026-10-19 · **A configuration-change report's day is a Lagos day.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 63b §3), `[landed]` Behaviour change, announced. The access-change report still bounds UTC days; named, not changed.
- 2026-10-19 · **A category policy save restates its class once, from one schedule per asset.** `[landed]` 63 left two recomputes per save (the depreciation module's `write` and the route) and two schedule builds per asset per recompute (value, end date): 76,168 builds on `test19`'s 19,042. The route's second recompute is gone and `engine.summary_at` builds once (pinned equal to the two calls over 38,400 policies). 22.2 s → 12.8 s in-process; over HTTP 13.2 / 20.6 s → 9.7 / 9.7 s. **Mail tracking of the four policy fields on every asset (3.7 s) is KEPT**: it is the per-asset history line.
- 2026-10-19 · **A tag number is drawn from a Postgres sequence per prefix.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 63b amended §3b), `[landed]` A row lock was the other option and loses: Odoo's REPEATABLE READ snapshot is taken before any lock the mint could take, so the second request still reads the first's max. `nextval` is outside every snapshot. The sequence is created on first use under a transaction-scoped advisory lock and started at the highest number already issued; a number issued around it (an import) lifts it. The collision left answers `409 tag_number_taken`, `details.retryable: true` — a new code, because unlike every other 409 the answer is to send it again.

## 2026-10-19 — handoff 64 (what the photo says)

- 2026-10-18 · **R11: Cadra identifies the photo — server-side, no model trained or shipped.** `[ruled]` by the owner (handoff 64 §0, `docs/AI-CAPTURE-EVALUATION.md` §2), `[landed]` An existing vision model is called by Cadra's server only (`POST /api/v1/ai/identify`), never by the phone or the browser; one route for the web app and the phone; the key never leaves the server; every call is recorded (`nrs.ai.suggestion`, append-only). The mobile map's model manifest and download rows are *not needed*. Photos leave our servers: this joins R7 — NRS's written acceptance names the provider before production photos are sent (GO-LIVE §33.1).
- 2026-10-19 · **The provider sits behind one adapter; Anthropic's Messages API through the official SDK.** `[landed]` `myservice_api/controllers/ai_provider.py`: a deterministic offline fake (`fake`, answers from the file name; every test uses it, and it answers only on a database marked `test`) and `AnthropicProvider`, the `anthropic` Python SDK pinned at 1.14.0 in both Odoo images and imported only when used (absent → a sentence, not a broken module). Structured outputs constrain the answer to the schema; Cadra validates it again against the tenant (a class it does not have, a condition off the enum, three alternatives → discarded and counted). Default model `claude-opus-5-5`, effort `low`, both settings. A make, a model or dimensions the provider proposes without having READ them are removed and said in `notes` — the prompt forbids guessing them and a photo has no scale.
- 2026-10-19 · **The key's road is the Turnstile secret's.** `[landed]` **Superseded by handoff 65 (the key lives in the server's environment), below.** `myservice_api.ai_api_key`, mirrored one-way by bootstrap from `CADRA_AI_API_KEY` (k8s: the `cadra-odoo` secret, optional) and written locally by `scripts/set_ai_key.sh <db>` from a hidden prompt on stdin. No route reads or accepts it (`key_placed` is the only thing said); the configuration-change report redacts it by name (`api_key`). Placing a key switches nothing on, and switching on is refused while the provider cannot answer.
- 2026-10-19 · **A photo for an asset not yet saved is HELD, not made a photo.** `[landed]` `nrs.asset.photo` requires an asset and is append-only, so 61's rules stay untouched: `nrs.ai.pending.photo` holds the bytes (checked by the photo's own `check_photo_size` and `sniff_photo`) for 24 h; the save claims it through `nrs.asset.photo._upload`, the one writer; a cron deletes the unclaimed. The suggestion keeps the hash, so what was identified stays named after the bytes go.
- 2026-10-19 · **What a person did with a suggestion is an asset event, not a write to the suggestion.** `[landed]` The suggestion is append-only, so the save writes `nrs.asset.event` `ai_suggestion_applied` (`ai_suggestion_id`, `photo_id`, per-field outcomes as JSON in `notes`): accepted (saved = proposed, text case-folded), changed (from → to), ignored (dismissed, or not sent). History type `details_suggested`, one line: *"Details suggested from a photo; 4 accepted, 1 changed."* The save carries `ai_suggestion: {id, ignored}`, taken off the body before the allow-list — it decides what is RECORDED, never what is written. A suggestion is applied once.
- 2026-10-19 · **An SAP candidate fills nothing.** `[landed]` (deviation from §3: "pick an SAP candidate to fill the SAP number, if the person may"). No asset route writes `sap_asset_number` — the Register Maker's gate and SAP's own answer do (`v1_assets`' SAP block says why: a client that could PATCH it could mark an asset reconciled SAP never booked). So nobody on the asset form "may", and the candidates are shown with their reasons, for reference. Linking a register asset to its SAP record is handoff 65.
- 2026-10-19 · **The daily cap is counted, not reserved.** `[landed]` Counted before the call, the row written after; calls at the same instant as the last allowed one can each pass, so a day can close over by the number in flight. Holding a lock across a 20-second provider call would queue every person on the tenant; the cap is a cost guard. Every call counts, a failed one included.
- 2026-10-19 · **Test Connection on an `asset_events` target tests what Cadra sends.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 64 §3b), `[landed]` A POST to each configured endpoint with the delivery's own headers and auth and a body with no event id — Vendra's receivers authenticate first and then refuse it 400 before writing anything — so 400/422 reads *reachable, token accepted*, 401/403 *token refused*, 404 *nothing receives at this path*, and each endpoint gets its own sentence. On `test19`'s real Vendra target: the old GET `/ping` → `HTTP 401 AuthenticationError`, the probe → both reachable, token accepted. **SAP's target has the same shape of false failure**: INT-06 publishes no `/ping`, so the button asks SAP for a path it never promised; unmeasured (no SAP here; `nrs19`'s SAP target is a local stub that is not running), and left as it was by the handoff.
- 2026-10-19 · **Found, not changed: `TestPublishedNamesResolve.test_every_bare_override_of_an_inherited_route_is_named` is order-dependent.** A bare `@http.route()` override carries no routing attribute until Odoo builds its routing map, so the test passes only when something earlier in the process built it (CI's full module run does) and fails run alone, naming the four back-office doors stale. Not this handoff's change.

## 2026-10-19 — handoff 65 (the link)

- 2026-10-19 · **A link is maker-checker, on its own record, never deleted.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 65 §1), `[landed]` `nrs.sap.link` (`kind` link/unlink, `state` proposed → approved | returned): created and decided only through its acts (a context flag; a decided row frozen; `unlink` refuses), one open proposal per asset and per SAP record by partial unique index. The no-two-gates rule is reused with two gates of its own, `link_make` and `link_check`, one pair for both directions so the maker who proposed a link may propose undoing it; `_two_gates_refusal` now reads `gate_fired` and `sap_link` events (`_segregation_events`). A link never moves `register_state`; the gate switch changes nothing.
- 2026-10-19 · **An approval writes SAP's number and sends nothing.** `[landed]` `sap_asset_number` (normalised), the sub-number when not the main record, the company code, `sap_code_source = linked_from_sap_mirror` — a new value, refused to every writer but the approval. No workflow hook watches those fields; `_queue_sap_event` already refuses a master for an asset carrying a number. Proved against a live stub SAP target (`myservice_workflow/tests/test_sap_link_outbox.py`, whose control shows the same target queues a master for an unlinked affix). `_set_sap_master` keeps a linked number against a different INT-07 answer, as it keeps the maker's.
- 2026-10-19 · **A link waits for an asset-master posting already in flight.** `[landed]` (Verification pass, finding 2.) A gates-off affix on an asset with no number queues `asset.master.create`; linking SAP's own number on top would give SAP two masters. Proposal and approval refuse while `sap_posting_state` is `queued` or `posted`, in words. `nrs19`: 19 of 11,958 register assets with no SAP number are `queued`.
- 2026-10-19 · **"Confident" is computed once, for the list, the proposals and the report.** `[landed]` `nrs.sap.asset._confident_targets` (asset → record, basis; record → claimants); `_confident_links` counts it, returning the same numbers as before (`nrs19`: 3,404 / 2,445 / 959). An approval of an old-tag proposal re-checks that no other register asset has come to claim the record (finding 9); a serial-basis proposal is not re-checked — named.
- 2026-10-19 · **The contested list is a review, and a flag is an event.** `[ruled]` (handoff 65, ruling 1 on report 63), `[landed]` Each record two or more assets point at, side by side; the maker proposes one (its reason says "chosen from N"), or flags the group: one append-only `sap_duplicate_flagged` event per asset with the note, shown as `sap.duplicate_flag`. No model for the flag — the event is the record, and nothing is merged.
- 2026-10-19 · **The 5% guard counts before it writes.** `[ruled]` (handoff 65 §3, ruling 4), `[landed]` A run against a non-empty mirror first reads the file for its keys only, into an UNLOGGED scratch table, and compares with the records in the latest export; over 5% it is `held` and has written nothing to the mirror; under it rewinds and writes. "Yes, mark them absent" is a second upload with `mark_absent=true`, recorded on the run (who). The alternative — guarding only the final "mark absent" step — was rejected because the rows would already have been rewritten from a wrong file. Cost: a guarded import reads the file twice (`nrs19`'s full restore: 348 s on a busy bench, against 63's 100 s). A run already writing at upgrade time finishes without counting (finding 3); the scratch is emptied on failure (finding 4).
- 2026-10-19 · **The class evidence is evidence, not a mapping.** `[ruled]` (handoff 65 §4), `[landed]` `class_evidence` on the coverage report counts, per Cadra category, the SAP classes of its linked assets — any register asset whose number names a mirror record — with `via_link` apart. Nothing writes `sap_asset_class` from it.
- 2026-10-19 · **The AI key lives in the server's environment, not the database.** `[ruled]` handoff 65 §0a.1 (report 64 deviation 2), `[landed]` A parameter row is readable by any back-office system administrator and lands in every backup. `ai_provider.provider_key()` reads `CADRA_AI_API_KEY` on every call; compose passes it (the overlays inherit), k8s from the `cadra-odoo` secret (optional) on the Odoo Deployment; bootstrap's mirror deleted; migration `myservice_api` 19.0.3.36.0 deletes the row and logs per tenant whether one existed (none did locally); `scripts/set_ai_key.sh` writes `.env`, no database argument. One key per server; the tenant switch is unchanged.
- 2026-10-19 · **A suggestion the register already bore out is accepted, not ignored.** `[ruled]` handoff 65 §0a.3, `[landed]` A proposed field neither written nor named in `ignored`, equal to what the asset holds, is `accepted` with `already_held: true`; a named field stays `ignored`.
- 2026-10-19 · **The access-change report's day is a Lagos day.** `[ruled]` (handoff 65 §0), `[landed]` through 63b's `lagos_days_in_utc`; behaviour change, announced.
- 2026-10-19 · **`nrs19` is measured read-only; the persona drives run on `test19f`.** `[landed]` (Deviation.) A classified copy of `nrs19` with the demo personas was refused by the Builder's safety layer — `nrs19` holds a real NRS staff extract — and the copy was dropped. `nrs19` has no credential holding `sap_mirror:read` and no Register Maker or Checker; linking its assets needs the owner to classify it and seed personas, or two real NRS officers.


## 2026-10-19 — handoff 66 (the audit in the room)

- 2026-10-19 · **An audit capture is its own path, with three outcomes and two people.** `[ruled]` by the Architect (handoff 66 §1, R1), `[landed]` `nrs.audit.capture`: everything the phone sent is frozen at receipt (`AI-NFR-05`'s trail); only review moves it. a · on the register → a verification in place (location, condition, photo; the verifier is the CAPTURER, who stood in the room, the notes name the reviewer); b · in SAP → a new asset at `registered` with SAP's number and the code the phone printed, `sap_code_source = audit_from_sap_mirror` (a new value, refused to every writer but the acceptance), an `audit_capture` event saying why the receipt gates did not apply; c · in neither → an exception, its code `in_review`. The reviewer is never the capturer: refused on the act (`segregation_of_duties`), on the campaign (one person in both lists) and at grant (`nrs.role.exclusion`, the ninth pair).
- 2026-10-19 · **A move found by an audit is approved by the acceptance.** `[landed]` A register asset found in another room is moved by the reviewer's acceptance under a new exemption reason, `audit` (`approval_policy.EXEMPT_REASONS`) — declared in one place, after the capturer ≠ reviewer check; the movement row says `approval`, with the campaign and capture as its reason. The second person has already agreed; asking for a change request as well would ask twice.
- 2026-10-19 · **The reviewer's acceptance is the approval of a reprint.** `[ruled]` (handoff 66 §5), `[landed]` A capture saying the label is missing or unreadable is reprinted under the same code by 61's rules (old print voided with `lost`/`damaged`, a reprint row one higher). R3's approver is an Asset Manager; for an audit it is the reviewer, honoured by `_may_decide` only for the one label the acceptance names (context `cadra_audit_reprint`), never for the requester (the capturer).
- 2026-10-19 · **The ledger is one table of every code; blocks are drawn from the asset-code sequence.** `[ruled]` (R2, R2a), `[landed]` `nrs.asset.code`, `reserved → in_review → active | void`; written by `nrs.asset.create` for every new asset (active if registered, `in_review` for a receipt candidate, activated in `_gate_move` at registration, voided at an IAD rejection) and by block issue. **Backfill deviates by one word from the handoff**: every existing code is recorded, but a candidate's as `in_review` and an IAD-rejected receipt's as `void`, not all as `active` — R2a says a code is active at registration. Counts: `test19` 23,405 of 23,405 (21,288 active, 2,111 in review, 6 void), `nrs19` 11,960 of 11,960 active, `test19f` 400 of 400. A ledger row holds its asset `set null`, not restrict: a ledger row must not pin every asset from birth.
- 2026-10-19 · **What `_order = 'asset_code desc'` now means.** `[landed]` (report 61 §7.2.) Codes in a block are drawn when the block is issued and used days later, so an asset registered from an audit capture sorts below assets created since: the order is the order codes were DRAWN, which is creation order only for an asset coded at creation. Nothing user-facing changes: every list in the app comes through the API, which pages on `id` (`?sort=newest` is id-descending). The order reaches only the back office's list views — administrators only since handoff 45 — and the back-office station's search (`order='asset_code'`). Left as it is.
- 2026-10-19 · **A device is not a credential.** `[ruled]` (R4), `[landed]` `nrs.audit.device` names itself in `X-Cadra-Device` (a `DEV-…` code generated at registration), checked against the ONE person it is bound to; the person signs in as themselves. Rebinding is refused while a block issued to the previous person is open — return it first, so no code is left reserved to somebody who no longer holds the phone.
- 2026-10-19 · **A capture is idempotent on its own `capture_id`, stored unique.** `[ruled]` (findings §7.3), `[landed]` Not the 24-hour cache: a phone may sync five working days later. A second sync of the same id by the same person answers the stored capture (`replayed: true`, still 201); by another person, 403. `captured_at` may be at most five working days (Mon–Fri, Lagos) in the past, never more than five minutes ahead.
- 2026-10-19 · **A duplicate is held, never refused.** `[ruled]` (handoff 66 §4), `[landed]` A second capture of the same Cadra asset, SAP record or old tag in a campaign carries `duplicate_of`; a room's batch acceptance leaves held duplicates for one-by-one review; accepting one whose twin is already accepted is refused (return it). No visual signature (ruling 3).
- 2026-10-19 · **A capture's photos are their own records until accepted.** `[landed]` `nrs.audit.photo`, 61's checks (`check_photo_size`, `sniff_photo`), kept without expiry (a review takes days, the AI hold is 24 h), one row per person and SHA-256 so a retried sync is the same row; copied onto the asset's append-only photos at acceptance (`photos_copied` says how many fitted under the 12-photo cap).
- 2026-10-19 · **A found-not-in-SAP item registered as new waits on the Register Maker.** `[ruled]` (handoff 66 §5), `[landed]` The Asset Controller's *register as new* creates the asset at `tagged` (its label — the capture's code — is on it and affixed by the capturer), with a `gate_fired` event whose `gate` is empty (`audit-exception`): nobody is recorded as having received, verified or tagged it, so the maker and checker are free to act, and INT-06's master goes at the checker's approval, because SAP does not hold it. The code stays `in_review` until then. *Void* voids the code with the reason.
- 2026-10-19 · **Closing a campaign keeps its two lists as they stood.** `[landed]` Refused while any capture waits (the count in the refusal). *In SAP, not found*: active, latest-export records of the campaign's offices that no accepted capture matched and no Cadra asset links to; records with no office a COUNT (without SAP's location extract they cannot be placed). Stored at closing (`closing_json`); an open campaign's lists are computed now and say `as_of: now`.
- 2026-10-19 · **`/ai/identify` opens to the capturer.** `[landed]` Behaviour change, announced: the route's scope is `assets:write` OR `audit:capture`, checked in the body (a route's `scopes` are all-of). The capturer holds no `assets:write`: capturing an item is not creating an asset.
- 2026-10-19 · **Report 65's two "found, not changed" items, corrected.** `[landed]` The serial-basis contested re-check is built (the twin of the old-tag one). The CHANGELOG duplication report 65 named is not in the file — one heading each for 63b and 64 at HEAD and in the eight commits before it; nothing deduped.


## 2026-10-20 — handoff 67 (the station captures)

- 2026-10-20 · **A shared station's shift change: return the block, rebind, issue a new one.** `[landed]` (handoff 67 §1 asked for the simpler of two.) Chosen: the rule 66 already has — rebinding is refused while a block issued to the previous person is open. Rejected: a block per person on one device, which would need a device to carry several open blocks and a capture to pick the right one, i.e. a new rule. What makes the chosen one workable at a station is §2.3: the person leaving returns their own block (unused codes voided, never reissued), the Asset Controller rebinds the station and issues the next person a block. Cost: codes voided at every shift; codes are sequence numbers, so the cost is gaps, and the void reason names the block.
- 2026-10-20 · **A station's print of a block code is recorded on the ledger.** `[landed]` (Deviation from "print through the station's existing print funnel (handoff 10)".) The station draws labels in the browser (61 found it never reaches Odoo's PDF funnel) and records a tag label's print with `POST /tags/printed`; a block code has no tag label until an asset carries it, so its prints are counted on its ledger row (`print_count`, first/last printed, by whom) through `POST /audit/codes/printed`, called by the station's print path right after the print dialog opens. The capture flow uses the station's same `printLabels`.
- 2026-10-20 · **The capturer voids a spoiled code and returns their own block.** `[ruled]` (handoff 67 §2.2–2.3), `[landed]` A void needs a reason and only a block code reserved to the caller and still `reserved`; no device header (a phone may be broken). Returning: `audit:manage` OR `audit:capture`, the latter only for a block issued to the caller (`holder_only`, checked in the model).
- 2026-10-20 · **GPS is the capture's alone.** `[ruled]` (AI-FR-10), `[landed]` Three columns and a `gps_recorded` flag on `nrs.audit.capture` (an Odoo float is never NULL, and 0,0 is a place); published only on capture reads (`audit:read`); never on the asset, and `/ai/identify` has no part that could carry it.
- 2026-10-20 · **The size band is words, labelled approximate, never a dimension.** `[ruled]` (AI-FR-03), `[landed]` `size_band` in the provider's schema as `small|medium|large|unknown` (`unknown` published as null — a null in an enum is not something every structured-output engine accepts), defined in words in `ai_provider.SIZE_BAND_WORDS`, which the prompt and the response both read. Prompt version 67.1. Not a field a save writes.
- 2026-10-20 · **"Accepted unchanged" means every proposed field kept.** `[landed]` (AI-NFR-08.) A suggestion is *used* when an asset save (an `ai_suggestion_applied` event) or an audit capture names it; *accepted unchanged* when none of its proposed fields was changed or left out — for an asset save, the event's own counts; for a capture, the same `_outcomes` comparison against what the capture confirmed (and, for an asset on the register, what it already held). Class is the class suggested; site is the campaign location covering the capture's room; unused suggestions are one site row.

## 2026-10-20 — handoff 68 (a second provider)

- 2026-10-20 · **OpenAI beside Anthropic, chosen per tenant; the Responses API.** `[ruled]` (the owner, 20 October: the Anthropic account is held at verification), `[landed]` `ai_provider.OpenAIProvider`, the official `openai` SDK pinned at 3.26.1 (12 days old; 3.35.1 was two days old), imported only when used. The Responses API rather than Chat Completions because OpenAI's current documentation builds on it: `instructions` carries the system prompt, `text.format` a strict JSON schema, and every current model is served there. The image at `detail: high`, `store: false` (nothing is fetched again, so nothing is kept for retrieval), `reasoning.effort` from the tenant's setting, `max_output_tokens` 8,192 because OpenAI counts reasoning inside it, SDK retries off. Default model `gpt-6.1-sol` — OpenAI's page recommends it "to balance intelligence and cost", at a fifth of GPT-6 Astra's price.
- 2026-10-20 · **One prompt, one schema, unchanged.** `[landed]` The shared schema already meets strict mode's two rules (every object closed, every property required); it goes to OpenAI as it is, and `ai_provider.strict_schema_problems` with its unit test fails the day an edit breaks either rule. No provider-specific transformation exists. `AnthropicProvider` is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- 2026-10-20 · **Usage is recorded the way the record already meant it.** `[landed]` OpenAI's `input_tokens` includes cached and cache-written tokens; Anthropic's does not. Cadra records uncached input apart for both, so one price table and one `cost_usd` serve both. A dated snapshot id (`gpt-6.1-sol-2026-09-29`) is priced as its model.
- 2026-10-20 · **A model per provider.** `[landed]` (handoff 68 §3, "the model field follows the provider".) Anthropic keeps `cadra.ai.model`; OpenAI's is `cadra.ai.model.openai`. Rejected: one shared parameter reset to the new provider's default on a switch — switching back would lose a model the owner had typed. Each parameter is a configuration row, so a switch and a model change are both on the configuration record.
- 2026-10-20 · **"No quota" is told apart from "slow down".** `[landed]` OpenAI says an empty account with `429 insufficient_quota`, not a 403; it gets its own sentence, because waiting does not cure it. Every other mapping uses Anthropic's sentences, the key variable named being OpenAI's.

## 2026-10-25 — the label artwork (owner's request, no handoff)

- 2026-10-25 · **The asset label is stripped to what is read and scanned, replacing "Option 1".** `[ruled]` (the owner, 25 October, from a photograph of a printed label: the old one was too small to read or scan.) The QR on the left with the asset name, the serial in brackets (left off when the asset has none) and the tag number beside it; the Code 128 on a row of its own across the whole label. Gone: the brand column and logo, the coloured *FACILITIES MANAGEMENT* band, the floor/room line, the asset-code line and the repeat of the number under the bars. Both renderers changed together, the app's (`src/lib/labels.ts`) and the back office's (`asset_tag_report.xml`).
