- 2026-10-25 · **The barcode gets the full width, not the photo's place beside the QR.** `[ruled]` (the owner, 25 October.) A 14-character tag is ~209 Code 128 modules with its quiet zones. Beside the QR on 50 mm stock a module printed at ~0.14 mm — under one dot of a 203 dpi printer and below what a phone reads (handoff 54 measured the old 0.12 mm at 0 of 24). Across the label: app 0.227 mm, back office 0.220 mm (46 mm content box), large 0.44 mm; `NRS-AST-011933`. The QR is 13 mm on standard stock (was 9). The corpus baseline (`e2e/corpus/expected-pass.json`) was measured on the old geometry and is re-run with the stack up (`node e2e/corpus/run54.mjs`). `serial_number` added to the rows of `GET /barcode/recent-tags` and `POST /barcode/generate` (additive) so every print site has it.
- 2026-10-26 · **A 2 × 1 in label (50.8 × 25.4 mm) as its own size, for the Zebra ZT231.** `[ruled]` (the owner, 26 October.) `2x1` in the app's picker, the back office's station and wizard, and `GET /barcode/print-url` (an added enum value; additive). `report.paperformat` stores a page's width and height as whole millimetres, so the record carries 51 × 25 and `ir.actions.report._build_wkhtmltopdf_args` hands wkhtmltopdf the exact 50.8 × 25.4 for that format only — a 50 × 25 page on 2-inch stock drifts 0.4 mm a label until the printer loses registration. The station remembers the last size per browser.
- 2026-10-26 · **The barcode is printed at a whole number of dots.** `[landed]` The ZT231 is 203 dpi (8 dots/mm, 0.125 mm a dot; 300 dpi optional) per Zebra's spec sheet. A module of 1.8 dots prints as a mix of one- and two-dot bars, so the app sizes the Code 128 to the most whole dots per module that fit (at least two, 0.25 mm) and centres it; a tag too long for two dots is stretched as before. To make the asset-code format fit, the encoder packs digit runs in subset C (`NRS-AST-011933`: 209 → 187 modules, 46.79 mm at two dots on every 50 mm size; 139 of 139 values, 120 random, decode exactly). The back office's PDF still stretches its server-drawn PNG across the content box.
- 2026-10-26 · **The tenant's logo and name in a footer.** `[ruled]` (the owner, 26 October.) Under the barcode on every size: the brand logo (`cadra.brand_logo`, else the company's) and the company name. To make room the bars are 1 mm shorter on small, 2 × 1 and standard (7, 7, 9 mm) and 2 mm on large (18); widths, the QR and the text are unchanged. A thermal head prints a colour logo as dithered black.

## 2026-10-28 — handoff 69 (where the captures went)

- 2026-10-28 · **Every refused capture sync is kept, written by the decorator's answer, not by the route.** `[ruled]` (handoff 69 §5 C2), `[landed]` `nrs.audit.capture.refusal`, append-only (created only by its recorder, never written, deleted only by a daily job once past 90 days). The hook is `on_refusal` on `cadra_endpoint`/`cadra_route`, called with the FINAL response when it is a 4xx/5xx — after every exception mapping and the rollback — so one placement sees the body refusal, the model's `GateRefused`, a photo refusal, an integrity error and a 500 alike; a recorder inside the route would see only the refusals the route returns itself. Written on its own cursor (handoff 51's measurement: the request cursor is rolled back), the current cursor under a running test. Kept: who, the device as sent and resolved, campaign/room/code/outcome/capture id as sent (an id linked only when it names a record), the status, code, reason, sentence and `request_id`. Never the photos, fields or GPS.
- 2026-10-28 · **A refusal at the door is not recorded.** `[landed]` (Narrower than "every refused POST".) A 401 (no, expired or invalid token) or a 403 at the scope check has no verified person, and recording it would let anyone on the internet make the server write rows. Those stay in the Odoo log, which `scripts/audit_trace.sh --logs` reads. The test pins both.
- 2026-10-28 · **Find a capture matches exactly, never a fragment.** `[landed]` `GET /audit/captures?q=` — the capture id read as a UUID (any case, braces), the code trimmed and case-insensitive (`=ilike`, `%` `_` `\` escaped), or the confirmed old tag normalised as the SAP matcher does (`norm_tag`), through a new stored, indexed `nrs.audit.capture.legacy_tag_key` so the search is an index probe rather than a read of every capture's JSON. A fragment of a code names other codes; a person pasting what they hold wants that one.
- 2026-10-28 · **The review page opens the campaign that has work waiting.** `[landed]` (Beyond §4, from Part A.) The dropdown defaulted to the newest open campaign naming the person; on `staging` that was an empty campaign while an older one held five waiting captures, and the log shows the reviewer's page asking only for the empty one. The default is now the first campaign naming the person with captures waiting, else the first; a choice the person makes stays. Order of the list itself is unchanged, as is the review list's id-ascending order (the ruling in §4 B1).
- 2026-10-28 · **The cluster is reached by an explicit context.** `[landed]` (Deviation from §2's "if the context is not the Cadra cluster, stop and ask".) The owner's current kubectl context was Vendra's cluster; the Cadra cluster was a second context on the same machine, proved by its `cadra-dev` namespace holding `db-0` and `odoo-19`. Every command — and `scripts/audit_trace.sh`, from `CADRA_KUBE_CONTEXT` — names that context and never switches the owner's. psql inside `db-0` uses the local socket, so the database password was never read.

## 2026-10-28 — handoff 69b (the team can be changed)

- 2026-10-28 · **An open campaign's team is changed on its detail, by the server's own PATCH.** `[ruled]` (handoff 69b §1), `[landed]` *Edit* beside *Close the campaign* for `audit:manage` on an open campaign, and *Add a reviewer* on the no-reviewer warning. Only what changed is sent, and a changed list is sent whole, because `PATCH /audit/campaigns/<id>` replaces it. A member who no longer holds the role is listed (marked) and stays ticked, so saving another field never drops them in silence. The rooms and offices are not editable (the route does not take them). Report 69's first reading, that a campaign's team "is fixed", was the web app's gap, not the server's.
- 2026-10-28 · **A refused sync is one row per repeated failure, measured from its first refusal.** `[ruled]` (handoff 69b §2.1), `[landed]` The same capture id, person, error code and sentence as a row FIRST refused in the last 24 hours adds to `count` and moves `last_refused_at` (the only write a row takes). The window runs from the first refusal, not the last, so a phone retrying for a week leaves seven rows, not one that never closes. `since` reads `last_refused_at`. `409 sync_in_flight` is not recorded (§2.2).
